# 📘 COMPOUNDING, DISPENSING & PHARMACEUTICS
## A Complete Topper's Notes Book
### For Pharmacist / Compounder / Dispenser / Drug Inspector & allied competitive examinations

---

> **Scope of this book** — This book covers, exhaustively and line-by-line, the following
> syllabus block:
>
> 1. Kinds of drugs, characteristics of drugs, Balsams, Gums etc.
> 2. Pharmaceutical Process and Methods
> 3. General directions on dispensing, weighing and measuring. How prescriptions are written,
>    prescription reading. How to calculate doses, weights and measures, formulae for converting
>    from one scale to other, abbreviations used in prescriptions.
> 4. Doses of drugs, pharmacopoeial preparations and their doses, incompatibility — physical,
>    chemical, physiological and therapeutical.
> 5. **Suppositories** — How to prepare suppositories of special medicines, pessaries, bougies,
>    plasters etc.
> 6. Ointments, Spray solutions or Nebulas, Inhalations, General rules about preparation of
>    Mixtures.
> 7. Ordinary bazaar medicines, their recognition, doses and uses.

| ⭐ **EXAM POINT** | A fact that is repeatedly asked. Highest yield. |
| 🧠 **MEMORY HOOK** | Mnemonic or trick to lock the fact in. |
| ⚠️ **TRAP** | The exact place where candidates lose marks (look-alike facts). |
| 🔢 **NUMBER TO MEMORISE** | A figure, ratio, temperature, dose or constant. |

| 📖 **OFFICIAL IN** | Pharmacopoeial status (IP / BP / USP). |
| 🔁 **COMPARE** | Side-by-side differentiation table. |

---

## 🎯 HOW TO USE THIS BOOK

1. **First pass (understanding).** Read a chapter fully, ignoring nothing. Every table in this
   book exists because that table's content has been asked as an objective question.
2. **Second pass (extraction).** Read only 📌 ⭐ 🔢 ⚠️ boxes and all tables.
3. **Third pass (24 h before exam).** Read only `14-appendices-and-revision.md`.
4. **Numericals** (Ch. 6, Ch. 9 displacement value) must be *worked with a pen*, not read.
5. **Latin abbreviations** (Ch. 5) and **bazaar medicines** (Ch. 13) are pure memory scoring
   areas — revise them daily in small doses rather than once in bulk.

---

## ⚖️ A NOTE ON DOSES & FORMULAE

* All doses quoted are **adult oral doses** unless otherwise stated.
* Doses and formulae follow the classical pharmacopoeial/dispensing literature
  (Indian Pharmacopoeia, British Pharmacopoeia, British Pharmaceutical Codex,
  *Cooper & Gunn's Dispensing for Pharmaceutical Students*, *Bentley's Textbook of
  Pharmaceutics*, *Carter's Cooper & Gunn's Tutorial Pharmacy*) because **that is what the
  examination is set from**.
* Several of these classical preparations are obsolete in modern therapeutics; they are retained
  here **only because the syllabus and question papers still test them**.
* Ranges differ by one or two units between editions. Where an examiner's key may differ, the
  book gives the **most commonly printed value first**, with alternatives in brackets.
* ⚠️ This is an **examination revision aid, not a prescribing guide.** Never dispense or
  administer on the strength of these notes alone.

# CHAPTER 1
# 💊 KINDS OF DRUGS & CHARACTERISTICS OF DRUGS

---

## 1.1 THE WORD "DRUG"

📌 **DEFINITION (Etymology)**
The word **drug** is derived from the French word **"Drogue"** — meaning *a dry herb*.
(Some texts trace it to the Dutch/Low German **"Droog"** = dry.)
⭐ Examiners ask: *"Drug is derived from which word?"* → **Drogue (French) = dry herb.**

📌 **DEFINITION (Pharmacological)**
A **drug** is any substance or product that is used or intended to be used to **modify or explore
physiological systems or pathological states** for the benefit of the recipient (WHO, 1966).

📌 **DEFINITION (Drugs & Cosmetics Act, 1940 — Sec. 3(b), India)**
"Drug" includes —
1. All medicines for **internal or external** use of human beings or animals, and all substances
   intended to be used for or in the **diagnosis, treatment, mitigation or prevention** of any
   disease or disorder in human beings or animals.
2. Such substances (other than food) intended to **affect the structure or any function** of the
   human body, or intended to be used for the destruction of **vermin or insects** which cause
   disease in human beings or animals.
3. All substances intended for use as a **component** of a drug, including empty gelatin capsules.
4. Such **devices** intended for internal or external use in diagnosis, treatment, mitigation or
   prevention of disease, as notified by the Central Government.

📌 **DEFINITION (Cosmetic — Sec. 3(aaa))**
Any article intended to be rubbed, poured, sprinkled or sprayed on, introduced into or otherwise
applied to the human body for **cleansing, beautifying, promoting attractiveness or altering the
appearance**, and includes any article intended for use as a component of a cosmetic.

⚠️ **TRAP** — Soap is *excluded* from the definition of a cosmetic under the Act.

📌 **DEFINITION (Crude drug)**
A **crude drug** is a naturally occurring, unrefined substance of vegetable, animal or mineral
origin, subjected to no processing other than **collection and drying**.

🧠 **MEMORY HOOK — the 5 "must-know" definitions:** Drug → Crude drug → Pharmacognosy →
Pharmacy → Pharmacopoeia.

| Term | Definition | Etymology |
|---|---|---|
| **Pharmacognosy** | Study of crude drugs of natural (plant, animal, mineral) origin | Gk. *pharmakon* = drug + *gnosis* = knowledge |
| **Pharmacy** | Art & science of selecting, preparing, compounding, dispensing and storing drugs | Gk. *pharmakon* = drug/medicine |
| **Pharmacology** | Study of drugs and their actions on living systems | *pharmakon* + *logos* = study |
| **Pharmacodynamics** | What the **drug does to the body** | — |
| **Pharmacokinetics** | What the **body does to the drug** (ADME) | — |
| **Posology** | Science of **dosage** / dose determination | Gk. *posos* = how much + *logos* |
| **Pharmacopoeia** | Legally recognised official book of drug standards | Gk. *pharmakon* + *poiein* = to make |
| **Formulary** | Book of formulae/collection of prescriptions (e.g. National Formulary of India) | L. *formula* |
| **Materia Medica** | "Materials of medicine" — older term for the study of source, preparation & use of drugs | L. |
| **Therapeutics** | Application of remedies for the treatment of disease | Gk. *therapeuein* |
| **Toxicology** | Study of poisons — detection, action, treatment | Gk. *toxikon* = poison |
| **Dispensing** | Preparation and supply of medicine to a patient against a prescription | L. *dispensare* = weigh out |
| **Compounding** | Combining two or more ingredients to make a medicine | — |

⭐ **EXAM POINT** — **Father of Pharmacognosy** → *C. A. Seydler* coined the term (1815);
**Father of Medicine** → *Hippocrates*; **Father of Pharmacology** → *Rudolf Buchheim* /
*Oswald Schmiedeberg*; **Father of Indian Pharmacology** → *Ram Nath Chopra*;
**Father of Pharmacognosy in India** → *T. E. Wallis* (widely printed); **Father of Botany** →
*Theophrastus*; **Father of Materia Medica** → *Dioscorides* (*De Materia Medica*);
**Father of Toxicology** → *Paracelsus* (also called Father of Chemotherapy/Iatrochemistry);
**Father of Antisepsis/aseptic surgery** → *Joseph Lister*; **Father of Chemotherapy (modern)** →
*Paul Ehrlich*.

---

## 1.2 "KINDS OF DRUGS" — ALL THE CLASSIFICATION SCHEMES

⭐ This heading in the syllabus means: *"In how many ways can drugs be classified?"*
Objective papers pick a random one of these eight schemes. Learn all eight.

### Scheme 1 — Classification by SOURCE / ORIGIN

```
                            DRUGS
                              │
   ┌────────────┬─────────────┼─────────────┬──────────────┐
 NATURAL     SYNTHETIC   SEMI-SYNTHETIC  BIOLOGICAL   BIOTECHNOLOGICAL
   │                                     (biologicals)   (r-DNA)
   ├── Plant (vegetable)
   ├── Animal
   ├── Mineral
   ├── Microbial (fermentation)
   └── Marine
```

| Source | Explanation | Examples |
|---|---|---|
| **Plant / Vegetable** | Largest single source of crude drugs | Digitalis (leaf), Opium (latex), Senna, Rauwolfia (root), Cinchona (bark), Castor oil (seed), Acacia, Tragacanth |
| **Animal** | Glands, organs, secretions, whole animals | Cod liver oil, Shark liver oil, Insulin (pancreas), Thyroid, Heparin (lung/intestinal mucosa), Gelatin, Honey, Beeswax, Wool fat (lanolin), Musk, Cochineal, Cantharides, Spermaceti, Pepsin, Trypsin, Vitamin A & D, Placental extract |
| **Mineral** | Inorganic substances of earth | Kaolin, Bentonite, Talc, Sulphur, Borax, Boric acid, Alum, Magnesium sulphate, Sodium chloride, Iodine, Calamine, Liquid paraffin & Petrolatum (mineral/petroleum origin), Bismuth salts, Ferrous sulphate |
| **Microbial** | Fungi, bacteria, actinomycetes — via fermentation | Penicillin (*Penicillium chrysogenum/notatum*), Streptomycin (*Streptomyces griseus*), Chloramphenicol (*S. venezuelae*), Tetracycline (*S. aureofaciens*), Erythromycin (*S. erythreus*), Griseofulvin, Ergot alkaloids (*Claviceps purpurea*), Lovastatin |
| **Marine** | Marine flora & fauna | Agar & Alginates (seaweeds), Carrageenan, Cod/shark liver oil, Cephalosporin C (marine fungus *Cephalosporium acremonium*), Cytarabine (sponge-derived model), Spongothymidine |
| **Synthetic** | Made wholly in the laboratory | Aspirin, Paracetamol, Sulphonamides, Chloroquine, Chloramphenicol (now synthetic), Barbiturates, Phenytoin |
| **Semi-synthetic** | Natural nucleus chemically modified | Ampicillin/Amoxicillin (from 6-APA), Cephalexin (from 7-ACA), Ethinyl-oestradiol, Homatropine, Diacetylmorphine (heroin), Bromoscopolamine, Dihydroergotamine |
| **Biotechnological / r-DNA** | Genetically engineered | Human insulin, Human growth hormone, Interferons, Erythropoietin, Hepatitis-B vaccine, Streptokinase, tPA |

🧠 **MEMORY HOOK for natural sources — "PAM-MM"** → **P**lant, **A**nimal, **M**ineral,
**M**icrobial, **M**arine.

⚠️ **TRAP** — *Liquid paraffin, hard paraffin, soft paraffin (petrolatum) and paraffin wax are of
MINERAL (petroleum) origin — not animal, not vegetable.* Whereas **wool fat (lanolin), beeswax,
spermaceti and cod liver oil are ANIMAL.** **Carnauba, candelilla and cocoa butter are VEGETABLE.**

### Scheme 2 — Classification by PHYSICAL STATE

| State | Examples |
|---|---|
| **Solid** | Powders, crystals (potassium permanganate), granules, waxes, gums, dried leaves |
| **Semisolid** | Ointments, creams, pastes, gels, soft extracts, balsams, resinous exudates |
| **Liquid** | Water, syrups, tinctures, fixed & volatile oils, glycerin, spirits |
| **Gaseous** | Oxygen, nitrous oxide, ether & chloroform vapour, aerosol propellants |

### Scheme 3 — Classification by PART / MORPHOLOGY (crude vegetable drugs)

| Group | Sub-group | Examples |
|---|---|---|
| **ORGANISED** (have cellular/histological structure; direct plant/animal parts) | Leaves | Senna, Digitalis, Eucalyptus, Vasaka, Belladonna, Datura |
| | Barks | Cinchona, Cinnamon, Cascara, Quillaia, Kurchi |
| | Roots & Rhizomes | Rauwolfia, Ipecac, Ginger, Turmeric, Liquorice, Jalap, Podophyllum, Aconite |
| | Flowers | Clove, Saffron, Chamomile, Pyrethrum, Santonica |
| | Fruits & Seeds | Fennel, Coriander, Colocynth, Nux vomica, Castor, Isapghula, Bael, Capsicum |
| | Woods | Sandalwood, Quassia, Red sandalwood |
| | Herbs / Entire plants | Ephedra, Mentha, Vinca, Ergot |
| **UNORGANISED** (no cellular structure; products/exudates/extractives) | Gums | Acacia, Tragacanth, Sterculia, Guar |
| | Resins & Oleoresins | Colophony, Asafoetida, Ginger oleoresin, Capsicum oleoresin |
| | Balsams | Tolu, Peru, Benzoin, Storax |
| | Latex & dried juices | Opium, Papain, Aloe, Kino, Catechu |
| | Fixed oils & fats | Castor, Arachis, Olive, Sesame, Cocoa butter |
| | Volatile oils | Peppermint, Clove, Eucalyptus, Lemon |
| | Waxes | Beeswax, Carnauba, Spermaceti |
| | Extracts | Liquid extract of liquorice, Belladonna dry extract |

⭐ **EXAM POINT — Organised vs Unorganised**

| Feature | Organised drug | Unorganised drug |
|---|---|---|
| Cellular structure | **Present** | **Absent** |
| Identification | By **microscopy** (histology) | By **physical & chemical** tests |
| Nature | Direct part of plant/animal | Product/exudate/extractive |
| Examples | Senna leaf, Cinchona bark | Acacia gum, Opium, Beeswax |

🧠 **MEMORY HOOK** — "**Organ**ised = you can see the **organ**/cells."

### Scheme 4 — Classification by THERAPEUTIC / PHARMACOLOGICAL ACTION

📌 The most heavily examined scheme in "Kinds of drugs". Learn each term's *meaning*, because MCQs
ask "Emetic means…?" or "Which of the following is a carminative?"

| Class (kind of drug) | Definition — what it does | Classical examples |
|---|---|---|
| **Analgesic** | Relieves pain without loss of consciousness | Aspirin, Paracetamol, Morphine |
| **Antipyretic** | Reduces fever | Paracetamol, Aspirin |
| **Anaesthetic (general)** | Produces reversible loss of all sensation + consciousness | Ether, Halothane, Thiopentone |
| **Anaesthetic (local)** | Loss of sensation in a limited area | Lignocaine, Procaine, Benzocaine |
| **Antiseptic** | Prevents growth of micro-organisms on **living tissue** | Dettol, Boric acid, Iodine, Gentian violet |
| **Disinfectant / Germicide** | **Kills** micro-organisms on **inanimate objects** | Phenol, Cresol, Bleaching powder, Formaldehyde |
| **Antibiotic** | Substance of microbial origin that kills/inhibits microbes | Penicillin, Streptomycin |
| **Anthelmintic / Vermifuge / Vermicide** | Expels (vermifuge) or kills (vermicide) intestinal worms | Piperazine, Santonin, Mebendazole, Male fern, Quassia |
| **Amoebicide** | Kills *Entamoeba histolytica* | Emetine, Metronidazole |
| **Antacid** | Neutralises gastric acid | Sodium bicarbonate, Magnesium trisilicate, Aluminium hydroxide gel |
| **Carminative** | Relieves flatulence, expels gas from GIT | Peppermint oil, Ginger, Cardamom, Fennel, Dill, Ajowan, Asafoetida, Sodium bicarbonate |
| **Cathartic / Purgative / Laxative** | Evacuates bowel — laxative (mild) → purgative → drastic cathartic | Isapghula (bulk), Liquid paraffin (lubricant), Magnesium sulphate (saline), Senna/Cascara (stimulant), Castor oil, Jalap, Colocynth (drastic) |
| **Antidiarrhoeal / Astringent** | Precipitates proteins, contracts tissue, checks discharge | Catechu, Kino, Tannic acid, Alum, Zinc oxide, Kaolin, Bismuth subcarbonate |
| **Emetic** | Induces **vomiting** | Ipecacuanha, Apomorphine, Mustard + warm water, Copper sulphate, Zinc sulphate |
| **Anti-emetic** | Prevents vomiting | Promethazine, Metoclopramide, Ginger |
| **Expectorant** | Promotes expulsion of bronchial secretion (loosens cough) | Ammonium chloride, Potassium iodide, Ipecac, Squill, Vasaka, Liquorice, Tolu |
| **Antitussive** | **Suppresses** cough reflex | Codeine, Noscapine, Dextromethorphan |
| **Demulcent** | Soothes and protects **internal/mucous** surfaces | Acacia, Tragacanth, Glycerin, Honey, Liquorice, Starch mucilage |
| **Emollient** | Softens and soothes **skin** (external) | Olive oil, Almond oil, Wool fat, Petrolatum, Cocoa butter |
| **Protective** | Forms a physical barrier on skin | Talc, Zinc oxide, Kaolin, Collodion, Titanium dioxide |
| **Counter-irritant / Rubefacient** | Irritant applied to skin to relieve deep-seated pain; reddens skin | Turpentine oil, Camphor, Menthol, Methyl salicylate, Mustard, Capsicum |
| **Vesicant** | Blisters the skin | Cantharides |
| **Escharotic / Caustic / Corrosive** | Destroys tissue, forms a scar (eschar) | Silver nitrate, Phenol, Trichloroacetic acid, Copper sulphate |
| **Keratolytic** | Softens & dissolves horny layer of skin | Salicylic acid, Sulphur, Resorcinol, Urea |
| **Antifungal** | Kills fungi | Benzoic acid, Salicylic acid (Whitfield's ointment), Griseofulvin, Clotrimazole |
| **Scabicide / Pediculicide** | Kills scabies mite / lice | Benzyl benzoate, Sulphur ointment, Gamma-BHC, Permethrin |
| **Diuretic** | Increases urine output | Frusemide, Thiazides, Caffeine, Theophylline, Potassium citrate |
| **Diaphoretic / Sudorific** | Increases sweating | Pilocarpine, Ammonium acetate solution |
| **Anhidrotic** | Reduces sweating | Aluminium chloride, Atropine |
| **Cholagogue** | Increases bile flow into duodenum | Magnesium sulphate, Bile salts |
| **Hepatic stimulant / Choleretic** | Increases bile secretion by liver | Bile salts, Dehydrocholic acid |
| **Bitter / Stomachic** | Bitter taste → reflex increase in appetite & gastric secretion | Gentian, Quassia, Chirata, Nux vomica, Cinchona |
| **Tonic** | Restores normal tone / general well-being | Iron & vitamin preparations, Gentian, Nux vomica |
| **Stimulant** | Increases functional activity of an organ/system | Caffeine, Nikethamide, Camphor, Amphetamine |
| **Sedative / Hypnotic** | Calms (sedative) / produces sleep (hypnotic) | Phenobarbitone, Diazepam, Bromides, Chloral hydrate |
| **Anticonvulsant / Antiepileptic** | Prevents convulsions | Phenytoin, Phenobarbitone, Diazepam |
| **Narcotic / Opioid analgesic** | Relieves severe pain, produces stupor & dependence | Morphine, Pethidine, Opium |
| **Antispasmodic** | Relieves smooth-muscle spasm | Atropine, Hyoscine, Belladonna, Dicyclomine |
| **Mydriatic** | **Dilates** pupil | Atropine, Homatropine, Ephedrine, Cocaine |
| **Miotic** | **Constricts** pupil | Pilocarpine, Physostigmine, Morphine |
| **Haematinic** | Corrects anaemia | Ferrous sulphate, Folic acid, Vitamin B12, Liver extract |
| **Haemostatic / Styptic** | Arrests bleeding | Alum, Tannic acid, Adrenaline, Vitamin K, Thrombin |
| **Anticoagulant** | Prevents clotting | Heparin, Warfarin, Sodium citrate, Dicoumarol |
| **Cardiotonic** | Increases force of cardiac contraction | Digitalis, Digoxin, Strophanthus, Squill |
| **Antihypertensive** | Lowers blood pressure | Rauwolfia (reserpine), Methyldopa, Nifedipine |
| **Vasodilator** | Dilates blood vessels | Glyceryl trinitrate, Amyl nitrite |
| **Vasoconstrictor / Pressor** | Constricts vessels, raises BP | Adrenaline, Ephedrine, Ergotamine |
| **Oxytocic / Ecbolic** | Stimulates uterine contraction | Ergometrine, Oxytocin, Ergot |
| **Emmenagogue** | Promotes menstrual flow | Ergot (classical), Aloes |
| **Abortifacient** | Induces abortion | Ergot, Quinine (large dose) |
| **Galactagogue** | Increases milk secretion | Metoclopramide, Fenugreek (traditional) |
| **Anti-galactagogue** | Suppresses lactation | Bromocriptine, Oestrogens |
| **Aphrodisiac** | Increases sexual desire | Nux vomica, Yohimbine, Cantharides |
| **Anaphrodisiac** | Decreases sexual desire | Camphor, Bromides |
| **Antidote** | Counteracts poison | Atropine (for organophosphates), Naloxone (for morphine), Universal antidote |
| **Anti-neoplastic / Cytotoxic** | Used in cancer | Vinca alkaloids, Podophyllum resin, Colchicine, Taxol |
| **Antidiabetic** | Lowers blood sugar | Insulin, Metformin, Sulphonylureas |
| **Antihistaminic** | Blocks histamine receptors | Chlorpheniramine, Promethazine |
| **Anti-inflammatory** | Reduces inflammation | Ibuprofen, Corticosteroids |
| **Antirheumatic** | Used in rheumatism | Salicylates, Colchicine (gout), Indomethacin |
| **Antileprotic** | Used in leprosy | Dapsone, Rifampicin, Chaulmoogra oil (classical) |
| **Antitubercular** | Used in TB | Streptomycin, INH, Rifampicin, Ethambutol |
| **Antimalarial** | Used in malaria | Quinine, Chloroquine, Primaquine, Artemisinin |
| **Antileishmanial** | Kala-azar | Sodium stibogluconate |
| **Antifilarial** | Filariasis | Diethylcarbamazine |
| **Antiviral** | Used in viral disease | Acyclovir, Idoxuridine |
| **Immunising agent** | Confers immunity | Vaccines (active), Antisera (passive) |
| **Anti-obesity / Anorectic** | Suppresses appetite | Amphetamine (classical) |
| **Refrigerant** | Cooling; relieves thirst & fever | Potassium nitrate, Citric acid, Lime juice, Tamarind |
| **Deodorant** | Removes bad odour | Chlorophyll, Potassium permanganate, Hydrogen peroxide |
| **Anti-perspirant** | Reduces perspiration | Aluminium chlorhydrate |
| **Sialagogue** | Increases salivation | Pilocarpine, Ginger, Cardamom |
| **Anti-sialagogue** | Decreases salivation | Atropine, Belladonna |
| **Mucolytic** | Liquefies mucus | Bromhexine, Acetylcysteine |
| **Antithyroid** | Depresses thyroid | Carbimazole, Methimazole |
| **Uricosuric** | Increases uric acid excretion | Probenecid, Sulphinpyrazone |
| **Urinary antiseptic** | Antiseptic in urinary tract | Methenamine (hexamine), Nitrofurantoin, Nalidixic acid |
| **Bulk / lubricant / saline / stimulant purgative** | See Cathartic (four sub-types) | Isapghula / Liquid paraffin / Mag. sulphate / Senna |

🧠 **MEMORY HOOK — the classic look-alike pairs**
* **Mydriatic** = **My**dri = **M**ore (dilate) · **Mio**tic = **Mi**nimise (constrict)
* **Emetic** causes vomiting · **Emollient** softens skin · **Demulcent** soothes inside
* **Antiseptic** = living tissue · **Disinfectant** = dead/ inanimate surface
* **Expectorant** brings sputum **out** · **Antitussive** shuts the cough **off**
* **Vermifuge** = worm **flees** · **Vermicide** = worm **dies**

### Scheme 5 — Classification by SITE / ROUTE OF ADMINISTRATION

| Route group | Routes | Typical dosage forms |
|---|---|---|
| **Enteral (via GIT)** | Oral, sublingual/buccal, rectal | Tablets, capsules, mixtures, syrups, suppositories, enemas |
| **Parenteral (injection)** | IV, IM, SC, ID, intrathecal, intra-articular, intracardiac | Injections, infusions, implants |
| **Topical** | Skin, eye, ear, nose, throat, vagina, urethra | Ointments, creams, lotions, drops, pessaries, bougies, sprays |
| **Respiratory / Inhalation** | Nose & lungs | Inhalations, nebulae, aerosols, insufflations, vapours, gases |
| **Transdermal** | Through intact skin for systemic action | Patches, medicated plasters |

### Scheme 6 — Classification by DOSAGE FORM (see also Ch. 7)

| Form | Sub-types |
|---|---|
| **Solid** | Powders, granules, tablets, capsules, pills, lozenges/troches, cachets, pastilles, suppositories, pessaries, bougies, implants, plasters |
| **Semisolid** | Ointments, creams, pastes, jellies/gels, cerates, poultices, glycerogelatins |
| **Liquid — monophasic** | Solutions, syrups, elixirs, tinctures, spirits, waters, extracts, infusions, decoctions, linctuses, gargles, mouthwashes, douches, enemas, lotions, liniments, paints, drops, sprays |
| **Liquid — biphasic** | Suspensions, emulsions, magmas/gels, mixtures |
| **Gaseous** | Aerosols, inhalations, medicinal gases, vapours |

### Scheme 7 — Classification by CHEMICAL NATURE / CONSTITUENT

| Chemical group | Nature | Drugs containing them |
|---|---|---|
| **Alkaloids** | Basic, N-containing, bitter, alkaline, mostly optically active; salts water-soluble, bases alcohol-soluble | Morphine, Quinine, Atropine, Reserpine, Ephedrine, Caffeine, Nicotine, Strychnine, Emetine, Colchicine, Ergotamine |
| **Glycosides** | Sugar + aglycone (genin) linked by glycosidic bond; hydrolysed by acid/enzyme | Digitoxin (cardiac), Sennosides (anthraquinone), Amygdalin (cyanogenetic), Sinigrin (isothiocyanate/glucosinolate), Salicin (phenolic), Barbaloin, Glycyrrhizin (saponin) |
| **Tannins** | Polyphenolic, astringent, precipitate proteins & gelatin, blue/green with FeCl₃ | Catechu, Kino, Myrobalan, Gall, Tea, Oak bark |
| **Volatile (essential) oils** | Odorous, volatile, steam-distillable, leave **no permanent grease stain** | Peppermint, Clove, Eucalyptus, Lemon, Cinnamon, Turpentine |
| **Fixed oils & fats** | Non-volatile glycerides, leave **permanent grease stain**, saponifiable, rancidify | Castor, Arachis, Olive, Sesame, Cod liver oil, Cocoa butter |
| **Waxes** | Esters of higher fatty acids + higher alcohols (not glycerol) | Beeswax, Carnauba, Spermaceti, Wool fat (sterol ester) |
| **Carbohydrates & derived products** | Sugars, starches, gums, mucilages, pectins, celluloses | Acacia, Tragacanth, Starch, Agar, Honey, Isapghula |
| **Resins & resin combinations** | Amorphous, non-N, water-insoluble, alcohol-soluble | Colophony, Asafoetida, Benzoin, Myrrh, Podophyllum |
| **Proteins & enzymes** | Amino-acid polymers | Gelatin, Papain, Pepsin, Trypsin, Insulin, Bromelain |
| **Vitamins & hormones** | Essential micronutrients / glandular secretions | Vitamin A, D, B-complex, Insulin, Thyroxine |
| **Steroids** | Cyclopentanoperhydrophenanthrene nucleus | Cortisone, Diosgenin, Digitoxigenin, Cholesterol |
| **Inorganic / mineral** | Salts, oxides, elements | Borax, Alum, Sulphur, Iodine, Magnesium sulphate |

### Scheme 8 — Classification by LEGAL STATUS (Drugs & Cosmetics Rules — schedules)

⭐ **VERY HIGH-YIELD TABLE**

| Schedule | Deals with |
|---|---|
| **Schedule A** | Application **forms** & licence forms |
| **Schedule B** | **Fees** for test/analysis by Central/State Drugs Laboratories |
| **Schedule C & C(1)** | **Biological & special products** (sera, vaccines, toxins, hormones, sterile injections) |
| **Schedule D** | **Exemptions** for import of drugs |
| **Schedule E(1)** | List of **poisonous substances** under Ayurvedic, Siddha & Unani systems |
| **Schedule F, F(1), F(2), F(3)** | Requirements for **blood banks, vaccines, surgical dressings, umbilical tapes** |
| **Schedule FF** | Standards for **ophthalmic preparations** |
| **Schedule G** | Drugs to be used **only under medical supervision** ("Caution" label) — e.g. many antineoplastics |
| **Schedule H** | **Prescription-only drugs** — label must bear **Rx** and the warning "to be sold by retail on the prescription of a Registered Medical Practitioner only" |
| **Schedule H1** | Certain antibiotics/anti-TB/habit-forming drugs — separate register, **red bordered box with Rx** |
| **Schedule J** | Diseases/ailments for which a drug **may not claim** to prevent or cure |
| **Schedule K** | **Exemptions** from certain provisions |
| **Schedule M** | **Good Manufacturing Practices (GMP)** & requirements of premises, plant & equipment |
| **Schedule M-I / M-II / M-III** | GMP for homoeopathic / cosmetics / medical devices |
| **Schedule N** | **Minimum equipment for a pharmacy** (essential for dispensing exams) |
| **Schedule O** | Standards for **disinfectant fluids** |
| **Schedule P** | **Life period (shelf-life)** & storage conditions of drugs |
| **Schedule P1** | **Pack sizes** of drugs |
| **Schedule Q** | Permitted **colours** & dyes in cosmetics |
| **Schedule R / R1** | Standards for **mechanical contraceptives** / condoms |
| **Schedule S** | Standards for **cosmetics** |
| **Schedule T** | **GMP for Ayurveda, Siddha & Unani** drugs |
| **Schedule U / U1** | **Manufacturing records** to be maintained (drugs / cosmetics) |
| **Schedule V** | Standards for **patent & proprietary medicines** (vitamin content) |
| **Schedule W** | Drugs marketed under **generic names only** (now largely omitted) |
| **Schedule X** | **Narcotic & psychotropic** drugs — special licence, **duplicate prescription retained 2 years**, separate storage |
| **Schedule Y** | Requirements & guidelines for **clinical trials / new drug** approval |

🧠 **MEMORY HOOK** — "**H** = **H**and over only against prescription; **X** = e**X**tra strict
(narcotics, keep duplicate copy); **G** = warning ‘to be taken under medical supervision’;
**N** = mi**N**imum equipment of pharmacy; **P** = shelf-**P**eriod; **M** = **M**anufacturing (GMP);
**Y** = clinical trials (Y = wh**Y** test?)."

⭐ Other legal "kinds of drugs":
* **New drug** — a drug not used in the country to any significant extent, or a new claim/route/
  dosage; treated as new for **4 years** from approval.
* **Spurious drug** — imitates or is a substitute for another drug, or bears a false label of
  another manufacturer (fraudulent).
* **Misbranded drug** — coloured/coated/powdered so as to conceal damage, or false/misleading label.
* **Adulterated drug** — contains filthy/putrid substance, unhygienically prepared, harmful colour
  or container.
* **Patent & proprietary medicine** — formulation of a licensed manufacturer under a brand name.
* **Bulk drug / API** — the active pharmaceutical ingredient in bulk.
* **Generic drug** — marketed under its non-proprietary (official) name.
* **Orphan drug** — for a rare disease, commercially unattractive.
* **Essential drug** — WHO/ National List of Essential Medicines (NLEM), satisfies priority
  healthcare needs.
* **OTC drug** — sold without prescription.
* **Habit-forming drug** — listed in Rule 76 (e.g. barbiturates, codeine).
* **Prohibited/banned FDC** — fixed-dose combinations banned under Sec. 26A.

---

## 1.3 CHARACTERISTICS OF DRUGS

📌 **DEFINITION** — The *characteristics* of a drug are the sum of its **organoleptic,
morphological, physical, chemical and biological properties**, which together are used to
**identify** the drug, **judge its quality**, and **detect adulteration**.

🧠 **MEMORY HOOK for the 5 characteristics/evaluation heads — "MOPCB"**
**M**orphological → **O**rganoleptic → **P**hysical → **C**hemical → **B**iological.
(Often taught as: Organoleptic/Morphological, Microscopical, Physical, Chemical, Biological.)

### A. ORGANOLEPTIC CHARACTERS (sensory — "evaluation by sense organs")

| Character | What is noted | Illustrative examples |
|---|---|---|
| **Colour** | Natural colour of surface & fracture | Turmeric — yellow; Saffron — orange-red; Gentian violet — violet; Potassium permanganate — dark purple; Ferrous sulphate — pale bluish-green; Copper sulphate — blue; Sulphur — yellow; Calamine — pink; Iodine — greyish-black lustrous |
| **Odour** | Aromatic, terebinthinate, alliaceous, balsamic, odourless | Asafoetida — strong **alliaceous** (garlic-like); Clove — aromatic; Turpentine — terebinthinate; Peru & Tolu balsam — balsamic vanilla-like; Valerian — unpleasant/ disagreeable |
| **Taste** | Bitter, sweet, saline, acidic, acrid, astringent, mucilaginous, tasteless | Quinine/Gentian/Chirata — intensely **bitter**; Liquorice — **sweet**; Alum & Catechu — **astringent**; Magnesium sulphate — **saline bitter**; Sodium chloride — saline; Capsicum & Ginger — **pungent/acrid**; Acacia — **mucilaginous**; Starch — tasteless |
| **Texture / Touch** | Smooth, rough, gritty, oily, greasy, unctuous | Talc — smooth soapy; Kaolin — earthy, adheres to tongue; Liquid paraffin — oily; Wool fat — unctuous |
| **Sound / Fracture** | Snap, crack, brittle, fibrous, horny, splintery | Cinchona bark — **fibrous** fracture; Cinnamon — **short**; Quillaia — **splintery**; Liquorice — coarsely fibrous; Gum acacia — brittle, glassy |
| **Appearance/shape** | Quilled, channelled, curved, recurved, compound, single | Cinnamon — **quilled/compound quill**; Cinchona — quills/channelled; Senna — lanceolate leaflets with **asymmetric base** |

⭐ **EXAM POINT** — Organoleptic evaluation is also called **evaluation by "organs of sense"**;
it is the **simplest, quickest, least precise** method, and always the **first** step.

### B. MORPHOLOGICAL / MICROSCOPICAL CHARACTERS

| Feature | Significance |
|---|---|
| **Size & shape** | Measured with stage micrometer; e.g. starch grain size distinguishes maize, wheat, potato, rice starch |
| **Stomata & stomatal index/number** | Anisocytic (Cruciferous — Datura, Belladonna), Paracytic (Rubiaceous — Senna, Coca), Diacytic (Caryophyllaceous — Mentha, Vasaka), Anomocytic (Ranunculaceous — Digitalis, Buchu) |
| **Trichomes** | Covering (non-glandular) vs glandular; uni/multicellular, stellate, T-shaped |
| **Calcium oxalate crystals** | Rosette (Senna, Cascara), prismatic, acicular/raphides (Squill, Ipecac), microsphenoidal, sandy |
| **Vein-islet number / veinlet termination number / palisade ratio** | **Leaf constants** — used to identify entire & powdered leaf drugs |
| **Lignified elements** | Fibres, tracheids, vessels, sclereids, stone cells |
| **Starch, aleurone grains, oil globules, inulin** | Cell inclusions |

🔢 **NUMBERS TO MEMORISE (leaf constants — classical values)**

| Drug | Palisade ratio | Stomatal index | Vein-islet no. |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Senna (Alexandrian)** | 5.5–15.0 | ~10–15 | ~25 |
| **Digitalis purpurea** | 3.7–4.2 (–5.4) | 2.5–4.5 | 2–5 |
| **Belladonna** | 6–10 | ~20 (upper 2–5) | ~2–7 |

⭐ **Palisade ratio** = average number of palisade cells beneath **one** upper epidermal cell.
⭐ **Stomatal index** = [S/(E+S)] × 100, where S = number of stomata, E = number of epidermal cells.
⭐ **Vein-islet number** = number of vein-islets per **mm²** in the central part of the lamina.
⭐ **Veinlet termination number** = number of veinlet terminations per mm².

### C. PHYSICAL CHARACTERISTICS (physical constants)

| Constant | Meaning / use | Typical exam values |
|---|---|---|
| **Solubility** | Nature & extent of dissolution in solvents | Volatile oils — soluble in alcohol; Gums — water-soluble, alcohol-**insoluble**; Resins — alcohol-soluble, water-**insoluble**; Balsams — partly both |
| **Specific gravity (relative density)** | Mass of substance / mass of equal volume of water at same temp | Water = 1.000; Alcohol (ethanol) ≈ 0.79; Glycerin ≈ 1.26; Chloroform ≈ 1.47–1.48; Liquid paraffin 0.83–0.89; Castor oil 0.945–0.965; Clove oil ≈ 1.04–1.06 (**heavier than water**); Turpentine oil ≈ 0.86 |
| **Melting point** | Sharp for pure chemicals | Cocoa butter (theobroma oil) **30–35 °C** (m.p. below body temp — key for suppositories); Beeswax 62–65 °C; Hard paraffin 50–57 °C; White soft paraffin (melting range) 38–56 °C; Wool fat 38–44 °C |
| **Boiling point** | For liquids | Water 100 °C; Alcohol 78.4 °C; Ether 34.6 °C; Chloroform 61 °C |
| **Optical rotation** | Rotation of plane-polarised light (polarimeter); +dextro / –laevo | Peppermint oil **laevo**-rotatory; Turpentine oil dextro; Quinine laevo; Sucrose dextro (+66.5°) |
| **Refractive index** | Ratio of velocity of light in air to that in the substance (Abbé refractometer) | Water 1.333; Volatile oils 1.45–1.55; Fixed oils ~1.46–1.48 |
| **Viscosity** | Resistance to flow | Glycerin, liquid paraffin high; controls pourability of mixtures |
| **Moisture content (LOD)** | Loss on drying — checks over-hydration & mould risk | Usually **< 5 %**; crude drugs must be dried to < 8–14 % |
| **Ash values** | **Total ash** (all inorganic), **acid-insoluble ash** (silica/sand → detects earthy adulteration), **water-soluble ash**, **sulphated ash** | Acid-insoluble ash of Ginger NMT 2 %; Senna total ash NMT 12 % |
| **Extractive values** | Alcohol-soluble, water-soluble, ether-soluble extractives — measure of active constituents | Low value = exhausted/adulterated drug |
| **Volatile oil content** | By Clevenger apparatus / hydro-distillation | Clove NLT 15 % v/w; Cinnamon bark ~1 % |
| **Swelling index** | Volume in mL occupied by 1 g of drug after swelling in water for 4 h | **Isapghula NLT 40**; Linseed ~4; Agar & Tragacanth high |
| **Foaming index** | Froth height — for saponin-containing drugs | Quillaia, Liquorice, Senega |
| **Bitterness value** | Reciprocal of the dilution still bitter, expressed in units per g relative to quinine HCl | Gentian ≥ 10 000; Quassia ≥ 3 500 |
| **Haemolytic index** | Saponin content measured by RBC lysis relative to standard Senega | Saponin drugs |
| **Iodine value** | g of iodine absorbed by 100 g fat → **degree of unsaturation** | Linseed oil very high (drying oil), Castor 82–90, Coconut low (~7–10) |
| **Acid value** | mg KOH needed to neutralise free fatty acids in 1 g → **rancidity indicator** | Should be low & is limited in pharmacopoeias |
| **Saponification value** | mg KOH needed to saponify 1 g fat → **inverse of molecular weight** of glycerides | Castor 176–187; Coconut 250–264 |
| **Ester value** | Sap. value – Acid value | For oils & waxes |
| **Acetyl value** | mg KOH to neutralise acetic acid from 1 g acetylated fat → hydroxyl groups | **Castor oil high** (ricinoleic –OH) |
| **Unsaponifiable matter** | Fraction not saponified | Liquid paraffin is fully unsaponifiable |
| **Fluorescence** | Under UV | Quinine — blue fluorescence in acid; Ergot & Aloe show characteristic fluorescence |
| **Deliquescence** | Absorbs so much atmospheric moisture that it **liquefies** | Calcium chloride, Zinc chloride, Potassium/Sodium hydroxide, Ferric chloride, Ammonium nitrate, Pepsin, Trypsin |
| **Efflorescence** | **Loses** water of crystallisation to air and becomes powdery | Sodium carbonate (Na₂CO₃·10H₂O), Ferrous sulphate, Magnesium sulphate, Sodium sulphate (Glauber's salt), Alum, Atropine sulphate, Codeine phosphate, Citric acid |
| **Hygroscopicity** | Absorbs moisture without liquefying | Glycerin, Sorbitol, PEG, Ammonium chloride |
| **Sublimation** | Solid → vapour → solid on heating, without melting | **Camphor, Menthol, Thymol, Iodine, Naphthalene, Benzoic acid, Salicylic acid, Ammonium chloride, Sulphur (partly)** |
| **Volatility** | Evaporates at room temperature | Alcohol, ether, chloroform, volatile oils, camphor |

⚠️ **TRAP — Efflorescent vs Deliquescent vs Hygroscopic**
* **Efflorescent** → **gives out** water → becomes **powdery** (Na₂CO₃·10H₂O).
* **Deliquescent** → **takes in** water → becomes **liquid** (CaCl₂).
* **Hygroscopic** → takes in moisture but **stays solid/semi-solid** (glycerin, NH₄Cl).
🧠 "Efflor**escent** = water **exits**; **D**eliquescent = **D**issolves itself."

⚠️ **TRAP — Volatile vs Fixed oil**

| Feature | Volatile (essential) oil | Fixed oil / fat |
|---|---|---|
| Volatility | Volatile, evaporates | Non-volatile |
| Grease stain on paper | **Temporary** (disappears on warming) | **Permanent** |
| Steam distillation | **Possible** | Not possible |
| Saponification | Not saponified (generally) | **Saponified** by alkali |
| Rancidity | Resinify/oxidise | **Become rancid** |
| Chemistry | Terpenes, sesquiterpenes, phenols, aldehydes | Glycerides of fatty acids |
| Odour | Strongly odorous | Nearly odourless |
| Examples | Clove, peppermint, eucalyptus, turpentine | Castor, olive, arachis, cod-liver |

### D. CHEMICAL CHARACTERISTICS

* **Nature of the active constituent** — alkaloid, glycoside, tannin, resin, volatile oil, etc.
* **Assay** — quantitative determination of active principle: acid–base, non-aqueous, complexometric,
  iodimetric, gravimetric, colorimetric/spectrophotometric, chromatographic (HPLC/GC).
* **Limit tests** — for chloride, sulphate, iron, arsenic, heavy metals, lead.
* **Identification tests** — colour reactions (see Ch. 2 & Ch. 13).
* **pH / acidity–alkalinity**, **loss on ignition**, **water content (Karl Fischer)**.
* **Chemical incompatibility potential** (see Ch. 8).

🧪 **CLASSICAL GROUP TESTS worth memorising**

| Constituent | Test | Positive result |
|---|---|---|
| **Alkaloid** | Dragendorff's (K-bismuth iodide) | Orange-red precipitate |
| | Mayer's (K-mercuric iodide) | Cream/white precipitate |
| | Wagner's (iodine–KI) | Reddish-brown precipitate |
| | Hager's (picric acid) | Yellow precipitate |
| | Tannic acid | Buff precipitate |
| **Glycoside (general)** | Molisch's | Purple ring |
| **Cardiac glycoside** | Baljet | Orange/yellow with picric acid–NaOH |
| | Keller–Kiliani | Reddish-brown ring at junction (deoxy-sugar) |
| | Legal's (Na-nitroprusside) | Pink to blood-red |
| | Kedde | Purple/violet |
| **Anthraquinone glycoside** | **Borntrager's** | Pink/red colour in ammonia layer |
| **Saponin glycoside** | Foam test | Persistent honeycomb froth |
| | Haemolysis test | Lysis of RBC |
| **Cyanogenetic glycoside** | Sodium picrate paper | Brick-red |
| **Tannin** | Ferric chloride | Blue-black (gallotannin) / green-black (catechol tannin) |
| | Gelatin test | White precipitate |
| | Goldbeater's skin | Brown colour |
| **Flavonoid** | Shinoda (Mg + HCl) | Pink/magenta |
| **Steroid/triterpene** | Salkowski / Liebermann–Burchard | Red / blue-green |
| **Protein** | Biuret | Violet |
| | Ninhydrin (amino acid) | Blue-purple |
| **Starch** | Iodine | Blue-black |
| **Reducing sugar** | Fehling's / Benedict's | Brick-red precipitate |
| **Fixed oil** | Grease spot on paper | Permanent translucent stain |
| **Volatile oil** | Sudan III / grease spot | Red-stained globules / temporary stain |
| **Mucilage** | Ruthenium red | Pink/red |
| | Iodine + water | Swells, no blue |
| **Lignin** | Phloroglucinol + HCl | Pink/red |
| **Calcium oxalate** | Insoluble in acetic acid, soluble in HCl (no effervescence) | Crystals persist |
| **Calcium carbonate** | Soluble in HCl **with effervescence** | Gas evolved |

### E. BIOLOGICAL CHARACTERISTICS (Bioassay)

📌 **Bioassay** = estimation of the **potency** of a drug by observing its effect on **living
matter** (animal, isolated tissue, micro-organism), compared with a **standard preparation**.

Used when chemical assay is impossible/unreliable:
* **Digitalis** — pigeon/guinea-pig, cat method (emesis in pigeon; cardiac arrest).
* **Insulin** — rabbit blood-sugar method / mouse convulsion.
* **Oxytocin** — isolated rat uterus, fowl blood pressure.
* **Adrenaline** — rabbit intestine / cat blood pressure.
* **Histamine** — guinea-pig ileum.
* **Vitamin D** — rat line test (antirachitic).
* **Antibiotics** — cup-plate / turbidimetric microbial assay.
* **Vaccines & sera** — LD₅₀, ED₅₀ methods; **pyrogen test (rabbit)**; **sterility test**.
* **Toxicity test** — LD₅₀ (Litchfield & Wilcoxon; Reed–Muench).
* **Types of bioassay** — Qualitative vs Quantitative; **Direct** (Digitalis in cat) vs
  **Indirect**; Graded response; All-or-none; **Matching**; **Bracketing**; **Interpolation**;
  **Multiple-point (2-, 3-, 4-point)**.

---

## 1.4 EVALUATION OF DRUGS (Standardisation)

📌 **DEFINITION** — Evaluation of a drug means confirming its **identity**, determining its
**quality & purity**, and **detecting the nature of adulteration**.

⭐ **Five methods (learn the order):**
1. **Organoleptic / Morphological** — sensory, macroscopic.
2. **Microscopical** — histology, cell contents, leaf constants, powder analysis.
3. **Physical** — solubility, m.p., ash, extractive, moisture, refractive index, optical rotation,
   swelling & foaming index, viscosity, specific gravity.
4. **Chemical** — qualitative tests, assays, limit tests.
5. **Biological** — bioassay, toxicity, antimicrobial activity, pyrogen & sterility test.

🧠 **MEMORY HOOK — "O-M-P-C-B"** ("**O**ld **M**an **P**icks **C**rude **B**ark").

### Adulteration & Substitution

📌 **Adulteration** = intentional or unintentional substitution of a drug wholly or partly with an
inferior, spurious, defective or harmful substance.

| Type of adulteration | Description | Example |
|---|---|---|
| **Substitution with substandard commercial variety** | Similar-looking cheaper drug | Indian senna for Alexandrian senna; Japanese mint for peppermint |
| **Substitution with exhausted drug** | Active constituent already extracted | Exhausted ginger, clove (oil removed), gentian, fennel |
| **Substitution with artificially manufactured substance** | Synthetic look-alike | Paraffin wax blocks coloured as beeswax; artificial invert sugar for honey |
| **Substitution with inferior/foreign organic matter** | Other plant parts or unrelated matter | Senna stalks & pods with leaves; ergot-free rye; papaya seeds for pepper |
| **Harmful adulterants** | Waste/harmful material | Limestone, white oil, glass pieces, iron filings, mud |
| **Adulteration of powders** | Cheap powdered filler | Powdered bark/dextrin/starch in powdered drugs; brick powder in chilli/turmeric |
| **Addition of inseparable matter (sophistication)** | Cannot be removed by sifting | Water sprinkling to increase weight; sand & stone in gums |
| **Vitiation (deterioration)** | Loss of quality by age, moisture, heat, light, insects | Rancid oils, mouldy drugs, faded flowers |
| **Inferiority** | Genuine but of poor quality | Under-mature or wrongly collected drug |
| **Spoilage** | Microbial/insect attack | Weevils in cereal drugs |

🧪 **Common bazaar adulteration detection (frequently asked)**
* **Turmeric powder** + metanil yellow → add conc. HCl to aqueous extract → **violet/pink** colour.
* **Chilli powder** + brick powder/sand → shake in water → grit settles; red colour separates.
* **Asafoetida** adulterated with starch/soapstone → **iodine gives blue** (starch);
  ash residue high.
* **Honey** adulterated with sugar syrup → **Fiehe's test** (resorcinol + HCl → cherry-red for
  invert sugar/HMF).
* **Beeswax** with paraffin → melting point & saponification value change.
* **Silver leaf (chandi ka warq)** with aluminium → aluminium burns into grey-black ash ball.
* **Coconut oil** with other oils → freeze test / cool below 24 °C.
* **Milk** with starch → iodine blue; with water → lactometer.
* **Ghee/butter** with vanaspati → **Baudouin test** (furfural + HCl → **pink/crimson**, detects
  sesame oil marker).
* **Mustard oil** with argemone oil → **nitric acid test** (brownish-red).

---

## 1.5 DETERIORATION, STORAGE & SHELF-LIFE OF DRUGS

### Causes of deterioration

| Factor | Effect | Example |
|---|---|---|
| **Air (O₂)** | Oxidation | Vitamin C, adrenaline, fixed oils turning rancid, ferrous salts → ferric |
| **Moisture / humidity** | Hydrolysis, caking, mould, effervescence | Aspirin → salicylic acid + acetic acid; effervescent powders; ampicillin |
| **Light** | Photolysis, discolouration | Silver salts, chlorpromazine, riboflavin, phenothiazines, sodium nitroprusside |
| **Heat** | Increased reaction rate, melting, loss of volatiles | Suppositories melt, vitamins degrade, insulin denatures |
| **Micro-organisms** | Spoilage, fermentation | Syrups, mucilages, infusions (short shelf-life!) |
| **Insects & rodents** | Physical destruction, contamination | Crude vegetable drugs |
| **Enzymes** | Autolysis, hydrolysis of glycosides | Digitalis leaf must be dried below 60 °C to kill enzymes |
| **Trace metals** | Catalyse oxidation | Ascorbic acid + Cu²⁺/Fe³⁺ |
| **pH** | Acid/base catalysed hydrolysis | Penicillin unstable in acid; procaine hydrolyses |
| **Time / age** | Loss of potency | Fixed shelf life (Schedule P) |

### Signs of a deteriorated drug ("recognition of spoilage")
* Change in **colour** (faded/darkened), **odour** (rancid, sour, mouldy), **taste**.
* **Caking, clumping, mottling, softening, liquefaction** of solids.
* **Cloudiness, precipitate, sediment, gas, cracking of emulsion**, mould growth in liquids.
* **Loss of effervescence**; **cracked/leaking/swollen** containers; **crystal growth**.
* Ointments: **separation of liquid (bleeding)**, rancid smell, granular feel.

### General storage rules (pharmacopoeial terminology)

| Term | Temperature |
|---|---|
| **Deep freezer** | –20 °C to –10 °C (below –18 °C) |
| **Cold / refrigerator** | **2 °C – 8 °C** |
| **Cool place** | **8 °C – 25 °C** |
| **Room temperature** | 15 °C – 30 °C (IP: 15–25 °C) |
| **Warm** | 30 °C – 40 °C |
| **Excessive heat** | above 40 °C |

🔢 **NUMBER TO MEMORISE** — **Cold storage = 2–8 °C** (vaccines, insulin, sera, ergometrine,
oxytocin, suppositories, most eye drops after opening).

⭐ **Storage practice**
* **Light-sensitive** → **amber/ dark coloured, light-resistant** container.
* **Moisture-sensitive/deliquescent** → **well-closed, air-tight** container, in a dry place;
  add **desiccant** (silica gel).
* **Volatile & sublimable** (camphor, iodine, ether, spirit) → **tightly/hermetically closed**,
  cool place, away from flame.
* **Inflammable** (ether, alcohol, collodion) → cool, away from fire, separate flame-proof store.
* **Oxidising agents** (KMnO₄, chlorates, peroxides) stored **away from reducing/organic matter**
  (fire hazard).
* **Poisons & Schedule X** → separate **locked** cupboard marked "POISON", register maintained.
* **Narcotics** → double-locked safe.
* **Vegetable crude drugs** → dry, cool, dark, insect-proof (fumigation with e.g. carbon
  tetrachloride/ chloroform classically; today gamma irradiation/ ETO).
* **Antibiotics, biologicals, vaccines** → cold chain, never frozen (except as directed).
* **Rubber/plastic-incompatible** drugs → glass only.

📌 **Shelf-life (expiry date)** — the time during which a drug retains **not less than 90 % (or
95 % for some) of its labelled potency** under the stated storage conditions. In India, expiry is
governed by **Schedule P**.

⭐ **Preparations with very short shelf-life (extemporaneous)**
* **Infusions — 12 hours** (fresh); concentrated infusions keep longer.
* **Mucilages, decoctions — 24 hours to a few days** (require preservative).
* **Emulsions & suspensions (unpreserved)** — a few days.
* **Aqueous extemporaneous mixtures** — usually labelled to be used within **7–14 days**.

---

## 1.6 DRUG NOMENCLATURE

| Name type | Meaning | Example |
|---|---|---|
| **Chemical name** | Systematic IUPAC name | N-acetyl-p-aminophenol |
| **Non-proprietary / Generic / Official name** | Approved common name (INN, IP name) | Paracetamol (INN) / Acetaminophen (USAN) |
| **Proprietary / Brand / Trade name** | Manufacturer's registered name | Crocin®, Calpol® |
| **Code name** | Pre-approval research code | RO-15-1788 |
| **Biological/ botanical name** | Binomial Latin name of source | *Cinchona officinalis* |

⭐ **Pharmacopoeias & official books**

| Book | Abbrev. | Note |
|---|---|---|
| Indian Pharmacopoeia | **IP** | 1st edition **1955**; Addendum 1960; 2nd 1966; latest editions by **IPC, Ghaziabad**; official standards under Sec. 16 of D&C Act. Legal book of standards for India. |
| British Pharmacopoeia | BP | First 1864 |
| United States Pharmacopeia–National Formulary | USP-NF | USP first 1820 |
| International Pharmacopoeia | Ph. Int. | Published by **WHO** — **not legally binding**, recommendatory |
| European Pharmacopoeia | Ph. Eur. | Council of Europe |
| British Pharmaceutical Codex | BPC | Not legal, informative |
| National Formulary of India | **NFI** | First published **1960**; guides rational prescribing/dispensing |
| Ayurvedic/Siddha/Unani/Homoeopathic Pharmacopoeia of India | API/SPI/UPI/HPI | Official for ASU&H drugs |
| Extra Pharmacopoeia | **Martindale** | Comprehensive reference, not official |

🧠 **MEMORY HOOK** — "**I**ndia **1955**, **U**SA **1820**, **B**ritain **1864**, **NFI 1960**,
**WHO** = *International* Pharmacopoeia (**advisory only**)."

---

## 1.7 CHAPTER 1 — RAPID REVISION SHEET

* Drug ← French **"Drogue"** = dry herb.
* Sources: **P**lant, **A**nimal, **M**ineral, **M**icrobial, **M**arine, Synthetic,
  Semi-synthetic, Biotechnological.
* **Organised** drug = has cells (identified microscopically); **Unorganised** = no cells
  (identified by physical/chemical tests).
* **Antiseptic** → living tissue; **Disinfectant** → inanimate object.
* **Mydriatic** dilates; **Miotic** constricts.
* **Emollient** = external softener; **Demulcent** = internal soother.
* **Vermifuge** expels; **Vermicide** kills.
* **Carminative** relieves flatulence; **Refrigerant** cools and quenches thirst.
* **Efflorescent** loses water (Na₂CO₃·10H₂O, FeSO₄, MgSO₄, alum, citric acid);
  **Deliquescent** absorbs water & liquefies (CaCl₂, ZnCl₂, NaOH, KOH, FeCl₃).
* **Sublimable**: camphor, menthol, thymol, iodine, benzoic acid, salicylic acid, NH₄Cl,
  naphthalene.
* **Volatile oil** → temporary grease stain, steam-distillable; **Fixed oil** → permanent stain,
  saponifiable, becomes rancid.
* Evaluation = **O**rganoleptic, **M**icroscopic, **P**hysical, **C**hemical, **B**iological.
* **Acid-insoluble ash** detects **sand/earthy adulteration**.
* **Swelling index of Isapghula ≥ 40**.
* **Iodine value** → unsaturation; **Acid value** → rancidity; **Saponification value** → inverse
  of molecular weight; **Acetyl value high** → **castor oil**.
* **Cold storage = 2–8 °C**; **cool place = 8–25 °C**.
* **Infusion shelf-life = 12 hours**.
* **Schedule H** = prescription drug, **X** = narcotic (retain copy 2 years), **N** = minimum
  pharmacy equipment, **M** = GMP, **P** = shelf-life, **Y** = clinical trials.
* IP **1955**; NFI **1960**; *International Pharmacopoeia* by **WHO** (not legally enforceable).
* **Bioassay** = potency measured on living tissue against a standard.

# CHAPTER 2
# 🌿 GUMS, MUCILAGES, RESINS, BALSAMS, OLEORESINS & LATEX
### ("Balsams, Gums etc." of the syllabus)

---

## 2.1 THE FAMILY TREE OF PLANT EXUDATES

```
                        PLANT EXUDATES / SECRETIONS
                                    │
        ┌───────────────────────────┼───────────────────────────┐
   CARBOHYDRATE TYPE           RESINOUS TYPE               OTHERS
        │                           │                          │
   ┌────┴────┐          ┌───────────┼───────────┐        ┌─────┴─────┐
  GUMS   MUCILAGES    RESINS   OLEORESINS   BALSAMS    LATEX      DRIED
 (exudate) (in cells)  (pure)  (resin+VO)  (resin +   (milky      JUICES
                                            benzoic/   juice)    (Aloe,
                                            cinnamic             Kino,
                                            acid)                Catechu)
                            │
                    ┌───────┴────────┐
              OLEO-GUM-RESINS   GLYCO-RESINS
              (gum+resin+VO)    (resin+glycoside)
              Asafoetida,       Jalap, Podophyllum
              Myrrh, Gamboge
```

⭐ **THE SINGLE MOST IMPORTANT DEFINITION TABLE OF THIS CHAPTER**

| Class | Composition | Key identifying feature |
|---|---|---|
| **Gum** | Carbohydrate (polysaccharide salts) | **Water-soluble/ swells**; alcohol-insoluble |
| **Mucilage** | Carbohydrate — **normal cell constituent** | Formed **inside** the plant, not by injury |
| **Resin** | Complex mixture of resin acids, resin alcohols (resinols), resinotannols, esters, inert resenes | Water-**insoluble**, **alcohol-soluble**; burns with sooty flame |
| **Oleoresin** | **Resin + Volatile oil** | Natural (Turpentine, Copaiba) or prepared (Ginger, Capsicum) |
| **Oleo-gum-resin** | **Resin + Volatile oil + Gum** | Partly water-, partly alcohol-soluble → **emulsion** with water |
| **Balsam** | **Resin + Benzoic acid and/or Cinnamic acid** (or their esters) | ⭐ **MUST contain benzoic/cinnamic acid** |
| **Glycoresin (glucoresin)** | Resin + glycoside | Jalap, Podophyllum, Ipomoea |
| **Latex** | Milky emulsion in latex vessels | Coagulates on exposure; e.g. Opium, Papain, Rubber |

🧠 **MEMORY HOOK — the balsam rule:**
> **"NO BENZOIC OR CINNAMIC ACID → NOT A BALSAM."**
> Therefore **Copaiba balsam, Canada balsam and Gurjun balsam are NOT true balsams** — they are
> **oleoresins**, wrongly named "balsam". ⚠️ *This is the single most-asked trap of the chapter.*

⭐ **TRUE BALSAMS (only these four are true):**
**Tolu balsam, Peru balsam, Storax (Styrax), Benzoin.**
🧠 "**T**he **P**harmacist **S**ells **B**alsams" → **T**olu, **P**eru, **S**torax, **B**enzoin.

⚠️ **FALSE BALSAMS (oleoresins in disguise):** **Copaiba, Canada balsam, Gurjun balsam,
Balsam of Mecca (partly), Japanese balsam.**

---

## 2.2 GUMS & MUCILAGES

### 2.2.1 Definitions & general nature

📌 **GUM** — A gum is a **pathological product** formed by the **gummosis** (disintegration of
cellulose/ starch of cell walls) of plant tissue as a result of **injury, insect attack or
unfavourable conditions**. Chemically, gums are **salts (Ca, Mg, K) of complex polysaccharide
acids** (uronic acids + sugars).

📌 **MUCILAGE** — A mucilage is a **normal physiological product** of metabolism, formed **within
the cell** (in mucilage cells, canals or cavities) **without injury**; chemically it is also a
polysaccharide, and it forms slimy masses with water.

📌 **In pharmacy**, "mucilage" also means the *viscous aqueous solution prepared from a gum* —
e.g. **Mucilage of Acacia (Mucilago Acaciae) = 35 % w/v** of acacia in water.

🔁 **COMPARE — GUM vs MUCILAGE**

| Feature | GUM | MUCILAGE |
|---|---|---|
| Origin | **Pathological** (injury/disease) | **Physiological** (normal metabolism) |
| Site of formation | Exudes **outside** the plant | Formed & stored **inside** the cell |
| Water | Dissolves or swells → **adhesive/ sticky** | Forms **slimy** mass |
| Alcohol | Insoluble | Insoluble |
| Adhesiveness | **Strongly adhesive** | Weakly adhesive |
| Examples | Acacia, Tragacanth, Karaya, Ghatti | Isapghula, Linseed, Agar, Salep, Aloe leaf gel |

🔁 **COMPARE — GUM vs RESIN** (asked in almost every paper)

| Feature | GUM | RESIN |
|---|---|---|
| Chemistry | Carbohydrate (polysaccharide) | Non-carbohydrate; resin acids/alcohols/esters, terpenoid or phenolic |
| Nitrogen | Absent | Absent |
| Solubility in **water** | **Soluble / swells** | **Insoluble** |
| Solubility in **alcohol** | **Insoluble** | **Soluble** |
| Solubility in organic solvents (ether, chloroform, acetone) | Insoluble | Soluble |
| On heating | Chars, smells of burnt sugar (caramel) | **Softens & melts**, burns with a **luminous sooty flame** |
| Hydrolysis | Gives sugars & uronic acids | Does not give sugar (except glycoresins) |
| Use | Suspending, emulsifying, binding, demulcent | Antiseptic, purgative, plaster/varnish, adhesive |

### 2.2.2 Uses of gums & mucilages in pharmacy

⭐ **The 8 pharmaceutical uses (very frequently asked):**
1. **Suspending agent** (indiffusible powders in mixtures) — Tragacanth, Acacia, Sterculia.
2. **Emulsifying agent** — Acacia (the classic **primary emulsifier**), Tragacanth (thickener).
3. **Binder/ granulating agent** in tablets — Acacia (10–25 %), Starch mucilage (5–10 %),
   Tragacanth.
4. **Demulcent & soothing agent** (cough linctuses, throat pastilles) — Acacia, Tragacanth, Honey.
5. **Bulk laxative** — Isapghula, Sterculia, Agar, Guar gum, Linseed.
6. **Adhesive** — mucilage of acacia/ tragacanth for labels, pill excipient.
7. **Thickening/ stabilising agent** in creams, jellies, toothpaste.
8. **Gelling/ suspending in food, and colloid stabiliser**; also **plasma substitute**
   (dextran, acacia historically), **tablet coating**, and **culture medium base (Agar)**.

### 2.2.3 🧪 GENERAL IDENTIFICATION TESTS FOR GUMS

| Test | Procedure | Result |
|---|---|---|
| **Solubility** | Water vs alcohol | Soluble/swells in water, insoluble in alcohol (90 %) |
| **Ruthenium red** | Mount powder in ruthenium red | **Pink/red** → mucilage present |
| **Iodine** | Add iodine solution | **No blue** (differentiates from starch) |
| **Lead acetate** | Add to solution | Precipitate |
| **Molisch's test** | α-naphthol + conc. H₂SO₄ | Purple/violet ring (carbohydrate) |
| **Fehling's after hydrolysis** | Hydrolyse then Fehling's | Brick-red ppt (reducing sugars) |
| **Charring** | Heat strongly | Smell of **burnt sugar / caramel** |

⭐ **THE FOUR CLASSICAL DIFFERENTIATING TESTS (Acacia vs Tragacanth) — memorise verbatim**

| Test | ACACIA | TRAGACANTH |
|---|---|---|
| **Water** | **Completely & freely soluble** → clear, non-viscous, slightly acidic mucilage | **Insoluble but swells** → stiff, opalescent, highly viscous gel |
| **Ruthenium red** | Not stained/faint | **Stains pink** |
| **Iodine** (with the gum) | No colour | **Olive-green** with a small amount of starch present (tragacanth contains starch) |
| **Lead acetate (basic/ neutral)** | Precipitate with **lead acetate** | Precipitate with **basic lead acetate** |
| **Peroxidase / oxidase (H₂O₂ + benzidine)** | ⭐ **POSITIVE — deep blue** (acacia contains **oxidase/peroxidase enzyme**) | **NEGATIVE** |
| **FeCl₃** | No change | No change |
| **Viscosity** | Low | **Very high (most viscous of all gums)** |

🧠 **MEMORY HOOK** — "**A**cacia has the **enzym A**se (oxidase → blue with benzidine + H₂O₂);
**T**ragacanth is **T**hick and **T**urns ruthenium red pink."

⚠️ **TRAP** — Because acacia contains **oxidase (peroxidase) enzyme**, it **oxidises easily
oxidisable drugs** (e.g. adrenaline, apomorphine, thymol, phenol, tannins, alkaloid salts,
easily oxidised vitamins). To destroy the enzyme, **heat the mucilage at 100 °C for ~30 min**
(or use another emulgent).

### 2.2.4 📋 MASTER MONOGRAPH TABLE — GUMS & MUCILAGES

| Drug | Synonym | Biological source | Family | Chief constituents | Pharmaceutical use | Dose |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **ACACIA** | Gum arabic, Gum acacia, Babul gum, Indian gum | Dried gummy exudate from stem & branches of ***Acacia senegal*** (& other *Acacia* spp.; Indian *A. arabica/nilotica*) | **Leguminosae (Fabaceae/ Mimosaceae)** | **Arabin** = Ca, Mg, K salts of **arabic acid** (L-arabinose, L-rhamnose, D-galactose, D-glucuronic acid); **oxidase/peroxidase enzyme**; ~ 14 % moisture; ash NMT 4 % | **Emulsifying agent (primary emulgent), suspending agent, binder, demulcent, tablet binder, mucilage 35 % w/v** | Demulcent, 0.5–2 g (as required) |
| **TRAGACANTH** | Gum tragacanth, Goat's thorn gum, Shiraz/Persian gum | Dried gummy exudate from stem & branches of ***Astragalus gummifer*** & other *Astragalus* spp. | **Leguminosae** | **Bassorin (60–70 %, water-insoluble, swells)** + **Tragacanthin (30–40 %, water-soluble)**; starch; cellulose; traces of enzyme absent | **Suspending agent (best for indiffusible powders), thickener, emulsion stabiliser, binder; mucilage; base of jellies & pastes** | 0.5–2 g; Compound tragacanth powder 0.5–2 g |
| **STERCULIA** | **Karaya gum**, Indian tragacanth, Kadaya | Dried gummy exudate of ***Sterculia urens*** (& *S. villosa*) | **Sterculiaceae** | Acetylated polysaccharide of D-galactose, L-rhamnose, D-galacturonic acid; **~8 % acetyl groups (smell of acetic acid)** | **Bulk laxative**, suspending agent, denture fixative, thickener; **swells to 60–100× in water**, does not dissolve | Bulk laxative 5–10 g |
| **GUAR GUM** | Guaran, Jaguar gum | Endosperm of seed of ***Cyamopsis tetragonolobus*** | **Leguminosae** | **Galactomannan** (mannose : galactose = 2 : 1) | **Bulk laxative, thickener, binder, disintegrant, appetite suppressant; food stabiliser** | 5–10 g laxative |
| **GHATTI** | Indian gum, Gum ghati, Dhawa gum | Exudate of ***Anogeissus latifolia*** | **Combretaceae** | Arabinogalactan (L-arabinose, D-galactose, D-mannose, D-xylose, D-glucuronic acid) | **Substitute for acacia** as emulsifying/suspending agent | — |
| **AGAR** | Agar-agar, Japanese isinglass, Vegetable gelatin, Chinese gelatin | Dried mucilage (hydrophilic colloid) from ***Gelidium***, ***Gracilaria***, ***Pterocladia*** spp. (red algae — Rhodophyceae) | **Gelidiaceae / Rhodophyceae** | **Agarose + Agaropectin** (galactose sulphuric acid esters) | **Bulk laxative; suspending & emulsifying agent; culture medium for bacteria (solidifies at ~ 32–39 °C, melts ~ 85–100 °C); tablet disintegrant; suppository base component** | 4–16 g as bulk laxative |
| **ALGINATES / SODIUM ALGINATE** | Algin, Norgine | Cell wall of brown seaweeds ***Laminaria***, ***Macrocystis***, ***Ascophyllum*** (Phaeophyceae) | Laminariaceae etc. | **Alginic acid** = D-mannuronic + L-guluronic acid polymer | **Suspending/ thickening/ emulsifying agent; haemostatic (calcium alginate dressings); antacid–reflux formulations; tablet binder & disintegrant** | — |
| **ISAPGHULA** | **Ispaghula / Psyllium / Spogel seeds / Flea seed** | Dried ripe **seeds** of ***Plantago ovata*** (also *P. psyllium*, *P. indica*) | **Plantaginaceae** | **Mucilage (in epidermis of seed coat, ~30 %)** — xylose, arabinose, galacturonic acid; fixed oil; protein; aucubin glycoside | ⭐ **Bulk laxative** (also demulcent, in chronic dysentery & diarrhoea) | **Seeds: 4–16 g** (usually 5–10 g) with plenty of water; husk 3–6 g |
| **LINSEED** | Flax seed, Alsi | Dried ripe seeds of ***Linum usitatissimum*** | **Linaceae** | **Fixed oil 30–40 %** (linolenic, linoleic), mucilage 6 %, protein; **linamarin** (cyanogenetic glycoside) | **Demulcent, emollient, poultice (crushed linseed poultice), laxative; oil = drying oil, laxative & liniment ingredient** | Seed 4–16 g; oil 15–30 mL |
| **STARCH** | Amylum | Grains of ***Zea mays*** (maize), ***Oryza sativa*** (rice), ***Triticum aestivum*** (wheat), tuber of ***Solanum tuberosum*** (potato), rhizome of ***Manihot*** (tapioca) | Graminae / Solanaceae | **Amylose (soluble, ~20–30 %) + Amylopectin (insoluble, ~70–80 %)** | **Tablet diluent, binder (as mucilage/paste 5–10 %), disintegrant, dusting powder, antidote in iodine poisoning, base of glycerin of starch** | — |
| **HONEY** | Madhu, Purified honey, Mel | Saccharine secretion deposited in honeycomb by ***Apis mellifera*** & other spp. | **Apidae (animal origin)** | **Dextrose ~35 %, Levulose (fructose) ~40 %, sucrose, dextrin, wax, volatile oil, formic acid; enzymes (invertase, diastase)** | **Sweetening & demulcent agent, vehicle for linctuses, mild laxative, wound dressing; base of Honey of Borax** | — |
| **PECTIN** | — | Inner rind of citrus fruits, apple pomace | Rutaceae / Rosaceae | Partially methoxylated **polygalacturonic acid** | **Antidiarrhoeal (with kaolin), gelling & thickening agent, haemostatic, emulsifier** | — |
| **GELATIN** | — | Partial hydrolysis of **collagen** of skin, bone, connective tissue of animals | Animal | Protein (glycine, proline, hydroxyproline) — **Type A (acid-treated, pI 7–9)**, **Type B (alkali-treated, pI 4.7–5)** | **Capsule shells, suppository & pessary base (glycero-gelatin), plasma expander, tablet binder, absorbable haemostatic sponge, pastilles** | — |
| **TAMARIND / OTHERS** | — | *Tamarindus indica* seed | Leguminosae | Polysaccharide | Thickener, suspending agent | — |
| **DEXTRAN** | — | Fermentation of sucrose by ***Leuconostoc mesenteroides*** | Microbial | α-1,6-glucan | **Plasma volume expander** | — |
| **SALEP / MUSLI** | Orchid root mucilage | Dried tubers of *Orchis* spp. | Orchidaceae | Mucilage + starch | Demulcent, nutritive | — |

⭐ **HIGHEST-YIELD "SOURCE-FAMILY" LIST** (examiners love family names)
* Acacia → **Leguminosae**
* Tragacanth → **Leguminosae**
* Guar → **Leguminosae**
* Sterculia (Karaya) → **Sterculiaceae**
* Ghatti → **Combretaceae**
* Isapghula → **Plantaginaceae**
* Linseed → **Linaceae**
* Agar → **Gelidiaceae (Rhodophyceae — red algae)**
* Alginates → **Phaeophyceae (brown algae)**
* Honey → **Apidae (animal)**

🔢 **NUMBERS TO MEMORISE (gums)**
* **Mucilage of Acacia = 35 % w/v**; Mucilage of Tragacanth = **1.25 % w/v** (with alcohol +
  chloroform water); Mucilage of Starch = **~ 2 % (1 in 50)**.
* **Compound Powder of Tragacanth (Compound Tragacanth Powder)** = **Tragacanth 15 g, Acacia
  20 g, Starch 20 g, Sucrose (powdered) 45 g** → total 100 g. 🧠 "**15-20-20-45**".
* **Swelling index — Isapghula ≥ 40**; Sterculia swells 60–100 times.
* Agar gels at **~ 32–39 °C**, melts at **~ 85 °C**; gel forms at **1–2 %** concentration.
* Acacia's mucilage is **acidic (pH ~ 4.5–5.0)**; **specific rotation of acacia solution is
  laevorotatory (≈ –25° to –35°)**, whereas most gum adulterants are dextrorotatory.

⭐ **Emulsifying capacity ("Gum : Oil : Water" ratios) — the FOUR classic ratios**

| Type of oil | Oil : Water : Gum (Acacia) |
|---|---|
| **Fixed oil** (e.g. castor, arachis, olive, cod-liver) | **4 : 2 : 1** |
| **Mineral oil** (liquid paraffin) | **3 : 2 : 1** |
| **Volatile oil** (turpentine, peppermint, cinnamon) | **2 : 2 : 1** |
| **Oleo-resins** (e.g. balsam of Peru, male fern) | **1 : 2 : 1** |

🧠 **MEMORY HOOK — "4-3-2-1 for Fixed, Mineral, Volatile, Oleoresin."** (Water & gum stay 2 : 1.)
⭐ **Dry gum method = Continental method (4:2:1)**; **Wet gum method = English method**;
**Bottle/ Forbes bottle method** for volatile oils; **Nascent soap method** for oil + lime water
(*in situ* soap = calcium oleate, e.g. **Lime liniment/ Carron oil = olive oil + lime water 1:1**).

---

## 2.3 RESINS

### 2.3.1 Definition & general properties

📌 **DEFINITION** — **Resins** are **amorphous, solid or semi-solid, non-nitrogenous,
non-crystalline complex mixtures** of resin acids, resin alcohols (resinols), resino-tannols,
esters and inert resenes; they are **insoluble in water**, **soluble in alcohol, ether, chloroform
and other organic solvents**, and are formed as a result of **oxidation and polymerisation of
volatile oils / terpenes** in the plant. They soften and melt on heating and burn with a
**characteristic sooty, luminous flame**.

### 2.3.2 Classification of resins

**(A) By composition**

| Class | Composition | Examples |
|---|---|---|
| **Resins (true/ pure)** | Resin only | **Colophony (rosin), Mastic, Sandarac, Guaiacum, Cannabis (charas)** |
| **Oleoresins** | Resin + volatile oil | **Turpentine, Copaiba, Canada balsam, Ginger, Capsicum, Male fern, Aspidium, Cubeb** |
| **Oleo-gum-resins** | Resin + volatile oil + gum | **Asafoetida, Myrrh, Gamboge, Ammoniacum, Galbanum, Olibanum (frankincense)** |
| **Balsams** | Resin + benzoic/ cinnamic acid | **Tolu, Peru, Storax, Benzoin** |
| **Glycoresins (glucoresins)** | Resin + glycoside | **Jalap, Podophyllum, Ipomoea (Indian jalap)** |

**(B) By chemical nature of constituents**

| Type | Chief constituent | Examples |
|---|---|---|
| **Resin acids** | Free acids (–COOH) — soluble in alkali, form soaps | Colophony (**abietic acid**), Copaiba (copaivic acid), Myrrh (commiphoric acid), Sandarac (sandaracopimaric acid) |
| **Resin esters** | Esters of resin acids/alcohols with benzoic, cinnamic, salicylic acid | **Benzoin, Storax, Peru, Tolu, Turpentine** |
| **Resin alcohols (resinols)** | High-molecular alcohols, no tannin reaction | **Benzoresinol, storesinol, guaiaresinol, peruresinol** (Benzoin, Storax) |
| **Resinotannols** | Resin alcohols giving **tannin reactions** (FeCl₃) | **Siaresinotannol, sumaresinotannol (Benzoin), peruresinotannol, toluresinotannol, aloeresinotannol, myroxocarpin** |
| **Resenes** | **Chemically inert**, neutral, no acid/alcohol/ester groups | **Colophorene** (colophony), **olibanoresene**, **gurjuresene**, **copaivic resene**, **cannabene** |
| **Glycoresins** | Resin combined with sugar | Jalapin/ convolvulin (Jalap), Podophyllotoxin glycoside (Podophyllum) |

🧠 **MEMORY HOOK** — "**Resin acid** = sour; **Resinol** = alcohol; **Resinotannol** = alcohol +
tannin (gives colour with FeCl₃); **Resene** = "**rest** — does nothing" (inert)."

### 2.3.3 🧪 GENERAL TESTS FOR RESINS

| Test | Observation |
|---|---|
| Solubility | Insoluble in water; **soluble in alcohol**, ether, chloroform, acetone, benzene |
| Alkali | Resin acids **dissolve in NaOH/KOH** forming soap-like solution |
| Heating | Softens, melts, then burns with **luminous, sooty (smoky) flame** and aromatic fumes |
| Alcoholic solution + water | **Turbidity/ milkiness** (resin precipitates) |
| FeCl₃ | Green/blue-black if **resinotannol** present (Benzoin, Peru, Tolu) |
| Litmus | Alcoholic solution of resin acids is **acidic** |

### 2.3.4 📋 MASTER MONOGRAPH TABLE — RESINS, OLEORESINS, OLEO-GUM-RESINS

| Drug | Synonym | Biological source | Family | Chief constituents | Uses | Dose |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **COLOPHONY** | **Rosin, Rosin resin, Yellow resin, Amber resin, Pine resin** | Residue left after distilling off the volatile oil from the oleoresin of ***Pinus palustris***, *P. longifolia* & other *Pinus* spp. | **Pinaceae** | **Abietic acid (~ 90 % resin acids)**, colophoresene, pimaric acid, bitter substances | **Ingredient of adhesive plasters, ointments (e.g. resin/ Deshler's ointment), varnishes, dental cements; mild antiseptic & stimulant to skin; soldering flux; sizing** | External only |
| **TURPENTINE / TURPENTINE OIL** | Terebinthina; **Oil of turpentine = Turpentine oil, Spirit of turpentine** | Concrete oleoresin from *Pinus* spp.; **turpentine oil = volatile oil distilled from it** | **Pinaceae** | Oil: **α- & β-pinene, camphene, limonene, dipentene**; residue = colophony | **Counter-irritant, rubefacient, antirheumatic liniment; carminative; expectorant; solvent for waxes & resins; treatment of intestinal worms & parasites (classical)** | Oil (internal, obsolete) 0.3–1 mL; usually **external only** |
| **ASAFOETIDA** | **Hing**, Devil's dung, Food of the gods, Gum asafoetida | Oleo-gum-resin obtained by incision of living **rhizomes & roots** of ***Ferula asafoetida***, *F. foetida*, *F. narthex* | **Umbelliferae (Apiaceae)** | **Resin 40–65 % (asaresinotannol + ferulic acid esters), gum 25 %, volatile oil 3–17 % (contains organic sulphur compounds → alliaceous odour), free ferulic acid, umbelliferone** | **Carminative, antispasmodic, expectorant, nervine stimulant, anthelmintic; flavouring** | **0.3–1 g** (0.06–0.2 g of tincture per dose; Tincture 2–4 mL) |
| **MYRRH** | **Gum myrrh, Bola, Hirabol, Bol** | Oleo-gum-resin from stem of ***Commiphora molmol*** / *C. myrrha* (*Balsamodendron myrrha*) | **Burseraceae** | **Resin 25–40 % (commiphoric acid, commiphorinic acid, α-, β-, γ-commiphoric acid, heerabomyrrholic acid), gum 50–60 %, volatile oil 2–10 % (heerabolene, eugenol, cuminaldehyde)** | **Astringent, antiseptic, carminative, emmenagogue; mouthwash & gargle (tincture of myrrh), stimulant expectorant; incense** | Tincture 2–4 mL; myrrh 0.3–1 g |
| **GAMBOGE** | Gutta gamba | Gum resin from ***Garcinia hanburyi*** | Guttiferae | Gambogic acid, resin ~ 70 %, gum | **Drastic hydragogue purgative** (obsolete); pigment | 0.12–0.3 g |
| **OLIBANUM** | **Frankincense, Salai guggul (Indian olibanum), Luban** | Oleo-gum-resin of ***Boswellia serrata*** / *B. carteri* | **Burseraceae** | Resin (boswellic acid), gum, volatile oil (olibanol); **olibanoresene** | Anti-inflammatory (boswellic acid), antiseptic, incense, plasters | — |
| **GUGGUL** | Indian bdellium, Gum guggul | Oleo-gum-resin of ***Commiphora wightii/mukul*** | **Burseraceae** | **Guggulsterones E & Z**, resin, gum, volatile oil | **Hypolipidaemic (lowers cholesterol), anti-inflammatory, antiarthritic** | 2–4 g |
| **AMMONIACUM** | Gum ammoniac | *Dorema ammoniacum* | Umbelliferae | Resin, gum, volatile oil | Expectorant, stimulant, plaster | 0.3–1 g |
| **GALBANUM** | — | *Ferula galbaniflua* | Umbelliferae | Resin, gum, volatile oil (umbelliferone) | Expectorant, antispasmodic, plaster | — |
| **GINGER (Oleoresin)** | Zingiber, Adrak/Sonth | **Rhizome** of ***Zingiber officinale*** | **Zingiberaceae** | **Volatile oil 1–3 % (zingiberene, zingiberol), pungent principle = gingerol & shogaol; resin; starch** | **Carminative, aromatic stimulant, flavouring, antiemetic (motion sickness), sialagogue, counter-irritant** | **0.5–2 g** (Tincture of ginger 0.5–2 mL; strong tincture 0.25–0.5 mL) |
| **CAPSICUM (Oleoresin)** | **Chillies, Cayenne pepper, Lal mirch** | Dried ripe **fruits** of ***Capsicum annuum*** / ***C. frutescens*** (C. minimum) | **Solanaceae** | **Capsaicin (0.1–1 %) — pungent principle; capsanthin (colour); volatile oil; fat; vitamin C** | **Counter-irritant, rubefacient (plasters, liniments), carminative, stomachic, appetite stimulant; local analgesic (capsaicin cream)** | **30–120 mg** (Tincture 0.06–0.2 mL) |
| **MALE FERN** | Aspidium, Filix mas, Rhizoma filicis | Rhizome & stipe bases of ***Dryopteris filix-mas*** | **Polypodiaceae** | **Filicin (≥ 1.5 %), filicic acid, flavaspidic acid, aspidinol, albaspidin; fixed oil** | ⭐ **Anthelmintic — specific for TAPEWORM (taenicide)**; given with a saline purgative but **NOT with castor oil/ fat/ alcohol** (increases absorption & toxicity) | Extract 3–6 g (max 8 g); ⚠️ Toxic — optic neuritis/blindness |
| **JALAP** | Jalapa, Vera Cruz jalap | Dried tubercles of ***Ipomoea purga*** (*Exogonium purga*) | **Convolvulaceae** | **Glycoresins — Jalapin (β-, ether-soluble) & Convolvulin (α-, ether-insoluble)**, starch, sugar | **Drastic hydragogue cathartic** | Powder 0.3–1 g; Jalap resin 0.1–0.3 g |
| **PODOPHYLLUM** | **May apple, American mandrake; Indian podophyllum (P. hexandrum/ emodi) = Bankakri** | Dried rhizome & roots of ***Podophyllum peltatum*** (American) / ***P. hexandrum*** (Indian) | **Berberidaceae** | **Podophyllotoxin, α- & β-peltatin, picropodophyllin, quercetin; resin (podophyllum resin/ podophyllin)** | **Cathartic (drastic purgative), cholagogue; ⭐ topical for condyloma/ venereal & plantar warts; podophyllotoxin = parent of anticancer etoposide & teniposide** | Resin 15–60 mg; **paint 15–25 % in compound benzoin tincture** |
| **CANNABIS** | **Indian hemp, Bhang (leaves), Ganja (flowering tops), Charas (resin)** | Dried flowering tops of female plants of ***Cannabis sativa*** | **Cannabinaceae (Moraceae)** | **Δ⁹-Tetrahydrocannabinol (THC — active), cannabidiol, cannabinol, cannabene; resin** | Narcotic, sedative, hallucinogen, analgesic; antiemetic in cancer chemotherapy; ⚠️ **Narcotic — Schedule X/NDPS control** | — (controlled) |
| **GUAIACUM** | Guaiac resin, Lignum vitae | Heartwood of *Guaiacum officinale* | Zygophyllaceae | Guaiaconic acid, guaiaretic acid, guaiacic acid, vanillin | Antirheumatic, reagent for **occult blood (guaiac test)** | — |
| **MASTIC** | Mastich | *Pistacia lentiscus* | Anacardiaceae | Mastichoresene, masticinic acid | Dental cement, varnish, chewing | — |
| **BENZOIN** | **Gum benjamin, Loban** (see Balsams) | *Styrax benzoin* (Sumatra) / *S. tonkinensis* (Siam) | **Styracaceae** | See balsam table | Antiseptic, expectorant, inhalation | — |
| **SHELLAC** | Lac, Lacca | Resinous secretion of the **insect *Laccifer lacca*** on trees | Animal (Coccidae) | Aleuritic acid, shellolic acid, lac dye (laccaic acid), wax | **Enteric tablet coating**, varnish, sealing wax, dental plates | — |
| **CUBEB** | Cubeba, Kabab chini | Fruits of *Piper cubeba* | Piperaceae | Cubebin, volatile oil, resin | Urinary antiseptic, carminative, expectorant | 1–4 g |
| **BALSAM OF COPAIBA** | Copaiba (⚠️ **oleoresin, not a true balsam**) | Oleoresin of *Copaifera langsdorfii* | **Leguminosae** | Volatile oil 40–60 %, copaivic acid resin | Urinary antiseptic, diuretic, expectorant | 1–2 mL |
| **CANADA BALSAM** | Canada turpentine (⚠️ **oleoresin, not a true balsam**) | Oleoresin from *Abies balsamea* | **Pinaceae** | Volatile oil + resin acids | ⭐ **Microscopy mountant (RI ≈ 1.52–1.54)**, cement for lenses | — |

⭐ **ANTHELMINTIC & PURGATIVE RESINS — "the drastic four":** **Jalap, Podophyllum, Gamboge,
Colocynth (fruit, not resin)**; anthelmintic resin = **Male fern (tapeworm)**.

⚠️ **TRAP — Male fern must never be given with castor oil, alcohol or fatty food.** The saline
purgative (magnesium sulphate) is given instead.
