  the drug must be **released** from the base (an "affinity" that is too high retards release).
* ⭐ **Hydration of the skin** — a **hydrated stratum corneum is far more permeable**
  → **occlusive dressings & greasy bases increase absorption several-fold**.
* ⭐ **Condition & site of the skin** — **broken/ abraded/ inflamed skin absorbs much more**;
  absorption is greatest where the skin is **thin** (scrotum, face, eyelid, post-auricular) and
  least on the **palms & soles**; ⚠️ **infants' skin absorbs more (greater surface-area : weight
  ratio)**.
* **Rubbing (inunction)** and the **duration of contact & surface area**.
* **Temperature & blood flow**; **penetration enhancers** (DMSO, propylene glycol, urea, azone,
  surfactants).
* **Follicular route** — via hair follicles & sweat ducts (a minor pathway).

---

## 10.3 ⭐⭐ OINTMENT BASES

📌 **DEFINITION** — An **ointment base** is the **vehicle** in which the medicament is incorporated;
it determines the **consistency, spreadability, drug release, occlusiveness & washability** of the
product.

### ⭐ IDEAL PROPERTIES OF AN OINTMENT BASE
1. **Inert, non-irritant, non-sensitising, non-toxic.**
2. **Compatible** with the medicament and with the skin's pH.
3. Should **release the drug readily** at the site of application.
4. **Smooth, unctuous, easily applied & easily removed.**
5. **Physically & chemically stable** (no rancidity, no separation, no phase inversion).
6. **Good consistency** — neither too stiff nor too runny, over the range of climate temperatures.
7. Should be **able to absorb water/ aqueous secretions** where required.
8. Should **not stain** clothing; should be **cosmetically acceptable & odourless**.
9. Should be **capable of being sterilised** where necessary (eye ointments).

### 📋 ⭐⭐ THE FOUR CLASSES OF OINTMENT BASE (USP/ IP classification)

```
                     OINTMENT BASES
                            │
   ┌─────────────┬──────────┴───────┬──────────────────┐
① OLEAGINOUS   ② ABSORPTION      ③ EMULSIFYING /     ④ WATER-SOLUBLE
  (Hydrocarbon)   BASES             WATER-REMOVABLE      (Greaseless)
  "Greasy"        (Absorb water)    (o/w emulsion)       • PEG /
  • Soft paraffin • Wool fat        • Emulsifying          Macrogol
  • Hard paraffin • Wool alcohols     ointment             ointment
  • Liquid        • Beeswax +       • Cetrimide/         • Tragacanth,
    paraffin        paraffin          Cetomacrogol         starch &
  • Simple        • Hydrophilic       emulsifying wax      cellulose
    Ointment        petrolatum      • Aqueous Cream         jellies
  • Paraffin      • Wool fat +
    ointment        soft paraffin
```

### 📋 THE MASTER BASE TABLE ⭐⭐

| Class | Composition & examples | ⭐ Properties | ⭐ Uses / notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| ⭐ **① OLEAGINOUS / HYDROCARBON (greasy) bases** | ⭐ **White & Yellow SOFT PARAFFIN (petrolatum/ vaseline)**, **Hard paraffin**, **Liquid paraffin**, **Paraffin Ointment**, ⭐ **SIMPLE OINTMENT BP**, vegetable oils, **Cerates**, **Plastibase** | ⭐ **Anhydrous, GREASY, highly OCCLUSIVE, EMOLLIENT; NOT water-washable; absorb only a small amount of water; chemically very stable (no rancidity, no hydrolysis)** | ⭐ **Best EMOLLIENT & OCCLUSIVE base — hydrates the skin and gives the GREATEST percutaneous absorption**; protective; vehicle for **water-sensitive drugs** (e.g. antibiotics, aspirin). ⚠️ Greasy, stains clothes, difficult to remove, **cannot absorb aqueous secretions** → not for exuding lesions |
| ⭐ **② ABSORPTION (emulsifiable/ "absorption") bases** | (a) **Anhydrous** — ⭐ **Wool fat (anhydrous lanolin), Wool alcohols Ointment, Hydrophilic Petrolatum, Simple Ointment BP (contains wool fat), beeswax + paraffin**; (b) **already containing water (w/o)** — ⭐ **Hydrous wool fat (Lanolin), Oily Cream, Cold Cream, Zinc Cream, Rose water ointment** | ⭐ **Anhydrous but can ABSORB LARGE AMOUNTS OF WATER/ aqueous solutions to form a w/o emulsion**; greasy but less than hydrocarbon; **not easily washed off**; ⭐ **emollient + occlusive** | ⭐ **Used when an AQUEOUS SOLUTION/ liquid must be incorporated into a greasy base**; good emollient for **dry, scaly skin**; ⚠️ **wool fat may cause CONTACT SENSITISATION (allergy)** and can become **rancid** |
| ⭐ **③ EMULSIFYING / WATER-REMOVABLE (o/w) bases** | ⭐ **EMULSIFYING OINTMENT BP** = ⭐ **Emulsifying wax 30 %, White soft paraffin 50 %, Liquid paraffin 20 %**; ⭐ **AQUEOUS CREAM BP** = ⭐ **Emulsifying ointment 30 %, Water 70 % (+ preservative)**; **Cetomacrogol emulsifying ointment (non-ionic), Cetrimide emulsifying ointment (cationic), Hydrophilic ointment USP** | ⭐ **o/w EMULSION → WATER-WASHABLE ("vanishing"), NON-greasy or less greasy, easily spread & removed, can be DILUTED with water, cosmetically elegant; allow exudate to escape** | ⭐ **The most widely used modern base**; for **hairy areas & large surfaces**; ⚠️ needs a **PRESERVATIVE** (microbial growth in the aqueous phase); ⚠️ ⭐ **less occlusive → less percutaneous absorption than a greasy base**; may be **incompatible** with drugs that react with the surfactant |
| ⭐ **④ WATER-SOLUBLE (greaseless) bases** | ⭐ **MACROGOL (PEG) OINTMENT** = ⭐ **PEG 4000 (or 3350) 40 % + PEG 400 60 %**; **Polyethylene glycol ointment USP**; **jellies of tragacanth, sodium alginate, methylcellulose, carbomer, starch–glycerin** | ⭐ **Completely WATER-SOLUBLE & washable, NON-greasy, non-staining, do not stain clothes, anhydrous or aqueous** | ⭐ Ideal where a **greaseless, easily removed** product is wanted; **good for hairy areas, lubricant jellies, and as a vehicle for water-soluble drugs**; ⚠️ **HYGROSCOPIC & dehydrating → may sting**; ⚠️ ⭐ **INCOMPATIBLE with phenols, benzocaine, aspirin, sulphonamides, silver salts, iodine, tannic acid, and with plastics**; poor emollient; limited water-absorbing capacity |

🔁 **⭐ SIDE-BY-SIDE SUMMARY OF THE FOUR BASES**

| Property | ① Oleaginous | ② Absorption | ③ Emulsifying (o/w) | ④ Water-soluble |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Water content | None | None (or w/o) | ⭐ **High** | Variable |
| Water-washable | ❌ | ❌ | ⭐ ✔ | ⭐ ✔ |
| Greasy | ⭐ **Most** | Yes | Slight | ⭐ **None** |
| Occlusive/ emollient | ⭐ **Best** | Good | Poor | ⭐ **Poorest** |
| Absorbs aqueous liquid | Very little | ⭐ **Large amount** | Yes | Small |
| Percutaneous absorption | ⭐ **Highest** | High | Moderate | Low |
| Needs a preservative | ❌ | Only if hydrous | ⭐ ✔ | ✔ if aqueous |
| Stability | ⭐ **Best** | May become rancid | Good | Good |

### ⭐⭐ OFFICIAL BASE FORMULAE — MEMORISE THESE NUMBERS 🔢

| Base | 🔢 Formula |
|---|---|
| ⭐ **SIMPLE OINTMENT BP** | ⭐ **Wool fat 5 g · Hard paraffin 5 g · Cetostearyl alcohol 5 g · White (or yellow) soft paraffin 85 g** → 100 g. 🧠 **"5-5-5-85"** |
| ⭐ **EMULSIFYING OINTMENT BP** | ⭐ **Emulsifying wax 30 g · White soft paraffin 50 g · Liquid paraffin 20 g** → 100 g. 🧠 **"30-50-20"** |
| ⭐ **EMULSIFYING WAX BP** | ⭐ **Cetostearyl alcohol 90 g · Sodium lauryl sulphate 10 g** (+ water) 🧠 **"90-10"** |
| ⭐ **AQUEOUS CREAM BP** | ⭐ **Emulsifying ointment 30 g · Phenoxyethanol 1 g (preservative) · Freshly boiled & cooled purified water to 100 g** 🧠 **"30 in 100"** |
| ⭐ **OILY CREAM BP** | ⭐ **Wool alcohols ointment 50 g · Freshly boiled & cooled purified water 50 g** 🧠 **"50-50"** |
| ⭐ **WOOL ALCOHOLS OINTMENT BP** | ⭐ **Wool alcohols 6 g · Hard paraffin 24 g · White soft paraffin 10 g · Liquid paraffin 60 g** 🧠 **"6-24-10-60"** |
| ⭐ **HYDROUS OINTMENT / COLD CREAM (Rose Water Ointment)** | Beeswax, almond oil (or liquid paraffin), borax, rose water — a **w/o emulsion** ("cold" because water evaporates & cools) |
| ⭐ **PARAFFIN OINTMENT BP** | **Hard paraffin 30 g · Wool fat 30 g (or cetostearyl alcohol) · White soft paraffin 940 g** (per 1000 g) — a **very greasy, protective** base |
| ⭐ **MACROGOL (PEG) OINTMENT** | ⭐ **PEG 4000 (or 3350) 40 g · PEG 400 60 g** 🧠 **"40-60"** |
| ⭐ **SIMPLE EYE OINTMENT BASE BP** | ⭐ **Liquid paraffin 10 g · Wool fat 10 g · Yellow soft paraffin 80 g** — ⭐ **must be STERILISED (dry heat 160 °C) and filtered while molten** 🧠 **"10-10-80"** |
| ⭐ **HYDROPHILIC PETROLATUM USP** | Cholesterol 3 g · Stearyl alcohol 3 g · White wax 8 g · White petrolatum 86 g |
| ⭐ **HYDROPHILIC OINTMENT USP** | Sodium lauryl sulphate 10 g, propylene glycol 120 g, stearyl alcohol 250 g, white petrolatum 250 g, methyl & propyl paraben, water to 1000 g (an **o/w** base) |
| **ZINC CREAM BP** | Zinc oxide 32 %, in a w/o base (arachis oil, wool fat, calcium hydroxide, oleic acid, purified water) |
| **CETOMACROGOL EMULSIFYING WAX** | Cetostearyl alcohol 80 g · Cetomacrogol 1000 20 g (**non-ionic**) |
| **CETRIMIDE EMULSIFYING WAX** | Cetostearyl alcohol 90 g · Cetrimide 10 g (**cationic**) |

⭐ **THE THREE TYPES OF EMULSIFYING WAX — a favourite question**

| Emulsifying wax | Surfactant | Charge | Compatible with |
|---|---|---|---|
| ⭐ **Emulsifying wax BP** | **Sodium lauryl sulphate** | ⭐ **ANIONIC** | Anionic & non-ionic drugs; ⚠️ **incompatible with CATIONIC drugs** (cetrimide, chlorhexidine, benzalkonium) |
| ⭐ **Cetrimide emulsifying wax** | **Cetrimide** | ⭐ **CATIONIC** | Cationic drugs; ⚠️ **incompatible with ANIONIC drugs** (soaps, SLS, sulphonamide sodium) |
| ⭐ **Cetomacrogol emulsifying wax** | **Cetomacrogol 1000** | ⭐ **NON-IONIC** | ⭐ **Compatible with BOTH anionic & cationic drugs — the safest choice** |

🧠 **MEMORY HOOK** — "**S**odium lauryl sulphate = **S**ubtract (anion, −) · **Cetrimide** = **C**ation
(+) · **Cetomacrogol** = **C**ompatible with **C**ompletely everything (non-ionic)."

---

## 10.4 ⭐⭐ METHODS OF PREPARING OINTMENTS

📌 **THREE (four) METHODS** — the standard examination answer:

| Method | When used | Principle |
|---|---|---|
| ⭐ **1. FUSION (melting) method** | ⭐ When the base contains **several solid/ waxy ingredients of different melting points** (hard paraffin, beeswax, wool fat, cetostearyl alcohol, emulsifying wax, resins) | **MELT the ingredients together and stir until cold** |
| ⭐ **2. TRITURATION (mechanical incorporation/ levigation) method** | ⭐ When **insoluble powders** are to be incorporated into a **ready-made soft base** | **LEVIGATE the powder with a small quantity of the base (or a levigating liquid) and gradually incorporate the rest** |
| ⭐ **3. EMULSIFICATION method** | When the product is a **cream (an emulsion)** — an aqueous phase must be combined with an oily phase | **Heat both phases separately to the same temperature (~ 60–70 °C), add the internal phase to the external phase with continuous stirring, and stir until cold** |
| **4. Chemical reaction/ *in situ* method** | When the active substance is formed during preparation | e.g. **iodine ointment**; nascent soap formation |

### ⭐⭐ (1) THE FUSION METHOD — step by step

```
 ① Weigh all the ingredients
 ② MELT them in a porcelain dish/ water bath ⭐ in DESCENDING ORDER of melting point —
   i.e. the ingredient with the HIGHEST melting point FIRST (hard paraffin → beeswax →
   cetostearyl alcohol/ emulsifying wax → wool fat → soft paraffin → liquid paraffin)
 ③ Use the MINIMUM heat and the LOWEST temperature that will melt everything
 ④ Add any heat-labile or VOLATILE ingredient LAST, when the melt has cooled
   almost to setting point
 ⑤ ⭐ STIR GENTLY & CONTINUOUSLY UNTIL THE MASS IS COLD AND SET
 ⑥ If a gritty/ granular product results, ⭐ pass it through an OINTMENT MILL
   (or re-melt & re-stir)
 ⑦ Pack in a jar/ collapsible tube; label
```

⭐ **THE THREE "WHY" QUESTIONS OF THE FUSION METHOD**

| Question | ⭐ Answer |
|---|---|
| **Why melt in descending order of melting point?** | ⭐ The **highest-melting substance is melted first**, and the heat of that melt then melts the lower-melting ones — this **avoids overheating** the low-melting & heat-labile ingredients and **minimises the total heat applied** |
| **Why stir continuously until cold?** | ⭐ To keep the ingredients **uniformly mixed and prevent the high-melting waxes from SEPARATING/ CRYSTALLISING OUT in lumps as the mass cools**, and to produce a **smooth, homogeneous, non-granular** product |
| **Why not overheat?** | Overheating **volatilises** components, **degrades** heat-labile drugs, and causes **coarse crystals/ grittiness** on cooling |

⚠️ **Never stir so vigorously that air is beaten in** (an aerated ointment looks white & spongy and
oxidises).

### ⭐⭐ (2) THE TRITURATION / LEVIGATION METHOD — step by step

```
 ① Reduce the powder to a VERY FINE, IMPALPABLE powder (⭐ pass a no. 120–180 sieve);
   grittiness irritates the skin
 ② Place the powder on an OINTMENT SLAB/ TILE (glass or porcelain)
 ③ ⭐ LEVIGATE it with a SMALL QUANTITY of the base — or with a few drops of a
   LEVIGATING AGENT — using a STAINLESS-STEEL SPATULA, with firm shearing strokes,
   until a SMOOTH, GRIT-FREE PASTE is obtained
 ④ ⭐ Add the REST OF THE BASE GRADUALLY, in portions, by GEOMETRIC DILUTION
   (doubling up), working the mass with the spatula each time
 ⑤ Work with a "figure-of-eight"/ shearing motion, scraping the slab clean with the
   spatula, until uniform
 ⑥ Pack & label
```

⭐ **LEVIGATING AGENTS — a high-yield list**

| Levigating agent | Used with which base |
|---|---|
| ⭐ **Liquid paraffin (mineral oil)** | ⭐ **Oleaginous/ greasy bases** (soft paraffin, simple ointment) |
| ⭐ **Glycerin / Propylene glycol / Water** | ⭐ **Water-soluble & o/w (emulsifying) bases** |
| **A portion of the melted base itself** | Any base (the commonest & safest choice) |
| **Castor oil, olive oil, arachis oil** | Oleaginous bases |
| **Alcohol** (a few drops) | To dissolve/ wet camphor, menthol, iodine, resins |

🧠 **RULE:** ⭐ **The levigating agent must be MISCIBLE with the base** (and must not dissolve the
drug if a suspension is wanted).

⭐ **Apparatus:** **ointment slab/ tile (glass or porcelain)**, **stainless-steel spatula**
(⚠️ **use a HORN, PLASTIC or RUBBER spatula for iodine, tannic acid, salicylic acid, mercurials,
silver salts** — they react with metal), **mortar & pestle** (for small quantities/ liquids),
⭐ **OINTMENT MILL (triple roller mill)** for large-scale smoothing, **planetary/ sigma-blade mixer**
for bulk manufacture.

### ⭐ (3) THE EMULSIFICATION METHOD (for creams) — step by step

```
 ① Separate the ingredients into the OILY phase (waxes, paraffins, emulsifying wax,
   oil-soluble drugs & preservatives) and the AQUEOUS phase (water, water-soluble
   drugs, preservatives, humectants)
 ② ⭐ Heat BOTH phases SEPARATELY to the SAME temperature, about 60–70 °C
   (⭐ usually 5 °C above the melting point of the highest-melting component)
 ③ ⭐ Add the INTERNAL (dispersed) phase to the EXTERNAL (continuous) phase
   SLOWLY, with CONTINUOUS STIRRING
   • For an o/w cream → add the OIL phase to the WATER phase
   • For a w/o cream → add the WATER phase to the OIL phase
 ④ ⭐ STIR CONTINUOUSLY UNTIL COLD (or use a homogeniser/ Silverson mixer)
 ⑤ Add volatile & heat-labile ingredients (perfume, volatile oils) at ~ 40 °C
 ⑥ Make up any water lost by evaporation; homogenise; pack & label
```

⚠️ **Why must both phases be at the same temperature?** ⭐ If one phase is cooler, the **waxes
solidify prematurely at the interface**, giving a **lumpy, granular** cream and a **broken/ unstable
emulsion**.

---


## 10.5 ⭐⭐ INCORPORATING SPECIAL MEDICAMENTS INTO OINTMENTS
*(the exact counterpart of the suppository "special medicines" table — expect a question)*

| Nature of the medicament | ⭐ HOW TO INCORPORATE IT | Examples |
|---|---|---|
| ⭐ **Insoluble solid** | ⭐ Reduce to an **impalpable powder (no. 120–180 sieve)** → **LEVIGATE** with a little base or a levigating agent on a slab → incorporate the rest by **geometric dilution** | **Zinc oxide, calamine, sulphur, starch, boric acid, kaolin, titanium dioxide, hydrocortisone** |
| ⭐ **Solid soluble in the base (fat-soluble)** | ⭐ **Dissolve it in the melted base** (fusion), or triturate with a little **warmed** base | **Camphor, menthol, thymol, phenol, chloral hydrate, benzoic acid, salicylic acid (partly), volatile oils, coal tar** |
| ⭐ **Solid soluble in water** | ⭐ **Dissolve in the MINIMUM amount of water** and incorporate the solution: (a) into an **ABSORPTION base (wool fat)** which absorbs it, or (b) into the **aqueous phase of a cream**; ⚠️ **never simply add water to a hydrocarbon base** (it will not be absorbed and will "bleed") | **Sodium salts, potassium iodide, silver nitrate, zinc sulphate, morphine salts, ephedrine HCl** |
| ⭐ **Eutectic substances (liquefy on mixing)** | ⭐ **Mix them together FIRST and let the eutectic form deliberately**, then incorporate the liquid into the base; ⚠️ or **absorb** it (with a hardening wax/ absorbent) | ⭐ **Camphor + menthol · menthol + thymol · phenol + camphor** |
| ⭐ **Volatile substances** | ⭐ **Add LAST, at the LOWEST possible temperature** (below ~ 40 °C), and keep the container **covered**; then pack promptly in a **well-closed** container | **Volatile oils, camphor, menthol, chloroform, methyl salicylate** |
| ⭐ **Liquids (small volume)** | Stir directly into the base; a **greasy base holds only a small amount** — use an **absorption base (wool fat 10 %)** or **increase the wool fat/ emulsifying content** for larger volumes | **Ichthammol, coal tar solution, castor oil, balsam of Peru, tinctures (evaporate the alcohol first)** |
| ⭐ **Extracts & tinctures** | ⭐ **Evaporate the alcohol/ water on a water bath to a soft-extract consistency FIRST**, then incorporate — otherwise the base will not hold it | Belladonna extract, hamamelis |
| ⭐ **Substances that soften the base (lower its consistency)** | ⭐ **Add a HARDENING agent — hard paraffin, beeswax, white wax or cetostearyl alcohol** | **Phenol, chloral hydrate, camphor, coal tar, volatile oils, salicylic acid** |
| ⭐ **Substances that stain/ react with METAL** | ⭐ Use a ⭐ **HORN, PLASTIC, RUBBER or PORCELAIN spatula** and a **glass/ porcelain slab** | ⭐ **Iodine, tannic acid, salicylic acid, mercury salts (ammoniated mercury), silver nitrate, phenol, resorcinol, potassium permanganate** |
| ⭐ **Oxidising/ easily oxidisable substances** | Add an **antioxidant**; avoid metal contact & excessive heat; pack in a **collapsible tube** (excludes air) | Coal tar, resorcinol, hydroquinone |
| ⭐ **Thermolabile drugs** | ⭐ **Never use the fusion method for the drug itself** — prepare the base by fusion, cool it, then incorporate the drug by **trituration** | **Antibiotics, corticosteroids, enzymes, vitamins** |
| ⭐ **Potent drugs of very small dose** | Use a **trituration (1 in 10 with the base)** or the **aliquot method** | Corticosteroids, alkaloids |
| ⭐ **Gritty/ coarse products** | ⭐ Pass through an **OINTMENT MILL (triple roller mill)** or re-levigate | Any |

🧠 **THE FOUR-WAY RULE (same logic as suppositories):**
> **Soluble in the base → DISSOLVE. · Insoluble → LEVIGATE. · Water-soluble → minimum water into an
> ABSORPTION base or the aqueous phase. · Volatile/ heat-labile → LAST, COOL.**

---

## 10.6 ⭐ OFFICIAL / CLASSICAL OINTMENT & CREAM FORMULAE

| Preparation | 🔢 Formula / strength | ⭐ Use |
|---|---|---|
| ⭐ **Simple Ointment BP** | Wool fat 5, Hard paraffin 5, Cetostearyl alcohol 5, White/ Yellow soft paraffin 85 | Base; emollient |
| ⭐ **Emulsifying Ointment BP** | Emulsifying wax 30, White soft paraffin 50, Liquid paraffin 20 | ⭐ **o/w base; soap substitute for eczema** |
| ⭐ **Aqueous Cream BP** | Emulsifying ointment 30, Phenoxyethanol 1, Water to 100 | ⭐ **Emollient & soap substitute** |
| ⭐ **Oily Cream BP** | Wool alcohols ointment 50, Water 50 | Emollient (w/o) |
| ⭐ **Zinc Ointment BP** | ⭐ **Zinc oxide 15 %** in Simple Ointment | Protective, astringent, mild antiseptic |
| ⭐ **Zinc Oxide & Salicylic Acid Ointment ("Whitfield's Ointment")** | ⭐ **Benzoic acid 6 % + Salicylic acid 3 %** in Emulsifying ointment | ⭐ **ANTIFUNGAL — ringworm/ tinea** (benzoic acid = fungistatic, salicylic acid = keratolytic) 🧠 **"6 & 3"** |
| ⭐ **Compound Benzoic Acid Ointment** | = **Whitfield's** (Benzoic acid 6 %, Salicylic acid 3 %) | Antifungal |
| ⭐ **Salicylic Acid Ointment** | **2 %, 5 %, 10 %** | ⭐ **Keratolytic** — psoriasis, corns, warts, dandruff |
| ⭐ **Sulphur Ointment BP** | ⭐ **Sublimed/ precipitated sulphur 10 %** in Simple Ointment | ⭐ **SCABIES (scabicide)**, acne, seborrhoea |
| ⭐ **Coal Tar Ointment / Paste** | Coal tar **1–5 %** (Coal Tar & Salicylic Acid Ointment) | **Psoriasis, chronic eczema** |
| ⭐ **Zinc & Coal Tar Paste** | Coal tar, zinc oxide, starch, soft paraffin, emulsifying wax | Psoriasis |
| ⭐ **Compound Zinc Paste / Zinc & Salicylic Acid Paste (LASSAR'S PASTE)** | ⭐ **Zinc oxide 24 %, Salicylic acid 2 %, Starch 24 %, White soft paraffin 50 %** | ⭐ **Protective & keratolytic paste for chronic eczema & psoriasis** 🧠 **"24-2-24-50"** |
| ⭐ **Zinc Compound Paste BP** | Zinc oxide 25 %, Starch 25 %, White soft paraffin 50 % 🧠 **"25-25-50"** | Protective barrier |
| ⭐ **Magnesium Sulphate Paste (Morison's paste)** | Dried magnesium sulphate 45 %, Phenol 0.5 %, Glycerin to 100 % | ⭐ **Boils, carbuncles — osmotic "drawing" action** |
| **Calamine Ointment/ Cream** | Calamine 15 %, Zinc oxide 5 % | Soothing, antipruritic |
| **Ammoniated Mercury Ointment** | Ammoniated mercury 2.5 % | Antiseptic, psoriasis (obsolete) |
| **Boric Acid Ointment** | Boric acid 10 % | Mild antiseptic (⚠️ toxic in infants) |
| **Iodine Ointment** | Iodine + potassium iodide | Antiseptic, counter-irritant |
| **Methyl Salicylate Ointment / Liniment** | Methyl salicylate 25–50 % | Counter-irritant, analgesic |
| **Benzyl Benzoate Application** | Benzyl benzoate **25 %** emulsion | ⭐ **Scabies** |
| **Dithranol Ointment** | 0.1–2 % | Psoriasis |
| **Hydrocortisone Cream/ Ointment** | 0.5–2.5 % | ⭐ **Anti-inflammatory (eczema, dermatitis)** |
| **Betamethasone/ Clobetasol Cream** | 0.025–0.05 % | Potent corticosteroid |
| **Clotrimazole/ Miconazole Cream** | 1–2 % | Antifungal |
| **Neomycin/ Framycetin/ Bacitracin/ Mupirocin Ointment** | 0.5–2 % | Antibacterial |
| **Silver Sulphadiazine Cream** | 1 % | ⭐ **Burns** |
| **Lignocaine (Lidocaine) Gel/ Ointment** | 2 %, 5 % | Local anaesthetic, lubricant |
| ⭐ **Simple Eye Ointment BP** | ⭐ **Liquid paraffin 10, Wool fat 10, Yellow soft paraffin 80 — STERILISED** | Eye ointment base |
| **Chloramphenicol Eye Ointment** | 1 % in sterile base | Eye infection |
| ⭐ **Glycerin of Starch (Starch Glycerite)** | ⭐ **Starch 10 %, Purified water 20 %, Glycerin 70 %** — heated to 140 °C till translucent | Emollient, demulcent base |
| **Zinc Gelatin (Unna's boot)** | Zinc oxide 10 %, Gelatin 15 %, Glycerin 40 %, Water 35 % | ⭐ **Varicose ulcers** |
| **K-Y / Lubricant Jelly** | Tragacanth/ methylcellulose/ carbomer + glycerin + water + preservative | Lubricant for instruments |
| **Ichthammol Ointment/ Glycerin** | Ichthammol 10 % | Mild antiseptic, boils |
| **Dimethicone (Silicone) Barrier Cream** | Dimethicone 10 %, cetrimide | ⭐ **Barrier cream — nappy rash, incontinence, bed sores** |

---

## 10.7 CREAMS, PASTES, JELLIES & GELS — SPECIAL POINTS

### ⭐ CREAMS
* ⭐ **o/w (aqueous) creams** — external phase water → **non-greasy, water-washable, cooling,
  "vanishing"**; emulsifier = **emulsifying wax, cetomacrogol, SLS, triethanolamine soaps**;
  ⭐ **suitable for weeping/ moist lesions & hairy areas**.
* ⭐ **w/o (oily) creams** — external phase oil → **greasy, emollient, occlusive, NOT washable**;
  emulsifier = **wool fat, wool alcohols, beeswax–borax, sorbitan esters (Spans), calcium soaps**;
  ⭐ **suitable for dry, scaly skin** (cold cream, zinc cream, oily cream).
* ⭐ **Identifying the type of cream** — **dye test** (a water-soluble dye like amaranth colours the
  continuous phase of an **o/w** cream uniformly; an oil-soluble dye like Sudan III does so for a
  **w/o**); **dilution test** (an o/w cream is **dilutable with water**);
  **conductivity test** (⭐ **o/w conducts electricity; w/o does not**).
* ⭐ **HLB values:** ⭐ **o/w emulsifier HLB 8–18**; **w/o emulsifier HLB 3–6**;
  wetting 7–9; solubilising 15–18; antifoam 1–3.
* ⭐ **All creams containing water MUST contain a PRESERVATIVE** — **methyl & propyl parabens
  (0.2 % + 0.02 %), phenoxyethanol 1 %, chlorocresol 0.1 %, benzoic acid, benzalkonium chloride**.
* ⚠️ **Instability of creams:** **creaming, cracking (coalescence), phase inversion,
  bleeding (liquid separation), microbial spoilage, rancidity, "drying out" of the surface.**

### ⭐ PASTES
* ⭐ **Contain 20–50 % (or more) of insoluble powder** (zinc oxide, starch, calcium carbonate, talc,
  kaolin) → **very stiff**.
* ⭐ **Advantages:** **do NOT melt or flow at body temperature → remain exactly where applied**
  (good for **localised, well-demarcated lesions** such as psoriasis plaques);
  **absorb serous discharge**; **less occlusive than an ointment** (so they allow the skin to
  "breathe"); **protective barrier**; **can carry a high drug load**.
* ⚠️ **Disadvantages:** stiff & **difficult to apply & to remove** (⭐ remove with **arachis/ olive
  oil or liquid paraffin, not water**); can be uncomfortable & unsightly; may be **drying**.
* Prepared by the ⭐ **trituration/ levigation method**, often finished on an **ointment mill**.

### ⭐ JELLIES & GELS
* 📌 **Transparent/ translucent, non-greasy, semisolid** preparations containing a **gelling agent**
  in an **aqueous or hydro-alcoholic** medium.
* ⭐ **Gelling agents:** **tragacanth, sodium alginate, pectin, starch, gelatin, methylcellulose,
  sodium carboxymethylcellulose, hydroxypropyl methylcellulose (HPMC), carbomer (Carbopol),
  bentonite, magnesium aluminium silicate (Veegum), poloxamer, PVP.**
* ⭐ **Types:** **medicated jellies** (ephedrine, lignocaine, ichthammol), **lubricant jellies**
  (K-Y, catheter jelly), **spermicidal jellies (nonoxynol-9)**, **patch-test/ ECG electrode jellies**,
  **hair & cosmetic gels**.
* ⭐ **Advantages:** non-greasy, **water-washable, non-staining**, cooling, cosmetically elegant,
  **good for hairy areas, mucous membranes & eyes**, **thixotropic** (stiff at rest, flows on
  shearing).
* ⚠️ **Must contain a PRESERVATIVE**; may **dry out** → keep in a well-closed container;
  ⚠️ **carbomer gels must be neutralised** (with triethanolamine/ NaOH) to gel.
* ⭐ **Syneresis** — the **shrinking of a gel with the exudation of liquid**; **imbibition** — the
  taking up of liquid **without** an increase in volume; **swelling** — uptake **with** an increase in
  volume; **xerogel** — a dried gel (tragacanth ribbons, gelatin sheets, dry cellulose).

---

## 10.8 ⭐ EVALUATION, PACKING & LABELLING

⭐ **EVALUATION (QUALITY CONTROL) OF SEMISOLIDS**
1. ⭐ **Appearance, colour, odour, homogeneity & absence of GRITTINESS** (rub a little between the
   fingers/ on glass).
2. ⭐ **Consistency/ spreadability** — measured by a **penetrometer** or by the
   **spreadability (slip & drag) test**.
3. ⭐ **Rheology/ viscosity** — semisolids are **non-Newtonian, plastic & thixotropic**;
   they exhibit a **yield value**.
4. ⭐ **pH** — should be near the skin's pH (**4.5–6.5**).
5. **Drug content/ assay & content uniformity.**
6. ⭐ **Drug release/ diffusion studies** — **Franz diffusion cell**, agar-plate or membrane methods.
7. **Water number** — ⭐ the **maximum quantity of water that 100 g of a base can hold** at 20 °C
   (a measure of an absorption base's capacity).
8. ⭐ **Loss on drying / volatile content, minimum fill (net content), weight variation.**
9. ⭐ **Microbial limit test / sterility** (⭐ **eye ointments must be STERILE** and must also pass
   the **metal-particle test**).
10. **Stability testing** — freeze–thaw cycles, centrifugation, elevated temperature; check for
    **bleeding, phase separation, crystal growth, rancidity, colour change**.
11. **Irritancy/ patch test** (Draize test) & sensitisation.

⭐ **PACKING & STORAGE**
* ⭐ **Collapsible tubes (aluminium, tin, plastic/ laminate)** — the **best choice**: they
  **exclude air & moisture, prevent contamination by the patient's fingers**, are convenient, and
  allow easy dispensing of small amounts.
* **Wide-mouth glass or plastic jars** (amber for light-sensitive drugs) — used for larger
  quantities/ extemporaneous dispensing; ⚠️ **more liable to contamination**.
* ⭐ **Eye ointments** — **STERILE collapsible tubes of ≤ 5 g** with a **nozzle**.
* ⚠️ Store in a **cool place**, away from direct heat & sunlight; ⚠️ **do not freeze** (creams crack).
* ⚠️ **PEG bases must not be packed in polystyrene**; ⚠️ **coal tar & phenol attack some plastics**.

⭐ **LABELLING**
* ⭐ ⭐ **"FOR EXTERNAL USE ONLY"** — on a **RED (or red-bordered) label**.
* Name & strength of the preparation, **quantity**, **directions** ("Apply to the affected part
  twice daily"), **patient's name & date**, **"Keep out of the reach of children"**,
  **storage instructions**, expiry.
* Auxiliary labels as needed: **"Avoid contact with the eyes"** · **"Do not apply to broken skin"**
  (counter-irritants) · **"May stain clothing"** (coal tar, dithranol, iodine) ·
  **"Discard 4 weeks after opening"** (eye ointments) ·
  **"Wash hands after use"** · **"Store in a cool place"**.
* ⚠️ **Sterile products:** "Sterile" + the batch number + expiry.

---

## 10.9 CHAPTER 10 — RAPID REVISION SHEET

**DEFINITIONS**
* ⭐ **Ointment** = semisolid, external, drug in a **greasy base**, **anhydrous/ ≤ 20 % water**.
* ⭐ **Cream** = semisolid **EMULSION** (o/w or w/o) → contains **much water**.
* ⭐ **Paste** = **20–50 %+ insoluble powder** → **stiff, does not flow, PROTECTIVE**.
* ⭐ **Jelly/ Gel** = transparent, **non-greasy, water-soluble** gelling agent.
* ⭐ **Cerate** = wax-rich, does not melt at body temperature.
* ⭐ **Glycerogelatin** = melted before application, sets on the skin (**Unna's boot**).

**BASES (4 classes)**
1. ⭐ **Oleaginous/ hydrocarbon** (soft & hard paraffin, Simple Ointment) — **most greasy, most
   occlusive, greatest percutaneous absorption, most stable, NOT washable, absorbs little water**.
2. ⭐ **Absorption** (wool fat, wool alcohols ointment, hydrophilic petrolatum, oily cream) —
   ⭐ **anhydrous but absorbs LARGE amounts of water → for incorporating aqueous liquids**;
   ⚠️ wool fat **sensitises**.
3. ⭐ **Emulsifying/ water-removable (o/w)** (Emulsifying Ointment, Aqueous Cream) —
   ⭐ **water-washable, non-greasy, needs a PRESERVATIVE, less occlusive**.
4. ⭐ **Water-soluble/ greaseless** (**Macrogol/ PEG ointment**) — ⭐ **completely washable,
   non-staining**; ⚠️ **hygroscopic (stings), incompatible with phenols, benzocaine, aspirin,
   sulphonamides, silver salts, iodine, plastics**.

**FORMULAE TO MEMORISE** 🔢
* ⭐ **Simple Ointment = Wool fat 5 + Hard paraffin 5 + Cetostearyl alcohol 5 + Soft paraffin 85.**
* ⭐ **Emulsifying Ointment = Emulsifying wax 30 + White soft paraffin 50 + Liquid paraffin 20.**
* ⭐ **Emulsifying Wax = Cetostearyl alcohol 90 + Sodium lauryl sulphate 10.**
* ⭐ **Aqueous Cream = Emulsifying ointment 30 + Phenoxyethanol 1 + Water to 100.**
* ⭐ **Oily Cream = Wool alcohols ointment 50 + Water 50.**
* ⭐ **Wool Alcohols Ointment = Wool alcohols 6 + Hard paraffin 24 + White soft paraffin 10 +
  Liquid paraffin 60.**
* ⭐ **Macrogol Ointment = PEG 4000 40 + PEG 400 60.**
* ⭐ **Simple Eye Ointment = Liquid paraffin 10 + Wool fat 10 + Yellow soft paraffin 80 (STERILE).**
* ⭐ **Whitfield's Ointment = Benzoic acid 6 % + Salicylic acid 3 %.**
* ⭐ **Lassar's Paste = Zinc oxide 24 + Salicylic acid 2 + Starch 24 + White soft paraffin 50.**
* ⭐ **Zinc Compound Paste = Zinc oxide 25 + Starch 25 + Soft paraffin 50.**
* ⭐ **Sulphur Ointment = 10 % sulphur; Zinc Ointment = 15 % zinc oxide.**
* ⭐ **Glycerin of Starch = Starch 10 + Water 20 + Glycerin 70.**

**EMULSIFYING WAXES**
* ⭐ **Emulsifying wax BP → sodium lauryl sulphate → ANIONIC** (⚠️ not with cationic drugs).
* ⭐ **Cetrimide emulsifying wax → CATIONIC** (⚠️ not with anionic drugs).
* ⭐ **Cetomacrogol emulsifying wax → NON-IONIC → compatible with both.**

**METHODS OF PREPARATION**
* ⭐ **FUSION** — for bases with several waxy solids; ⭐ **melt in DESCENDING order of melting point,
  use minimum heat, add volatiles last, and STIR CONTINUOUSLY UNTIL COLD** (to prevent the waxes
  separating out and to avoid grittiness).
* ⭐ **TRITURATION/ LEVIGATION** — for insoluble powders; **fine powder (no. 120–180 sieve) →
  levigate on a slab with a spatula → geometric dilution**.
  ⭐ **Levigating agent must be MISCIBLE with the base: liquid paraffin for greasy bases;
  glycerin/ propylene glycol/ water for water-miscible bases.**
* ⭐ **EMULSIFICATION** — for creams; ⭐ **heat both phases separately to the SAME temperature
  (60–70 °C), add the internal phase to the external phase with constant stirring, stir till cold**
  (unequal temperatures → lumpy, unstable cream).
* ⭐ Grittiness is corrected with an **OINTMENT MILL (triple roller mill)**.

**SPECIAL MEDICAMENTS**
* ⭐ **Soluble in base → dissolve · Insoluble → levigate · Water-soluble → minimum water into an
  ABSORPTION base (wool fat) or the aqueous phase · Volatile/ thermolabile → add LAST when cool ·
  Eutectics (camphor + menthol) → let them liquefy first · Softening agents (phenol, chloral
  hydrate, coal tar) → add a HARDENING wax · Iodine, tannic acid, mercurials, silver salts →
  use a HORN/ PLASTIC spatula.**

**PERCUTANEOUS ABSORPTION**
* ⭐ Increased by **lipid solubility, an occlusive/ greasy base, skin hydration, broken or inflamed
  skin, thin skin (face/ scrotum), rubbing, larger area & longer contact, penetration enhancers**;
  ⚠️ **infants absorb more**.

**OTHER POINTS**
* ⭐ **o/w emulsifier HLB 8–18; w/o emulsifier HLB 3–6.**
* ⭐ **o/w cream conducts electricity & is dilutable with water; w/o does not.**
* ⭐ **All water-containing semisolids need a preservative** (parabens 0.2 + 0.02 %,
  phenoxyethanol 1 %, chlorocresol 0.1 %).
* ⭐ **Water number** = maximum water 100 g of a base can hold.
* ⭐ **Syneresis** = a gel shrinking & exuding liquid; **xerogel** = a dried gel.
* ⭐ **Eye ointments must be STERILE, in ≤ 5 g sterile tubes, free of metal particles, discarded
  4 weeks after opening.**
* ⭐ **Collapsible tubes are the best container** (exclude air & contamination).
* ⭐ **Label: "FOR EXTERNAL USE ONLY" on a RED label.**
* ⭐ **Pastes are removed with oil (arachis/ olive/ liquid paraffin), not water.**
* Semisolids are **non-Newtonian, plastic & thixotropic**, with a **yield value**;
  consistency measured by a **penetrometer**.

# CHAPTER 11
# 💨 SPRAY SOLUTIONS (NEBULAE), INHALATIONS & ALLIED PREPARATIONS
### *"Ointments, Spray solutions or Nebulas, Inhalations…"* of the syllabus

---

## 11.1 THE FAMILY OF RESPIRATORY & ENT PREPARATIONS — OVERVIEW ⭐

```
        PREPARATIONS FOR THE RESPIRATORY TRACT, THROAT, NOSE, EAR & MOUTH
                                    │
  ┌──────────────┬─────────────┬────┴─────┬──────────────┬───────────────┐
 SPRAYS       INHALATIONS  INSUFFLATIONS  GARGLES &     PAINTS, DROPS
 (NEBULAE)    (VAPOURS)    (dry powder)   MOUTHWASHES   (throat/ nose/ ear)
  │               │              │             │               │
 Liquid        Vapour of a    Powder blown   Liquid for     Applied with a
 broken into   volatile drug  into a cavity  rinsing the    brush, or
 fine droplets inhaled with   with an        mouth/ throat  instilled as
 by an         steam or from  insufflator    (⚠️ not to be   drops
 atomiser/     an inhaler                    swallowed)
 nebuliser
```

⭐ **THE MASTER DEFINITION TABLE (learn each word-for-word)**

| Preparation | 📌 DEFINITION | ⭐ Applied by |
|---|---|---|
| ⭐ **SPRAY (Nebula)** | ⭐ **A liquid preparation — usually an aqueous, oily or alcoholic solution or suspension of a medicament — which is applied to the mucous membranes of the NOSE, THROAT or RESPIRATORY TRACT by being BROKEN UP INTO A FINE SPRAY (fine droplets) by means of an ATOMISER, NEBULISER or PRESSURISED CONTAINER.** *(Nebula = Latin for "mist/cloud")* | **Atomiser/ nebuliser/ aerosol** |
| ⭐ **INHALATION (Inhalatio)** | ⭐ **A liquid preparation containing one or more VOLATILE substances, which is intended to be VAPORISED — usually by adding it to HOT WATER — and the VAPOUR inhaled, to relieve congestion & inflammation of the respiratory tract.** Also includes preparations inhaled directly from an inhaler | **Steam/ vapour inhalation** |
| ⭐ **VAPOUR (Vapor)** | A synonym for an inhalation containing a volatile substance; classically the "Vapours" of the BPC | Vaporised |
| ⭐ **INSUFFLATION** | ⭐ **A finely divided medicated POWDER intended to be BLOWN (insufflated) into a body cavity — the nose, throat, ear or lungs — by means of an INSUFFLATOR** | **Insufflator/ dry-powder inhaler** |
| ⭐ **AEROSOL (Pressurised inhalation/ Metered-dose inhaler, MDI)** | ⭐ **A pressurised dosage form in which the drug is dissolved or suspended in a liquefied or compressed gas PROPELLANT, and is expelled through a valve as a fine spray, foam or powder** | Pressurised canister |
| ⭐ **GARGLE (Gargarisma)** | ⭐ **An aqueous solution used to treat infections/ inflammation of the THROAT, usually dispensed in a CONCENTRATED form to be DILUTED with warm water before use** | Gargled in the throat |
| ⭐ **MOUTHWASH (Collutorium)** | ⭐ An aqueous solution used for **rinsing/ cleansing the MOUTH** (a deodorant, antiseptic or astringent effect) | Rinsed in the mouth |
| ⭐ **THROAT PAINT (Pigmentum)** | ⭐ **A viscous liquid (usually GLYCERIN-based) applied to the throat or mucous membrane with a BRUSH**, the glycerin making it adhere & act for a long time | Brush/ swab |
| ⭐ **NASAL DROPS (Guttae nasales)** | ⭐ An **aqueous (preferably isotonic, pH 5.5–7.5)** solution instilled into the nose | Dropper |
| ⭐ **EAR (Aural) DROPS (Guttae auriculares)** | Solution/ suspension in **water, glycerin, propylene glycol or alcohol**, instilled into the ear | Dropper |
| **DOUCHE** | An aqueous solution **directed against a part or into a cavity** (nasal, throat, eye, vaginal) | Stream/ irrigation |
| **LOZENGE/ PASTILLE** | Solid, sucked slowly for a local throat effect (Ch. 7) | Sucked |

🧠 **MEMORY HOOK** — "**Spray** = **liquid droplets** · **Inhalation** = **vapour** ·
**Insufflation** = **dry powder** · **Aerosol** = **propellant-driven** ·
**Gargle** = **throat (diluted)** · **Mouthwash** = **mouth** · **Paint** = **brush**."

---

## 11.2 ⭐⭐ SPRAYS (NEBULAE)

### 📌 Definition (repeat)
> **A nebula (spray) is a liquid preparation intended to be applied to the nose, throat or
> respiratory tract as a fine spray, by means of an atomiser or nebuliser.**

### ⭐ TYPES OF SPRAY (by vehicle)

| Type | Vehicle | ⭐ Notes |
|---|---|---|
| ⭐ **Aqueous sprays** | Water (isotonic, buffered) | ⭐ **The PREFERRED type** — physiological, do not interfere with ciliary movement |
| ⭐ **Oily sprays** | **Liquid paraffin, arachis oil** | ⚠️ ⭐ **DANGEROUS — droplets may reach the lungs & cause LIPOID (oil) PNEUMONIA, and they PARALYSE the CILIA of the nasal epithelium** → ⭐ **oily nasal sprays/ drops are now AVOIDED, especially in infants & the elderly** |
| **Alcoholic/ hydro-alcoholic sprays** | Alcohol, propylene glycol, glycerin | May be irritant/ drying |
| **Suspension sprays** | Aqueous with a suspending agent | Shake well |
| ⭐ **Pressurised (aerosol) sprays** | Propellant | Metered dose |

### ⭐ VEHICLE REQUIREMENTS FOR A NASAL PREPARATION (very examinable)
1. ⭐ **Isotonic** with nasal secretions — ⭐ **equivalent to 0.9 % w/v sodium chloride**
   (⚠️ hypertonic or hypotonic solutions damage the mucosa & impair ciliary action).
2. ⭐ **pH close to that of nasal fluid — about 5.5 to 6.5 (acceptable range 5.5–7.5)**;
   buffered where necessary. ⚠️ **Ciliary movement is inhibited below pH 5 and above pH 8.**
3. ⭐ **Should NOT inhibit ciliary movement** (⚠️ oils, high concentrations of preservatives,
   surfactants, cocaine & vasoconstrictors in excess all impair the cilia).
4. **Non-irritant, non-sensitising, of low viscosity** so it can be atomised.
5. **Should contain a PRESERVATIVE** (chlorbutol 0.5 %, benzalkonium chloride 0.01 %,
   phenylmercuric acetate/ nitrate 0.002 %, chlorhexidine).
6. **Should be free from suspended particles** (or well suspended & shaken).
7. **Compatible with the container & the atomiser**.

### ⭐ THE ATOMISER / NEBULISER — how a spray is produced

📌 An **atomiser** breaks a liquid into fine droplets by means of a **jet of air (the Venturi/
Bernoulli principle)** — air forced through a narrow orifice creates a **negative pressure** which
draws the liquid up a **dip tube**, and the air stream then **shatters** the liquid into a mist.

| Device | Principle |
|---|---|
| ⭐ **Squeeze-bulb atomiser (insufflator/ nasal spray)** | Hand-squeezed bulb forces air across the liquid → **Venturi effect** |
| ⭐ **Jet (air-jet/ pneumatic) nebuliser** | Compressed air/ oxygen breaks the liquid into an aerosol; **baffles** remove the coarse droplets |
| ⭐ **Ultrasonic nebuliser** | **High-frequency piezoelectric vibration** creates the mist; **no gas needed**, gives a dense fine mist |
| ⭐ **Mesh nebuliser** | Liquid forced through a vibrating fine mesh |
| ⭐ **Pump/ metered spray (mechanical)** | Finger-pump gives a **metered dose**, no propellant |
| ⭐ **Pressurised MDI** | Liquefied propellant expels a metered dose |
| **Spinhaler/ Rotahaler/ Diskhaler/ Turbohaler (DPI)** | Breath-actuated **dry powder** |

🔢 ⭐⭐ **PARTICLE SIZE & DEPOSITION — the most important numbers of this chapter**

| 🔢 Droplet/ particle size | ⭐ Where it deposits |
|---|---|
| **> 20 µm (and > 10 µm)** | ⭐ Impact in the **nose, mouth & throat (oropharynx)** — do not reach the lungs |
| **10 – 5 µm** | **Trachea & large bronchi** |
| ⭐ **5 – 1 µm** | ⭐ **THE OPTIMUM RANGE for deposition in the BRONCHIOLES & ALVEOLI** (the "respirable fraction"; often quoted as **1–5 µm**, best **2–5 µm** or **0.5–5 µm**) |
| **< 1 µm (0.5 µm)** | ⭐ Too fine — remain suspended and are **EXHALED again** without depositing |

🧠 **MEMORY HOOK — "1 to 5 for the lung; over 10 stays in the throat; under 1 is breathed back
out."**
⭐ **Mechanisms of deposition: inertial IMPACTION (large particles), SEDIMENTATION/ gravitational
settling (medium), and DIFFUSION/ Brownian motion (very small).**

### ⭐ TYPICAL SPRAY / NEBULA FORMULATIONS

| Spray | Composition | Use |
|---|---|---|
| **Ephedrine Nasal Drops/ Spray** | Ephedrine hydrochloride **0.5 % or 1 %** in sodium chloride solution (isotonic) with chlorbutol | ⭐ **Nasal decongestant (vasoconstrictor)** ⚠️ rebound congestion (rhinitis medicamentosa) on prolonged use |
| **Xylometazoline/ Oxymetazoline Nasal Spray** | 0.05 % (child) / 0.1 % (adult) | Decongestant |
| **Salbutamol Inhalation/ Nebuliser Solution** | 100 µg per metered dose (MDI); nebuliser 2.5 / 5 mg | ⭐ **Bronchodilator — asthma** |
| **Beclomethasone/ Budesonide MDI** | 50–250 µg per dose | ⭐ **Inhaled corticosteroid (prophylaxis of asthma)**; ⚠️ label **"rinse the mouth after use"** (oral candidiasis) |
| **Ipratropium bromide Inhaler** | 20–40 µg | Anticholinergic bronchodilator |
| **Sodium cromoglycate** | 20 mg (Spinhaler) | Mast-cell stabiliser (prophylaxis) |
| **Adrenaline (Epinephrine) Spray** | 1 : 1000 | Local vasoconstrictor/ haemostatic |
| **Lignocaine (Lidocaine) Topical Spray** | **4 % / 10 %** | ⭐ **Surface anaesthesia of the throat & larynx** before endoscopy |
| **Povidone-iodine/ Chlorhexidine Throat Spray** | 0.5–1 % | Antiseptic |
| **Benzocaine/ Menthol Throat Spray** | — | Local anaesthetic, soothing |
| **Saline nasal spray** | 0.65–0.9 % NaCl | Moisturising, decongestant |
| **Beclometasone + Formoterol / Salmeterol + Fluticasone** | Combination MDI | Asthma/ COPD |

### ⭐ AEROSOLS — the pressurised sprays

📌 **PROPELLANTS**

| Class | Examples | ⭐ Note |
|---|---|---|
| ⭐ **Liquefied gases — chlorofluorocarbons (CFCs)** | **Freon 11 (trichlorofluoromethane), Freon 12 (dichlorodifluoromethane), Freon 114** | ⚠️ ⭐ **BANNED/ phased out** under the **Montreal Protocol** because they **deplete the OZONE layer** |
| ⭐ **Liquefied gases — hydrofluoroalkanes (HFA)** | ⭐ **HFA-134a (norflurane), HFA-227** | ⭐ **The modern replacement for CFCs** |
| **Liquefied hydrocarbons** | **Propane, butane, isobutane** | Cheap, ⚠️ **inflammable** |
| **Compressed gases** | **Nitrogen, carbon dioxide, nitrous oxide** | ⚠️ Pressure **falls** as the container empties; used for foams/ sprays |

⭐ **COMPONENTS OF AN AEROSOL:** **container (aluminium/ tinplate/ glass)**, ⭐ **valve (metering
valve for inhalers)**, **actuator (mouthpiece/ nozzle)**, **dip tube**, **propellant**, and the
**product concentrate** (drug + cosolvent + surfactant/ suspending agent).
⭐ **Types of system:** **two-phase (solution) system** (drug + propellant vapour) and
**three-phase (suspension/ emulsion) system**; **foam aerosols**; **barrier/ bag-in-can systems**.
⭐ **Advantages:** metered accurate dose, protection from air & moisture, sterility maintained, rapid
onset, no contamination of the contents, convenient.
⚠️ **Disadvantages:** expensive, ⚠️ **pressurised → explosion/ fire risk if heated or punctured**,
**poor patient technique** (⭐ hence the **SPACER/ large-volume holding chamber** is used, especially
for children & the elderly), cold "freon effect", oropharyngeal deposition.
⭐ **Labels on an aerosol:** *"Shake well before use"*, *"Do not puncture or incinerate"*,
*"Store below 30 °C"*, *"Keep away from fire & heat"*, *"Rinse the mouth after use"* (steroids).

⭐ **Labels on a nasal/ throat spray:** *"For nasal use only"* / *"For use in the throat"*,
⚠️ *"NOT TO BE SWALLOWED"* (throat sprays), *"Shake well"* (suspensions),
*"Do not use for more than 5–7 days"* (decongestants — rebound congestion),
*"Discard 4 weeks after opening"*.

---

## 11.3 ⭐⭐ INHALATIONS (VAPOURS)

### 📌 Definition (repeat)
> **An inhalation is a liquid preparation containing one or more volatile substances which are
> vaporised — usually by adding the preparation to hot water — and the vapour is inhaled to give
> relief in congestion & inflammation of the respiratory passages.**

### ⭐ HOW AN INHALATION WORKS
* The **volatile substance** (a volatile oil, menthol, camphor, thymol, eucalyptol, benzoin) is
  carried in the **steam** to the mucous membranes of the nose, throat & bronchi.
* ⭐ The **STEAM (moist heat)** itself does most of the work — it **warms & moistens the mucosa,
  liquefies tenacious mucus, promotes drainage, soothes irritated mucosa and relieves congestion**;
  the volatile drug adds a **mild antiseptic, decongestant & counter-irritant** effect and a
  subjective feeling of easier breathing.

### ⭐⭐ THE CLASSICAL METHOD OF USE — *the standard label*
> ⭐ **"Add ONE TEASPOONFUL (about 5 mL — for Compound Benzoin Tincture, 1 mL) of the inhalation to
> a PINT (about 500 mL) of water at about 65 °C (NOT boiling) in a suitable vessel, and inhale the
> vapour for 5–10 minutes, with a towel over the head."**

⚠️ ⭐ **WHY NOT BOILING WATER?**
1. ⚠️ **Risk of SCALDING** the patient's face & respiratory mucosa (this is the main reason).
2. ⚠️ **Too-rapid, wasteful volatilisation** of the drug — the volatile substance is lost all at
   once instead of being released steadily.
3. ⚠️ In the case of **Compound Tincture of Benzoin, boiling water makes the resin coagulate into a
   sticky mass** that clings to the vessel.
🔢 **The recommended temperature is about 65 °C** ("hot, but not boiling").

### ⭐ FORMULATION POINTS
* ⭐ Many inhalations are ⭐ **"light magnesium carbonate" preparations** — the **volatile oil is
  triturated with LIGHT MAGNESIUM CARBONATE**, which acts as a ⭐ **DISPERSING/ DISTRIBUTING AGENT**,
  so that when the inhalation is added to hot water the oil is **finely & evenly dispersed** instead
  of floating in a single layer on the surface. *(Talc is used similarly for aromatic waters.)*
* Volatile ingredients must be added **last** and the container kept **tightly closed**.
* ⭐ Dispensed in a **well-closed, amber bottle**; **RED label — "NOT TO BE TAKEN"/ "For inhalation
  only"**.

### ⭐⭐ OFFICIAL / CLASSICAL INHALATIONS — THE FORMULA TABLE

| Inhalation | 🔢 Composition | ⭐ Dose / use |
|---|---|---|
| ⭐ **Compound Tincture of Benzoin (Friar's Balsam)** *— used as an inhalation* | ⭐ **Benzoin 10 g, Prepared storax 7.5 g, Tolu balsam 2.5 g, Aloes 2.5 g, Alcohol (90 %) to 100 mL** | ⭐ **1 mL (up to 5 mL) added to 500 mL (1 pint) of hot water; inhale the vapour** — laryngitis, bronchitis, catarrh |
| ⭐ **Menthol & Benzoin Inhalation BPC** | ⭐ **Menthol 2 g, Compound tincture of benzoin (to) 100 mL** | 5 mL to 500 mL hot water |
| ⭐ **Menthol Inhalation BPC** | ⭐ **Menthol 2 g, Light magnesium carbonate 1 g, Water to 100 mL** | 5 mL to 500 mL hot water — nasal congestion |
| ⭐ **Menthol & Eucalyptus Inhalation BP** | ⭐ **Menthol 2 g, Eucalyptus oil 10 mL, Light magnesium carbonate 7 g, Water to 100 mL** | ⭐ **5 mL (1 teaspoonful) to 500 mL of hot water** — the classic decongestant inhalation |
| ⭐ **Eucalyptus Inhalation / Oil** | Eucalyptus oil 10 %, light magnesium carbonate, water | Decongestant, antiseptic |
| ⭐ **Creosote Inhalation** | Creosote 10 mL, light magnesium carbonate 7 g, water to 100 mL | Antiseptic expectorant (obsolete) |
| **Pine Oil/ Terebene Inhalation** | Volatile oil + light magnesium carbonate + water | Decongestant |
| **Tincture of Benzoin (simple) Inhalation** | Benzoin 10 % in alcohol | Antiseptic |
| ⭐ **Amyl Nitrite Inhalation** | Amyl nitrite **0.2 mL in a crushable glass "vitrellae"/ ampoule** | ⭐ **Crushed & inhaled — vasodilator (angina); antidote in CYANIDE poisoning** |
| **Ammonia (Aromatic Spirit of Ammonia) Inhalation** | — | ⭐ **Inhaled as a reflex respiratory stimulant in FAINTING** ("smelling salts") |
| **Chloroform & Morphine Inhalation** | — | Antitussive (obsolete) |
| ⭐ **Oxygen inhalation / Nitrous oxide** | Medicinal gases | Hypoxia / anaesthesia & analgesia (**Entonox = 50 % N₂O + 50 % O₂**) |
| **Anaesthetic inhalations** | Ether, halothane, isoflurane, sevoflurane | General anaesthesia |

### ⭐ MODERN INHALATION DEVICES (worth a mention)

| Device | Principle | ⭐ Note |
|---|---|---|
| ⭐ **pMDI (pressurised metered-dose inhaler)** | Propellant-driven metered dose | Needs **coordination**; use a **SPACER** |
| ⭐ **DPI (dry-powder inhaler)** — Rotahaler, Spinhaler, Turbohaler, Diskhaler, Accuhaler | **Breath-actuated dry powder**, drug blended with **lactose carrier** | ⭐ **No propellant, no coordination needed**; ⚠️ needs an adequate inspiratory flow; ⚠️ moisture-sensitive |
| ⭐ **Nebuliser** (jet/ ultrasonic/ mesh) | Continuous mist from a solution | ⭐ **For infants, the very ill & acute severe asthma** — needs no coordination, delivers a large dose |
| ⭐ **Spacer/ holding chamber** | Holds the aerosol cloud so it can be inhaled slowly | ⭐ **Reduces oropharyngeal deposition & candidiasis; essential for children & the elderly** |
| **Steam inhaler/ vaporiser** | Classical steam inhalation | Domestic use |
| **Nasal inhaler stick** | Menthol/ camphor impregnated wick | Convenient decongestant |

### ⭐ ADVANTAGES & DISADVANTAGES OF THE INHALATION ROUTE
✔ ⭐ **Direct delivery to the target organ → rapid onset, small dose, minimal systemic side-effects
(e.g. inhaled steroids), avoids first-pass metabolism, huge alveolar absorbing surface (~ 70–100 m²)
and rich blood supply.**
⚠️ **Dose is difficult to control precisely; depends heavily on the patient's technique & inspiratory
effort; most of the dose is lost in the oropharynx & device; may irritate/ cause bronchospasm &
cough; expensive devices; unsuitable for irritant drugs.**

---

## 11.4 INSUFFLATIONS

📌 **DEFINITION** — **An insufflation is a finely divided, medicated powder intended to be blown
into a body cavity — the nose, throat, ear, or the respiratory tract — by means of an
INSUFFLATOR.**

⭐ **Points**
* The powder must be ⭐ **very fine (pass a no. 120–180 sieve, ideally 1–5 µm for the lung)**, dry &
  free-flowing.
* Delivered by an **insufflator** (a squeeze-bulb powder blower) or, in modern practice, by a
  ⭐ **dry-powder inhaler (DPI)** in which the micronised drug is blended with a ⭐ **coarse LACTOSE
  carrier** (the lactose improves flow & metering and gives the patient a "taste" confirming the
  dose).
* ⚠️ **Disadvantages:** the powder may **irritate** the mucosa & provoke coughing/ bronchospasm;
  dosing is inaccurate; ⚠️ **hygroscopic powders cake**.
* ⭐ **Examples:** **Sodium cromoglycate (Spinhaler), salbutamol & beclomethasone DPI,
  antibiotic/ sulphonamide throat & ear insufflations (classical), amphotericin, tobramycin**.
* ⭐ Store in an **air-tight, moisture-proof** container; label **"For insufflation — not to be
  taken"**.

---

## 11.5 GARGLES, MOUTHWASHES & THROAT PAINTS ⭐

### ⭐ GARGLES

📌 **DEFINITION** — **An aqueous solution used to treat infection or inflammation of the THROAT
(pharynx), usually dispensed in a CONCENTRATED form to be DILUTED with warm water before use.**

⭐ **Points**
* ⭐ Dispensed **concentrated** (economy of packing & transport) — ⭐ the **label must state the
  dilution** (e.g. *"Dilute one part with 10 parts of warm water"*).
* ⭐ Used **warm** (comfortable & more effective); brought into contact with the throat by
  exhaling through the liquid.
* ⭐ **LABELS: "NOT TO BE SWALLOWED", "Dilute before use", "For gargling only" — on a RED
  (external) label.**

| Gargle | Composition | Use |
|---|---|---|
| ⭐ **Potassium Chlorate & Phenol Gargle BPC** | Potassium chlorate, phenol, water (concentrated) | Antiseptic, astringent |
| ⭐ **Sodium Chloride Gargle / Compound Sodium Chloride Mouthwash** | Sodium chloride + sodium bicarbonate (isotonic saline) | ⭐ **Simple soothing, cleansing** |
| **Phenol Gargle** | Liquefied phenol 1.5 % + glycerin | Antiseptic, analgesic |
| **Povidone-iodine Gargle** | 1 % (diluted) | Antiseptic |
| **Chlorhexidine Gluconate Mouthwash/ Gargle** | 0.2 % | ⭐ Antiseptic, gingivitis; ⚠️ stains teeth |
| **Hydrogen Peroxide Mouthwash** | 1.5–3 % (6 % diluted) | Oxidising, deodorant, ⭐ anaerobic infection |
| **Potassium Permanganate Gargle** | 1 : 5000 – 1 : 10 000 | Antiseptic, deodorant |
| **Tincture of Myrrh Gargle** | Diluted tincture | Astringent |
| **Benzydamine Mouthwash/ Spray** | 0.15 % | Analgesic, anti-inflammatory |
| **Thymol/ Compound Thymol Glycerin** | Thymol, glycerin, alcohol, colour | Deodorant mouthwash |

### ⭐ MOUTHWASHES
📌 **Aqueous solutions with a deodorant, antiseptic, astringent or soothing action, used for
rinsing the MOUTH.** ⭐ Also often dispensed concentrated. ⭐ Label **"Not to be swallowed"**.
⭐ Contain: **antiseptics (chlorhexidine, cetylpyridinium, hexetidine, povidone-iodine)**,
**astringents (zinc chloride, alum, tannic acid)**, **deodorants (thymol, menthol, eucalyptol)**,
**alcohol & glycerin (vehicle)**, **sweeteners, flavours & colour**, ⭐ often **fluoride** (0.05 %
sodium fluoride) for caries prevention.

### ⭐ THROAT PAINTS (PIGMENTA)
📌 **Viscous liquid preparations, usually with GLYCERIN as the vehicle, applied to the throat or
oral mucosa with a BRUSH or swab.**
⭐ **Why glycerin?** — ⭐ It is **viscous & adhesive, so it CLINGS to the mucous membrane and
prolongs the contact/ action of the drug**; it is also **hygroscopic (so it draws out inflammatory
fluid, giving a decongestant & soothing effect)**, **sweet**, and **mildly antiseptic/ preservative**.

| Throat paint | Composition | Use |
|---|---|---|
| ⭐ **Compound Glycerin of Thymol (Mandl's Paint type)** | ⭐ **Iodine, potassium iodide, glycerin, peppermint oil, water** *(Mandl's paint)*; Compound Thymol Glycerin = thymol, glycerin, alcohol, colour | ⭐ **Antiseptic & astringent throat paint — pharyngitis, tonsillitis** |
| **Glycerin of Tannic Acid** | Tannic acid 10 % in glycerin | Astringent |
| ⭐ **Glycerin of Borax (Honey of Borax)** | Borax, glycerin, honey/ purified water | ⭐ **ORAL THRUSH (candidiasis) in infants**, sore mouth |
| **Glycerin of Ichthammol** | Ichthammol 10 % in glycerin | Mild antiseptic |
| **Gentian Violet (Crystal Violet) Paint 0.5–1 %** | Aqueous/ alcoholic | Antifungal, antiseptic (thrush) |
| **Magenta (Castellani's) Paint** | Basic fuchsin, phenol, resorcinol, acetone, boric acid, alcohol | Antifungal |
| **Compound Podophyllum Paint 15–25 %** | Podophyllum resin in compound tincture of benzoin | ⭐ **Genital & plantar warts** |
| **Salicylic Acid Collodion/ Paint** | 12–20 % | Corns, warts |
| **Iodine Paint / Tincture of Iodine** | Iodine 2.5 % + KI 2.5 % in alcohol | Skin antiseptic |

---

## 11.6 NASAL & EAR DROPS ⭐

### ⭐ NASAL DROPS
* ⭐ **Vehicle: AQUEOUS, isotonic (≈ 0.9 % NaCl), pH 5.5–7.5, with a preservative** —
  ⚠️ ⭐ **oily vehicles are avoided (LIPOID PNEUMONIA & ciliary paralysis)**.
* Contents: **decongestants (ephedrine 0.5–1 %, xylometazoline 0.05–0.1 %)**,
  **antihistamines**, **corticosteroids (beclomethasone, fluticasone)**,
  **antibiotics**, **saline**, **sodium bicarbonate (to soften crusts)**.
* ⭐ **Technique:** the patient should **lie supine with the head tilted back/ over the edge of the
  bed** so the drops reach the whole nasal cavity, and remain so for a minute.
* ⭐ **Labels:** *"For nasal use only"*, *"Do not use for more than 5–7 days"* (⚠️ **rebound
  congestion — rhinitis medicamentosa**), *"Discard 4 weeks after opening"*, *"Shake well"*
  (suspensions).

### ⭐ EAR (AURAL) DROPS
* ⭐ **Vehicles: water, GLYCERIN, PROPYLENE GLYCOL, alcohol, and (for wax) oils** —
  ⭐ **glycerin & propylene glycol are viscous & hygroscopic → they cling and are dehydrating/
  soothing.**
* Contents: **antibiotics (chloramphenicol, gentamicin, neomycin)**, **corticosteroids**,
  **antifungals (clotrimazole)**, ⭐ **wax softeners (olive/ arachis oil, sodium bicarbonate 5 %,
  glycerin, docusate, urea-hydrogen peroxide)**, **local anaesthetics (benzocaine)**,
  **antiseptics (boric acid in alcohol, acetic acid 2 %)**, **spirit (drying) drops**.
* ⭐ **Technique:** ⭐ **warm the bottle to body temperature in the hand before use** (⚠️ cold drops
  cause **giddiness/ vertigo & pain**); lie with the affected ear uppermost, instil, and stay for
  a few minutes.
* ⚠️ ⭐ **Aminoglycoside ear drops must NOT be used when the ear drum is PERFORATED**
  (risk of **ototoxicity**).
* ⭐ **Labels:** *"For use in the ears only"*, *"Warm before use"*, *"Discard 4 weeks after
  opening"*, *"Not to be taken"*.
* ⚠️ Ear drops for use after **ear surgery or with a perforated drum must be STERILE**.

---

## 11.7 CHAPTER 11 — RAPID REVISION SHEET

**DEFINITIONS**
* ⭐ **SPRAY (NEBULA)** = liquid **broken into fine droplets** by an **atomiser/ nebuliser** for the
  nose, throat or respiratory tract. (*Nebula = mist/cloud*)
* ⭐ **INHALATION** = liquid containing **volatile** substances, **vaporised (usually in hot water)**
  and the **vapour inhaled**.
* ⭐ **INSUFFLATION** = medicated **fine POWDER blown** into a cavity with an **insufflator**.
* ⭐ **AEROSOL/ MDI** = drug + **propellant** in a **pressurised** container, metered valve.
* ⭐ **GARGLE** = for the **THROAT**, dispensed **CONCENTRATED, to be diluted with warm water**.
* ⭐ **MOUTHWASH** = for **rinsing the MOUTH**. ⭐ Both are labelled **"NOT TO BE SWALLOWED"**.
* ⭐ **THROAT PAINT (Pigmentum)** = **viscous, GLYCERIN-based**, applied with a **BRUSH** —
  glycerin **clings & prolongs action, is hygroscopic (decongestant) and antiseptic**.

**PARTICLE SIZE (the key numbers)** 🔢
* ⭐ **> 10–20 µm → nose, mouth & throat.**
* ⭐ **5–10 µm → trachea & large bronchi.**
* ⭐ **1–5 µm → OPTIMUM: bronchioles & alveoli (the "respirable fraction").**
* ⭐ **< 1 µm → exhaled again.**
* Deposition by **impaction, sedimentation & diffusion**.

**NASAL PREPARATION REQUIREMENTS**
* ⭐ **Isotonic (≈ 0.9 % NaCl) · pH 5.5–6.5 (5.5–7.5) · must NOT inhibit ciliary movement ·
  preservative · non-irritant · AQUEOUS (⚠️ NOT oily).**
* ⚠️ ⭐ **Oily nasal drops/ sprays cause LIPOID PNEUMONIA and PARALYSE the CILIA → avoided.**
* ⚠️ **Decongestants for > 5–7 days → REBOUND congestion (rhinitis medicamentosa).**

**INHALATIONS**
* ⭐ **Standard direction: 1 teaspoonful (5 mL) — or 1 mL of Compound Benzoin Tincture — added to
  500 mL (1 pint) of hot water at about 65 °C; inhale the vapour.**
* ⚠️ ⭐ **NOT boiling water** — risk of **scalding**, **too-rapid loss of the volatile drug**, and
  (with benzoin) **coagulation of the resin into a sticky mass**.
* ⭐ **LIGHT MAGNESIUM CARBONATE is added as a DISPERSING/ DISTRIBUTING AGENT** so the volatile oil
  spreads evenly in the hot water.
* ⭐ **Menthol & Eucalyptus Inhalation BP = Menthol 2 g + Eucalyptus oil 10 mL + Light magnesium
  carbonate 7 g + Water to 100 mL.**
* ⭐ **Menthol Inhalation = Menthol 2 g + Light magnesium carbonate 1 g + water to 100 mL.**
* ⭐ **Compound Tincture of Benzoin (Friar's Balsam) = Benzoin 10 + Storax 7.5 + Tolu 2.5 +
  Aloes 2.5 + alcohol to 100.**
* ⭐ **Amyl nitrite** is inhaled from a **crushable ampoule (vitrellae)** — angina & **cyanide
  poisoning**; ⭐ **Aromatic spirit of ammonia** is inhaled in **fainting**.
* ⭐ Advantages of the inhalation route: **direct delivery, rapid onset, small dose, few systemic
  effects, avoids first pass, huge alveolar surface (70–100 m²)**.

**AEROSOLS**
* ⭐ **CFC propellants (Freon 11, 12, 114) are BANNED (Montreal Protocol — ozone depletion) and are
  replaced by HFA-134a.** Hydrocarbons (propane, butane) are **inflammable**; compressed gases
  (N₂, CO₂) lose pressure.
* ⭐ **Spacers reduce oropharyngeal deposition & candidiasis, and remove the need for coordination.**
* ⭐ **DPIs use a coarse LACTOSE carrier**, are breath-actuated, and need no propellant.
* ⭐ **Nebulisers** (jet/ ultrasonic) are for **infants, the very ill & acute severe asthma**.
* ⭐ Labels: *"Shake well"*, *"Do not puncture or incinerate"*, *"Rinse the mouth after use"*
  (steroids).

**EAR & NOSE DROPS**
* ⭐ **Ear drops: glycerin/ propylene glycol vehicles; WARM before use (cold → vertigo);
  aminoglycosides contraindicated with a PERFORATED drum (ototoxicity); sterile if the drum is
  perforated or after surgery.**
* ⭐ **Both ear & nasal drops: discard 4 weeks after opening.**

**KEY PAINTS/ GARGLES**
* ⭐ **Mandl's paint (compound glycerin of thymol/ iodine-KI-glycerin) — throat antiseptic.**
* ⭐ **Honey/ Glycerin of Borax — oral thrush in infants.**
* ⭐ **Compound Podophyllum Paint 15–25 % in compound benzoin tincture — warts.**
* ⭐ **Chlorhexidine 0.2 % mouthwash — gingivitis (⚠️ stains teeth).**
* ⭐ **Potassium permanganate gargle 1 : 5000 – 1 : 10 000.**
