# CHAPTER 12
# 🧪 MIXTURES — GENERAL RULES ABOUT THEIR PREPARATION
### *"…General rule about preparation of Mixtures"* of the syllabus

---

## 12.1 DEFINITION & CHARACTERISTICS

📌 **DEFINITION (learn word-for-word)**
> **A MIXTURE (Mistura) is a LIQUID PREPARATION FOR ORAL (internal) USE, in which one or more
> medicaments are DISSOLVED, SUSPENDED or DISPERSED in a suitable aqueous VEHICLE, and which is
> intended to be taken in DIVIDED DOSES.**

⭐ **KEY CHARACTERISTICS OF A MIXTURE**
* ⭐ **For INTERNAL (oral) use** → **plain bottle + WHITE label**.
* ⭐ Taken in ⭐ **DIVIDED DOSES** — usually **10 mL or 15 mL** (child 5 mL), from a bottle of
  **100, 150, 200 or 300 mL**.
* ⭐ The vehicle is **AQUEOUS** (water, chloroform water, aromatic waters, syrups).
* ⭐ **Extemporaneous**, freshly prepared, with a **SHORT SHELF-LIFE** (⭐ usually labelled to be
  used within **7–14 days**; discard after that).
* ⭐ **No preservative is added deliberately in most cases** — ⭐ the **chloroform of chloroform
  water (0.25 % v/v) acts as the preservative & mild flavour**.

⚠️ **TRAP — MIXTURE vs DRAUGHT**
* ⭐ **Mixture = taken in DIVIDED doses** (several doses in one bottle).
* ⭐ **Draught (Haustus) = a SINGLE dose, usually 50 mL.**

---

## 12.2 ⭐⭐ CLASSIFICATION / TYPES OF MIXTURES

```
                              MIXTURES
                                  │
   ┌───────────────┬──────────────┼───────────────┬────────────────┐
① TRUE SOLUTION  ② SUSPENSION   ③ EMULSION     ④ MIXTURES with  ⑤ EFFERVESCENT
   (all soluble)      │           (oil + water)   colloids/gums     MIXTURES
                      │                            (mucilages)
            ┌─────────┴─────────┐
     (a) DIFFUSIBLE      (b) INDIFFUSIBLE
        suspension          suspension
     (light, disperse    (heavy, settle fast →
      easily, settle      MUST have a suspending
      slowly — NO         agent)
      suspending
      agent needed)
```

### ⭐⭐ THE MOST IMPORTANT DISTINCTION OF THE CHAPTER
### DIFFUSIBLE vs INDIFFUSIBLE POWDERS

📌 **DIFFUSIBLE (dispersible) powders** — ⭐ **light, bulky, insoluble powders which, on shaking,
disperse readily & evenly throughout the vehicle and remain in suspension long enough for an
accurate dose to be poured; they therefore need NO suspending agent.**

📌 **INDIFFUSIBLE powders** — ⭐ **heavy, dense, insoluble powders which do NOT disperse readily,
settle rapidly to the bottom (and often CAKE), and therefore MUST be dispensed with a SUSPENDING
AGENT** so that a uniform dose can be poured.

| ⭐ **DIFFUSIBLE powders (no suspending agent)** | ⭐ **INDIFFUSIBLE powders (suspending agent NEEDED)** |
|---|---|
| **Light & Heavy MAGNESIUM CARBONATE** | ⭐ **Aspirin (acetylsalicylic acid)** |
| **Light & Heavy MAGNESIUM OXIDE** | ⭐ **Phenobarbitone / Phenacetin** |
| ⭐ **MAGNESIUM TRISILICATE** | ⭐ **SULPHUR (sublimed/ precipitated)** |
| **CHALK (Prepared/ Precipitated calcium carbonate)** | ⭐ **CALAMINE** |
| ⭐ **LIGHT KAOLIN** *(heavy kaolin is indiffusible)* | ⭐ **ZINC OXIDE** |
| **BISMUTH CARBONATE / Bismuth subnitrate** | ⭐ **HEAVY KAOLIN** |
| **Bismuth subgallate** | **Salol (phenyl salicylate) / Sulphonamides** |
| **Precipitated calcium phosphate** | **Resins & resinous matter (from tinctures)** |
| **Cinchona/ Rhubarb powder (light vegetable powders)** | **Iodoform, Bismuth subcarbonate (some texts)** |
| **Activated charcoal (light)** | **Barium sulphate** |

🧠 **MEMORY HOOK**
> ⭐ **DIFFUSIBLE = "the MAGNESIUMS + CHALK + LIGHT KAOLIN + BISMUTHS."**
> ⭐ **INDIFFUSIBLE = "ASPIRIN, SULPHUR, CALAMINE, ZINC OXIDE, PHENOBARBITONE, HEAVY KAOLIN."**
> 🧠 *"**A-S-C-Z-P-H** needs help to stay up."*

⚠️ **TRAP — LIGHT kaolin is DIFFUSIBLE, but HEAVY kaolin is INDIFFUSIBLE.**
⚠️ **TRAP — Magnesium trisilicate is DIFFUSIBLE (light & bulky)** — yet the official **Magnesium
Trisilicate Mixture** does contain a suspending agent (**compound tragacanth powder**) for elegance
and to keep the peppermint oil dispersed; be guided by the official formula.

---

## 12.3 ⭐⭐ SUSPENDING AGENTS

📌 **DEFINITION** — A **suspending agent** is a substance added to a liquid preparation to
⭐ **increase the VISCOSITY of the vehicle**, thereby **slowing the rate of sedimentation** of the
dispersed particles (Stokes' law) and keeping the suspension uniform long enough for an accurate
dose to be measured.

🔢 **STOKES' LAW — the theory behind suspending agents**
```
        d² (ρs − ρl) g
v  =  ──────────────────
             18 η
```
⭐ Sedimentation velocity **v** is **decreased** by: ↓ **particle diameter (d)**,
↓ **density difference (ρs − ρl)**, and ⭐ ↑ **VISCOSITY (η) of the vehicle** — the last is what a
suspending agent does.

### 📋 THE SUSPENDING AGENTS ⭐

| Class | Examples | 🔢 Usual concentration |
|---|---|---|
| ⭐ **Natural gums & mucilages** | ⭐ **TRAGACANTH (the classic best), ACACIA, Compound Tragacanth Powder, Sterculia (karaya), Guar gum, Agar, Sodium alginate, Pectin, Gelatin, Starch, Isapghula** | ⭐ **Tragacanth mucilage 1.25 % · Compound tragacanth powder 2 % · Acacia mucilage 35 % (as an emulgent)** |
| ⭐ **Semi-synthetic cellulose derivatives** | ⭐ **Methylcellulose, Sodium carboxymethylcellulose (Na-CMC), Hydroxyethyl/ Hydroxypropyl methylcellulose (HPMC), Microcrystalline cellulose** | 0.5–2 % |
| ⭐ **Synthetic polymers** | **Carbomer (Carbopol), Polyvinylpyrrolidone (PVP), Polyvinyl alcohol, Poloxamer** | 0.1–1 % |
| ⭐ **Clays (inorganic)** | ⭐ **BENTONITE, Magnesium aluminium silicate (Veegum), Attapulgite, Colloidal silicon dioxide (Aerosil)** | 1–5 % |
| **Sugars & polyols** | **Syrup, sorbitol, glycerin** (increase viscosity & palatability) | — |
| **Surfactants (wetting agents)** | **Polysorbates (Tween), Sodium lauryl sulphate, Sorbitan esters** | 0.05–0.5 % |
| **Flocculating agents** | **Electrolytes (NaCl, KCl), surfactants, polymers** | Small |

⭐ **THE OFFICIAL CHOICE IN DISPENSING: ⭐ COMPOUND TRAGACANTH POWDER, 2 g per 100 mL (2 % w/v).**
🔢 **Compound Powder of Tragacanth = Tragacanth 15 g + Acacia 20 g + Starch 20 g + Sucrose 45 g.**

⭐ **IDEAL PROPERTIES OF A SUSPENDING AGENT**
1. Should produce a suspension that ⭐ **settles slowly and re-disperses easily on shaking**.
2. Should be **chemically inert & compatible** with the medicaments.
3. Should be **non-toxic, tasteless & odourless**.
4. Should give a ⭐ **pourable (not too viscous) product** and be **thixotropic** (thick at rest,
   flows on shaking).
5. Should be **stable** over the shelf-life and over a range of pH & temperature.
6. Should be **cheap & readily available**.

⭐ **FLOCCULATED vs DEFLOCCULATED SUSPENSIONS** (frequently examined)

| Feature | ⭐ **FLOCCULATED** | ⭐ **DEFLOCCULATED** |
|---|---|---|
| Particles | Form **loose aggregates (flocs)** | Exist as **separate individual particles** |
| Rate of settling | ⭐ **RAPID** | ⭐ **SLOW** |
| Sediment | ⭐ **Loose, fluffy, LARGE volume — EASILY RE-DISPERSED** | ⭐ **Hard, dense, closely packed CAKE — DIFFICULT/ impossible to re-disperse** |
| Supernatant | **Clear** | **Cloudy/ turbid for a long time** |
| ⭐ **CAKING** | ⭐ **Does NOT cake** | ⭐ **CAKES — the major fault of a suspension** |
| Appearance | Less elegant | More elegant |
| ⭐ Which is preferred? | ⭐ **A CONTROLLED FLOCCULATION (with a suspending agent to slow the settling) is the IDEAL** | — |

🧠 **MEMORY HOOK** — "**FLOC = FLUFFY sediment, shakes up easily (good). DEFLOC = DENSE cake, will
not shake up (bad).**"

📌 **Sedimentation volume (F)** = ⭐ **F = Vu ÷ Vo** (volume of the final sediment ÷ original volume of
the suspension). ⭐ **The nearer F is to 1 (or > 1), the better the suspension.**
📌 **Degree of flocculation (β)** = F (flocculated) ÷ F (deflocculated).

---

## 12.4 ⭐⭐ THE GENERAL RULES FOR THE PREPARATION OF MIXTURES
### ⭐ (this is the exact syllabus question — learn the numbered list)

### ⭐ THE ORDER OF ADDITION — THE "GOLDEN RULE"
> ⭐ **Ingredients are added to the vehicle in the ORDER OF THEIR SOLUBILITY & NATURE:
> SOLIDS FIRST (soluble solids dissolved in the vehicle), then LIQUIDS, then the SUSPENDING AGENT
> & INSOLUBLE POWDERS, then the tinctures & resinous/ alcoholic liquids (SLOWLY, LAST), then the
> vehicle to volume.**

### ⭐⭐ THE STANDARD SEQUENCE (memorise this list — it answers the question directly)

| # | ⭐ Step / Rule | ⭐ Reason |
|---|---|---|
| **1** | ⭐ **Do the CALCULATIONS first** for the total quantity, and check the **dose & maximum dose** and for **incompatibilities** | Accuracy & safety |
| **2** | ⭐ **Select a clean, dry, graduated bottle of the correct size** — the finished mixture should nearly fill it | Elegance; allows shaking |
| **3** | ⭐ **Dissolve the SOLUBLE SOLIDS** first, in a **portion (about half to two-thirds) of the vehicle**, in a mortar or beaker — ⭐ the **most soluble/ largest quantity first**, and each solid **separately** if they may react | Complete dissolution; prevents interaction |
| **4** | ⭐ **Powder & triturate any solid** that dissolves slowly, and use **gentle warming** if permitted (⚠️ **never heat volatile or thermolabile substances**) | Speeds dissolution |
| **5** | ⭐ **Add the LIQUID ingredients** next — **syrups, glycerin, liquid extracts, aromatic waters** — with stirring | They are miscible with water |
| **6** | ⭐ For a SUSPENSION: **triturate the INSOLUBLE (indiffusible) POWDER with the SUSPENDING AGENT** (compound tragacanth powder) in a **DRY mortar first**, then add a **small amount of the vehicle to make a SMOOTH PASTE (levigation)**, and then **dilute gradually with more vehicle**, transferring to the bottle with rinsings | ⚠️ ⭐ **If water is added to a gum all at once, the gum forms LUMPS/ balls that will not disperse** — dry mixing then gradual addition prevents lumping |
| **7** | ⭐ **DIFFUSIBLE powders** are simply **triturated with a little vehicle** and added — ⭐ **no suspending agent** | They disperse readily |
| **8** | ⭐ **Add TINCTURES & other ALCOHOLIC/ RESINOUS liquids LAST but ONE — SLOWLY, in a fine stream, with CONSTANT STIRRING, into the BULK of the aqueous vehicle** (⚠️ **never the reverse — never add water to the tincture**) | ⭐ Prevents the **resin from precipitating in large sticky lumps**; gives a **fine, evenly dispersed precipitate** instead |
| **9** | ⭐ Where a **potent tincture/ liquid** is prescribed in a small volume, measure it with a **calibrated dropper/ pipette** or by the **aliquot method** | Accuracy |
| **10** | ⭐ **Add VOLATILE substances & flavours near the END, at room temperature** | Prevents loss by evaporation |
| **11** | ⭐ **MAKE UP TO THE FINAL VOLUME with the vehicle** ("ad 100 mL"), **using the vehicle to RINSE the mortar/ measure and adding the rinsings** | ⚠️ Ensures **no drug is lost** on the apparatus & the volume/ dose is correct |
| **12** | ⭐ **SHAKE/ mix well**, then **cork/ cap the bottle** | Uniformity |
| **13** | ⭐ **LABEL** — patient's name, date, name of the mixture, **DOSE & directions**, and the auxiliary labels: ⭐ **"SHAKE THE BOTTLE WELL BEFORE USE"** (for **all suspensions & emulsions**), *"Store in a cool place"*, *"Keep out of the reach of children"*, expiry | Legal & safety requirement |
| **14** | ⭐ **Final check** against the prescription, then dispense with **counselling** | Good dispensing practice |

⚠️ ⭐ **THE FIVE "NEVERS" OF MIXTURE PREPARATION**
1. ⚠️ **Never add water to a gum/ mucilage all at once** → lumps.
2. ⚠️ **Never add the aqueous vehicle TO a tincture** → the resin precipitates in a sticky lump.
   **Always add the tincture slowly to the vehicle.**
3. ⚠️ **Never heat volatile, thermolabile or alcoholic ingredients.**
4. ⚠️ **Never make up to volume without rinsing the mortar & measure into the bottle.**
5. ⚠️ **Never omit "Shake well" from a suspension or emulsion label.**

### ⭐ THE VEHICLES USED IN MIXTURES

| Vehicle | ⭐ Note |
|---|---|
| ⭐ **CHLOROFORM WATER (single strength = 0.25 % v/v chloroform)** | ⭐ **The commonest vehicle** — chloroform acts as a **PRESERVATIVE (antimicrobial), a mild flavour/ sweetener and a carminative**. Prepared by diluting **Double-Strength Chloroform Water (0.5 %) 1 : 1 with water** |
| **Purified/ Potable water** | Simple vehicle |
| ⭐ **Aromatic waters** — **Peppermint, Anise, Dill, Fennel, Cinnamon, Camphor water** | **Carminative & flavouring** vehicles |
| ⭐ **Syrup (66.7 % w/w sucrose)** | **Sweetening & viscosity-increasing** vehicle; self-preserving |
| **Glycerin, Sorbitol solution, Propylene glycol** | Sweet, viscous, cosolvent, preservative |
| **Alcohol (as part of tinctures)** | Cosolvent & preservative |
| **Mucilages (tragacanth/ acacia)** | Suspending vehicles |

---

## 12.5 ⭐ WORKED EXAMPLES — THE FOUR CLASSES OF MIXTURE

### ⭐ (A) A TRUE-SOLUTION MIXTURE
```
℞  Ammonium chloride            6 g
   Sodium citrate               3 g
   Liquorice liquid extract     8 mL
   Chloroform water        ad 100 mL
   M. ft. mist.  Sig.: 10 mL t.d.s. p.c.
```
⭐ **Method:** Dissolve the **ammonium chloride** and then the **sodium citrate** in about **60 mL of
chloroform water** in a beaker/ mortar (stir; both are freely soluble) → add the **liquorice liquid
extract**, stirring → transfer to a 100 mL bottle, **rinsing** the beaker with chloroform water →
**make up to 100 mL** → shake.
⭐ **Labels:** white label; ⚠️ **"Shake well" is NOT required** (it is a clear solution).
⭐ **Analysis:** Basis = ammonium chloride; Adjuvant = sodium citrate; Corrective = liquorice
(masks the salty taste); Vehicle = chloroform water. **Number of doses = 100/10 = 10.**

### ⭐ (B) A DIFFUSIBLE-SUSPENSION MIXTURE (no suspending agent)
```
℞  Light magnesium carbonate    5 g
   Sodium bicarbonate           4 g
   Peppermint water        ad 100 mL
   M. ft. mist.  Sig.: 10 mL t.d.s.
```
⭐ **Method:** Dissolve the **sodium bicarbonate** (soluble) in part of the peppermint water →
in a mortar, **triturate the light magnesium carbonate with a small amount of the vehicle to a
smooth cream**, then dilute gradually → combine, transfer with rinsings, **make up to volume**.
⭐ **No suspending agent** — magnesium carbonate is **diffusible**.
⭐ **Label: "SHAKE THE BOTTLE WELL BEFORE USE."**

### ⭐ (C) AN INDIFFUSIBLE-SUSPENSION MIXTURE (suspending agent REQUIRED)
```
℞  Sulphur, precipitated        3 g
   Compound tragacanth powder   2 g
   Chloroform water        ad 100 mL
   M. ft. mist.  Sig.: 10 mL b.d.
```
⭐ **Method:**
1. ⭐ **Mix the precipitated sulphur and the compound tragacanth powder together DRY** in a mortar
   (⭐ this is the crucial step — dry mixing prevents the gum from lumping).
2. Add a **small quantity of chloroform water** and triturate to a **smooth, lump-free paste
   (levigate)**.
3. **Gradually dilute** with more vehicle, triturating continuously.
4. Transfer to the bottle, **rinse the mortar** with the vehicle, add the rinsings, and
   **make up to 100 mL**. Shake well.
⭐ **Label: "SHAKE THE BOTTLE WELL BEFORE USE."**
⭐ **Why sulphur needs the suspending agent:** it is **heavy, hydrophobic (poorly wetted) &
indiffusible** — without it, the sulphur would **float or cake** and the doses would be grossly
uneven.

### ⭐ (D) A MIXTURE CONTAINING A TINCTURE (resinous precipitation)
```
℞  Compound tincture of benzoin  5 mL
   Syrup                        10 mL
   Chloroform water         ad 100 mL
```
⭐ **Method:** Place the **syrup and most of the chloroform water in the bottle/ beaker**, then
⭐ **add the compound tincture of benzoin SLOWLY, in a thin stream, with CONSTANT STIRRING/ shaking**
→ a **fine, milky, evenly dispersed** precipitate of resin forms → make up to volume.
⚠️ **If the water were added to the tincture, the resin would separate as sticky lumps that adhere
to the bottle.** Add a **suspending agent** if the precipitate settles too fast.
⭐ **Label: "Shake well before use."**

### ⭐ (E) AN EMULSION MIXTURE
```
℞  Liquid paraffin              30 mL
   Acacia (powdered)            q.s.
   Water                   ad 100 mL
```
⭐ **Method (dry gum/ Continental method):** liquid paraffin is a **MINERAL oil** →
⭐ **Oil : Water : Gum = 3 : 2 : 1** → for 30 mL of oil, use **10 g of acacia and 20 mL of water**.
Triturate the **acacia with the oil** in a **dry mortar**, add the **20 mL of water ALL AT ONCE**,
and triturate **rapidly & continuously in one direction** until a ⭐ **"crackling"/ clicking sound**
is heard and a **thick white PRIMARY EMULSION** is formed; then **dilute gradually** with water to
100 mL.
⭐ **Label: "Shake well before use."**

### ⭐ (F) AN EFFERVESCENT MIXTURE
📌 The **acid** and the **carbonate/ bicarbonate** are dispensed **SEPARATELY (in two bottles/
powders)** and **mixed by the patient immediately before drinking**, while effervescing.
⚠️ ⭐ **Never stopper an effervescing mixture tightly** — CO₂ pressure may **burst the bottle**.
⭐ Label: *"Mix the two solutions and drink while effervescing"*, *"Do not stopper tightly"*,
*"Keep in a cool place"*.

---

## 12.6 ⭐ OFFICIAL MIXTURES & THEIR TYPE (quick table)

| Mixture | ⭐ Type | ⭐ Dose |
|---|---|---|
| **Mistura Carminativa (Carminative Mixture)** | Solution | 10–20 mL |
| ⭐ **Mistura Cretae (Chalk Mixture)** | ⭐ **DIFFUSIBLE suspension** | 15–30 mL |
| ⭐ **Magnesium Trisilicate Mixture** | Suspension (with compound tragacanth powder) | 10–20 mL |
| **Kaolin Mixture** | Suspension | 10–20 mL |
| **Ammonium Chloride Mixture** | Solution (expectorant) | 10–20 mL |
| **Potassium Citrate Mixture** | Solution (urinary alkaliniser) | 10–20 mL |
| **Sodium Salicylate Mixture** | Solution | 10–20 mL |
| **Mistura Alba (White Mixture)** | Suspension (Mg sulphate + Mg carbonate) | 10–20 mL |
| **Compound Senna Mixture ("Black Draught")** | Solution (purgative) | 30 mL |
| **Liquid Paraffin Emulsion** | ⭐ **Emulsion** | 10–30 mL |
| **Castor Oil Emulsion** | Emulsion | 30 mL |
| **Milk of Magnesia (Magnesium Hydroxide Mixture)** | Magma/ suspension | 5–15 mL |
| **Iron Mixture (Ferrous sulphate)** | Solution | 10–15 mL |
| **Gentian Mixture (Alkaline/ Acid)** | Solution (bitter tonic) | 10–15 mL |

---

## 12.7 ⚠️ FAULTS IN MIXTURES & THEIR PREVENTION

| Fault | Cause | ⭐ Prevention |
|---|---|---|
| ⭐ **Caking of the sediment** | Deflocculated, indiffusible powder without a suspending agent | ⭐ **Add a suspending agent; aim for CONTROLLED FLOCCULATION** |
| ⭐ **Lumps of undispersed gum** | Water added to the gum all at once | ⭐ **Mix the gum DRY with the powder first, then add the vehicle GRADUALLY** |
| ⭐ **Sticky resinous lumps** | Vehicle added to the tincture, or the tincture added too fast | ⭐ **Add the tincture SLOWLY to the BULK of the vehicle with constant stirring** |
| **Gritty/ coarse suspension** | Powder not finely enough divided | **Reduce to a fine powder & levigate** |
| **Under-dose/ short volume** | Mortar & measure not rinsed into the bottle | ⭐ **Always rinse & add the rinsings; make up to volume** |
| **Uneven dosing** | Rapid settling; patient does not shake | ⭐ **Suspending agent + "SHAKE WELL" label + counselling** |
| **Cracked/ separated emulsion** | Wrong oil : water : gum ratio; alcohol/ electrolyte added; heat | ⭐ **Correct ratio (4:2:1 / 3:2:1 / 2:2:1 / 1:2:1); add alcohol last, diluted; avoid heat** |
| **Microbial spoilage/ mould** | Aqueous vehicle, no preservative, long storage | ⭐ **Chloroform water/ preservative; short expiry (7–14 days); cool storage** |
| **Bottle bursting** | Effervescence in a tightly stoppered bottle | ⭐ **Do not stopper tightly; dispense the acid & carbonate separately** |
| **Colour/ odour change, precipitate on standing** | Chemical incompatibility (Ch. 8) | ⭐ **Detect before compounding; correct or refer to the prescriber** |
| **Crystal growth on storage** | Temperature cycling of a nearly saturated solution | Avoid saturation; store at a steady cool temperature |

---

## 12.8 CHAPTER 12 — RAPID REVISION SHEET

* ⭐ **MIXTURE = liquid ORAL preparation, drug dissolved/ suspended/ dispersed in an AQUEOUS vehicle,
  taken in DIVIDED DOSES** (10–15 mL); **plain bottle + WHITE label**;
  shelf-life **7–14 days**.
* ⭐ **DRAUGHT = a SINGLE 50 mL dose.**
* ⭐ **Types: true solution · diffusible suspension · indiffusible suspension · emulsion ·
  effervescent · colloidal/mucilage.**
* ⭐ **DIFFUSIBLE (no suspending agent): light & heavy MAGNESIUM CARBONATE, MAGNESIUM OXIDE,
  MAGNESIUM TRISILICATE, CHALK, LIGHT KAOLIN, BISMUTH carbonate/ subnitrate/ subgallate.**
* ⭐ **INDIFFUSIBLE (suspending agent NEEDED): ASPIRIN, PHENOBARBITONE, SULPHUR, CALAMINE,
  ZINC OXIDE, HEAVY KAOLIN, salol, sulphonamides, resins.**
* ⚠️ ⭐ **LIGHT kaolin = diffusible; HEAVY kaolin = indiffusible.**
* ⭐ **The official suspending agent in dispensing = COMPOUND TRAGACANTH POWDER, 2 g per 100 mL
  (2 %)** = Tragacanth 15 + Acacia 20 + Starch 20 + Sucrose 45.
* ⭐ **A suspending agent works by INCREASING the VISCOSITY of the vehicle (Stokes' law).**
* ⭐ **FLOCCULATED = fluffy, large-volume sediment, settles fast, re-disperses easily, NO caking
  (desirable). DEFLOCCULATED = slow settling but a HARD CAKE that will not re-disperse
  (undesirable).** **Sedimentation volume F = Vu/Vo; the closer to 1, the better.**
* ⭐⭐ **THE GENERAL RULES / ORDER OF PREPARATION:**
  **(1) calculate & check the dose · (2) dissolve SOLUBLE SOLIDS in part of the vehicle ·
  (3) add the miscible LIQUIDS (syrups, glycerin, liquid extracts) ·
  (4) mix the INDIFFUSIBLE POWDER with the SUSPENDING AGENT DRY, levigate to a smooth paste, then
  dilute GRADUALLY · (5) add TINCTURES & resinous/ alcoholic liquids SLOWLY, LAST, into the BULK of
  the vehicle with constant stirring · (6) add volatiles & flavours near the end ·
  (7) MAKE UP TO VOLUME, rinsing the mortar & measure into the bottle · (8) shake, label
  ("SHAKE WELL"), final check.**
* ⚠️ ⭐ **NEVER add water to a gum all at once (lumps); NEVER add the vehicle to a tincture
  (sticky resin lumps); NEVER heat volatiles; NEVER forget the rinsings; NEVER omit "Shake well"
  from a suspension/ emulsion.**
* ⭐ **CHLOROFORM WATER (0.25 % v/v) is the commonest vehicle — it is the PRESERVATIVE and a mild
  flavour/ carminative.** Double-strength = 0.5 %, diluted 1 : 1.
* ⭐ **Emulsion ratios (oil : water : gum): fixed oil 4 : 2 : 1 · mineral oil 3 : 2 : 1 ·
  volatile oil 2 : 2 : 1 · oleoresin 1 : 2 : 1.** ⭐ The **primary emulsion is complete when a
  "crackling"/ clicking sound** is heard (dry gum/ Continental method).
* ⭐ **Effervescent mixtures: dispense the acid & the carbonate SEPARATELY; "mix and drink while
  effervescing"; DO NOT STOPPER TIGHTLY.**
* ⭐ **Chalk Mixture = the classic DIFFUSIBLE suspension; Magnesium Trisilicate Mixture & Kaolin
  Mixture use compound tragacanth powder; Liquid Paraffin Emulsion = the classic emulsion mixture.**
* ⭐ **"Shake well before use" goes on EVERY suspension & emulsion — but NOT on a true solution.**

# CHAPTER 13
# 🏪 ORDINARY BAZAAR MEDICINES
### Their RECOGNITION, DOSES and USES

---

## 13.1 WHAT ARE "BAZAAR MEDICINES"? ⭐

📌 **DEFINITION**
> **"Ordinary bazaar medicines" are the simple, cheap, commonly available drugs and chemicals that
> are sold in the open market (bazaar) and are stocked in every dispensary and household — chiefly
> inorganic salts, simple organic chemicals, oils, and common crude drugs — which a pharmacist must
> be able to RECOGNISE on sight (by their organoleptic characters and simple tests), and whose
> DOSES and USES he must know.**

⭐ **WHY THIS TOPIC EXISTS IN THE SYLLABUS**
* These drugs are handled **every day** in a dispensary, often supplied **loose/ unlabelled** from
  the bazaar.
* ⚠️ Many of them are **look-alikes** (white crystalline powders) and **confusing them can be
  fatal** — e.g. **boric acid vs oxalic acid**, **Epsom salt vs oxalic acid**, **sodium bicarbonate
  vs sodium nitrite**, **alum vs sugar**.
* ⭐ Therefore the pharmacist must be able to **identify them by RECOGNITION (appearance, taste,
  odour, solubility, behaviour on heating)** and by **simple confirmatory tests**.

### ⭐⭐ HOW TO "RECOGNISE" A BAZAAR MEDICINE — the systematic scheme

| # | Step | What to observe |
|---|---|---|
| **1** | ⭐ **Physical state & form** | Powder / crystal / granule / lump / liquid / semisolid; crystalline shape (cubic, needle, prismatic, flaky, amorphous) |
| **2** | ⭐ **Colour** | White, coloured, lustrous, metallic |
| **3** | ⭐ **Odour** | Odourless / aromatic / pungent / ammoniacal / garlic-like / phenolic |
| **4** | ⭐ **Taste** *(⚠️ ONLY where safe — NEVER taste an unknown/ poisonous substance)* | Saline, bitter, sweet, astringent, sour, acrid, metallic, tasteless |
| **5** | ⭐ **Touch/ feel** | Smooth & soapy (talc), gritty, greasy, unctuous, adheres to the tongue (kaolin) |
| **6** | ⭐ **Solubility** | In **cold water, hot water, alcohol, ether, dilute acid, alkali** |
| **7** | ⭐ **Behaviour in air** | ⭐ **Efflorescent** (Na₂CO₃, FeSO₄, MgSO₄, alum) · **Deliquescent** (CaCl₂, ZnCl₂) · **hygroscopic** · **volatile/ sublimable** (camphor, menthol, iodine, NH₄Cl) |
| **8** | ⭐ **Behaviour on HEATING** | ⭐ **Sublimes** (camphor, iodine, NH₄Cl, benzoic acid) · **melts** · **chars/ smells of caramel** (sugars, gums) · **burns with a sooty flame** (resins) · **effervesces/ gives CO₂** (carbonates) · **evolves ammonia** (ammonium salts) · **swells & puffs up** (alum, borax → "burnt alum", "borax bead") · **leaves a residue** |
| **9** | ⭐ **Behaviour with DILUTE ACID** | ⭐ **Effervescence (CO₂) → CARBONATE/ BICARBONATE** · **rotten-egg smell (H₂S) → sulphide** · **brown fumes → nitrite** · **vinegar smell → acetate** |
| **10** | ⭐ **Reaction to litmus/ pH** | Acidic / alkaline / neutral |
| **11** | ⭐ **Flame test (cation)** | ⭐ **Sodium — golden yellow · Potassium — lilac/ violet · Calcium — brick red · Barium — apple green · Copper — blue-green · Strontium — crimson · Lead/ Zinc — bluish-white** |
| **12** | ⭐ **Confirmatory chemical test** | See the individual monographs below |

🧠 **MEMORY HOOK — "S-C-O-T-T-S: State, Colour, Odour, Taste, Touch, Solubility"** —
then **heat it, acidify it, and flame-test it.**

### ⭐⭐ THE MOST USEFUL "GROUP" IDENTIFICATION TESTS 🧪

| Radical/ group | 🧪 TEST | ⭐ Positive result |
|---|---|---|
| ⭐ **Carbonate/ Bicarbonate (CO₃²⁻/ HCO₃⁻)** | Add dilute HCl | ⭐ **Brisk EFFERVESCENCE — CO₂ turns lime water milky** |
| ⭐ **Sulphate (SO₄²⁻)** | Add **barium chloride** solution | ⭐ **WHITE precipitate, insoluble in HCl** |
| ⭐ **Chloride (Cl⁻)** | Add **silver nitrate** + dilute HNO₃ | ⭐ **WHITE curdy precipitate, soluble in ammonia** |
| ⭐ **Iodide (I⁻)** | Silver nitrate / chlorine water + starch | ⭐ **Yellow precipitate / BLUE colour with starch** |
| ⭐ **Bromide (Br⁻)** | Silver nitrate | Pale yellow precipitate |
| ⭐ **Nitrate (NO₃⁻)** | ⭐ **Brown-ring test** (FeSO₄ + conc. H₂SO₄) | ⭐ **BROWN RING at the junction** |
| ⭐ **Nitrite (NO₂⁻)** | Dilute acid | **Brown fumes (NO₂)**; starch-iodide paper turns blue |
| ⭐ **Borate (BO₃³⁻)** | ⭐ **Turmeric paper** moistened with the acidified solution | ⭐ **Turmeric paper turns REDDISH-BROWN, and GREEN-BLACK on adding alkali** |
| **Borate (2nd test)** | Burn with alcohol + conc. H₂SO₄ | ⭐ **GREEN-EDGED flame** |
| ⭐ **Phosphate (PO₄³⁻)** | Ammonium molybdate + HNO₃ | Canary-yellow precipitate |
| ⭐ **Acetate (CH₃COO⁻)** | Warm with dilute H₂SO₄ | **Smell of VINEGAR**; FeCl₃ → deep red |
| ⭐ **Sulphide (S²⁻)** | Dilute HCl | ⭐ **Rotten-egg smell (H₂S)**; blackens lead acetate paper |
| ⭐ **Ammonium (NH₄⁺)** | Warm with NaOH | ⭐ **Pungent AMMONIA gas — turns red litmus blue** |
| ⭐ **Ferrous (Fe²⁺)** | Potassium ferricyanide | ⭐ **Deep BLUE precipitate (Prussian blue)** |
| ⭐ **Ferric (Fe³⁺)** | Potassium thiocyanate / ferrocyanide | ⭐ **BLOOD-RED colour / blue precipitate** |
| ⭐ **Zinc (Zn²⁺)** | NaOH (excess) | White precipitate, **soluble in excess** |
| ⭐ **Aluminium (Al³⁺)** | NaOH (excess) | Gelatinous white precipitate, **soluble in excess** |
| ⭐ **Calcium (Ca²⁺)** | Ammonium oxalate | White precipitate; **brick-red flame** |
| ⭐ **Magnesium (Mg²⁺)** | NaOH | White precipitate, **insoluble in excess** |
| ⭐ **Copper (Cu²⁺)** | Ammonia | **Deep blue** solution |
| ⭐ **Lead (Pb²⁺)** | Potassium iodide/ dilute HCl | Yellow PbI₂ / white PbCl₂ |
| ⭐ **Starch** | Iodine | ⭐ **BLUE-BLACK** |
| ⭐ **Reducing sugar** | Fehling's/ Benedict's | Brick-red precipitate |
| ⭐ **Salicylate/ Phenol** | Ferric chloride | ⭐ **VIOLET/ purple colour** |
| ⭐ **Tannin** | Ferric chloride / gelatin | Blue-black / white precipitate |
| ⭐ **Oxidising agent (permanganate, chlorate, peroxide)** | KI–starch paper | Blue colour |

⚠️ ⭐ **DANGEROUS LOOK-ALIKES — THE "WHITE POWDER" TRAP TABLE**

| Look-alike pair | ⭐ How to tell them apart |
|---|---|
| ⭐ **Boric acid vs Oxalic acid** *(both white crystalline; ⚠️ oxalic acid is a POISON)* | ⭐ **Boric acid: turmeric paper → reddish-brown; green flame with alcohol; feels SOAPY/ slippery; taste faintly bitter.** ⭐ **Oxalic acid: STRONGLY ACID to litmus, sour, gives a WHITE precipitate with calcium chloride (calcium oxalate, insoluble in acetic acid); burns without residue** |
| ⭐ **Epsom salt (MgSO₄) vs Oxalic acid** ⚠️ *the classic fatal confusion* | ⭐ **Epsom salt: NEUTRAL, saline-bitter taste, gives a white ppt with BaCl₂ (sulphate) and a white ppt with NaOH insoluble in excess (Mg).** ⭐ **Oxalic acid: strongly ACID, poisonous** |
| ⭐ **Sodium bicarbonate vs Sodium nitrite** ⚠️ | ⭐ **Bicarbonate EFFERVESCES with acid (CO₂).** ⭐ **Nitrite gives BROWN FUMES with acid** |
| ⭐ **Alum vs Sugar vs Magnesium sulphate** | ⭐ **Alum: sweetish-ASTRINGENT taste, PUFFS UP on heating ("burnt alum"), Al³⁺ + sulphate tests.** ⭐ **Sugar: sweet, CHARS with a caramel smell.** ⭐ **MgSO₄: bitter-saline** |
| ⭐ **Borax vs Boric acid** | ⭐ **Borax is ALKALINE to litmus (soapy, sweetish-alkaline) and swells to a glassy "borax bead" on heating; boric acid is faintly ACID** |
| ⭐ **Zinc oxide vs Calamine** | ⭐ **Zinc oxide is WHITE; calamine is PINK (zinc oxide coloured with ferric oxide)** |
| ⭐ **Light vs Heavy magnesium carbonate/ oxide** | ⭐ **Same chemical — differ only in BULK DENSITY (the "light" grade occupies a much larger volume for the same weight)** |
| ⭐ **Precipitated vs Sublimed sulphur** | ⭐ **Precipitated ("milk of sulphur") is a very fine, pale, almost odourless powder; sublimed ("flowers of sulphur") is a coarser, gritty, bright-yellow crystalline powder** |
| **Talc vs Kaolin vs Starch** | ⭐ **Talc: smooth, soapy, gritty-free, does NOT adhere to the tongue.** **Kaolin: earthy, ADHERES to the tongue, earthy smell when damp.** **Starch: iodine → BLUE-BLACK** |
| **Potassium permanganate vs Iodine** | ⭐ **KMnO₄: dark purple crystals, dissolves in water giving a PURPLE solution.** **Iodine: greyish-black LUSTROUS scales, SUBLIMES giving violet vapour, blue with starch, barely soluble in water but soluble in KI solution** |

---

## 13.2 ⭐⭐ MASTER MONOGRAPH TABLES OF BAZAAR MEDICINES

> ⚠️ **All doses are ADULT ORAL doses unless marked "EXTERNAL".**
> ⭐ Learn each row as: **Name → Synonym → Recognition → Dose → Use.**

### 📋 TABLE A — SODIUM & POTASSIUM COMPOUNDS ⭐

| Drug (synonym) | ⭐ RECOGNITION | ⭐ DOSE | ⭐ USES |
|---|---|---|---|
| ⭐ **Sodium bicarbonate** (Baking soda, *Meetha soda*, Bicarb) | White crystalline powder; **saline-alkaline taste**; ⭐ **EFFERVESCES briskly with acid**; alkaline to litmus; sodium → **golden-yellow flame**; soluble in water, insoluble in alcohol | ⭐ **1 – 5 g** | ⭐ **ANTACID**, urinary & systemic alkaliniser, carminative; in **effervescent powders & ORS**; **5 % ear drops to soften wax**; mouthwash; ⚠️ excess → alkalosis, "acid rebound", CO₂ distension |
| ⭐ **Sodium chloride** (Common salt, *Namak*, Saline) | White cubic crystals; ⭐ **purely SALINE taste**; ⭐ **white curdy ppt with AgNO₃**; golden-yellow flame | 1 – 5 g (as required); ⭐ **isotonic saline = 0.9 % w/v** | ⭐ **Electrolyte replacement, ORS, isotonic vehicle, IV infusion, gargle/ mouthwash, nasal wash, emetic (large dose in warm water)** |
| ⭐ **Sodium carbonate** (Washing soda, Soda ash, *Dhone ka soda*) | Translucent crystals; ⭐ **EFFLORESCENT (Na₂CO₃·10H₂O)**; strongly **alkaline**, soapy taste; effervesces with acid | ⚠️ **EXTERNAL/ not for internal use** (obsolete internal 0.3–1 g) | Water softener, cleansing, **detergent**, ⭐ **used to prepare "exsiccated sodium carbonate"**; ⚠️ caustic |
| ⭐ **Sodium sulphate** (Glauber's salt) | Large colourless **EFFLORESCENT** crystals; **bitter saline**; ⭐ **BaCl₂ → white ppt (sulphate)** | ⭐ **8 – 16 g** | ⭐ **SALINE PURGATIVE (cathartic)** |
| ⭐ **Sodium nitrite** ⚠️ | White/ faintly yellow crystals; ⭐ **BROWN FUMES with dilute acid**; ⚠️ **look-alike of NaHCO₃** | 30 – 120 mg | Vasodilator (obsolete); ⭐ **ANTIDOTE in CYANIDE poisoning** (with sodium thiosulphate) |
| ⭐ **Sodium salicylate** | White crystals/ powder; ⭐ **FeCl₃ → VIOLET**; sweetish-saline taste | **0.6 – 2 g** | Analgesic, antipyretic, **antirheumatic** |
| ⭐ **Sodium benzoate** | White powder; sweetish-saline; FeCl₃ → **buff/ flesh-coloured ppt** | 0.5 – 2 g | ⭐ **PRESERVATIVE (0.1–0.2 %)**, antifungal, expectorant; **caffeine solubiliser** |
| ⭐ **Sodium citrate** | White granular crystals; saline; effervesces with strong acid | **0.5 – 4 g** | ⭐ **Urinary alkaliniser, expectorant, ANTICOAGULANT for stored blood, in ORS**, diaphoretic |
| ⭐ **Sodium thiosulphate** ("Hypo") | Large colourless crystals; cooling saline taste; ⭐ **decolourises iodine**; with acid → S + SO₂ | 5 – 15 g | ⭐ **ANTIDOTE in CYANIDE poisoning**; photographic fixer; antifungal (20 % lotion for tinea versicolor) |
| ⭐ **Sodium phosphate** | Colourless efflorescent crystals; saline; alkaline | 4 – 8 g | Saline purgative |
| ⭐ **Sodium fluoride** | White powder | 0.5–1 mg fluoride/day; **0.05 % mouthwash** | ⭐ **Dental caries prevention** ⚠️ toxic in excess (fluorosis) |
| ⭐ **Potassium permanganate** ⚠️ (*Lal dawa*, Condy's crystals) | ⭐ **Dark PURPLE, almost black, LUSTROUS prismatic crystals**; ⭐ dissolves in water to a **deep purple/ pink** solution; ⭐ **powerful OXIDISING agent — decolourised by reducing agents** | ⚠️ ⭐ **EXTERNAL ONLY — 1 : 5000 to 1 : 10 000** (a pale pink solution) | ⭐ **ANTISEPTIC, DEODORANT, astringent — wet dressings, bathing weeping eczema & wounds, gargle, douche, snake-bite (classical), disinfecting water, "sitz bath"**; ⚠️ ⭐ **EXPLOSIVE with glycerin, sugar, charcoal, sulphur & organic matter — NEVER TRITURATE**; ⚠️ stains skin & clothes brown |
| ⭐ **Potassium chlorate** ⚠️ | White crystals; ⭐ **strong oxidiser; DEFLAGRATES/ explodes when heated with organic matter** | 0.3 – 1 g (⚠️ mainly **gargle/ lozenge**) | Antiseptic **gargle & lozenge** for sore mouth/ throat; ⚠️ **toxic — methaemoglobinaemia; explosion hazard** |
| ⭐ **Potassium citrate** | White granular powder/ crystals; saline; deliquescent | ⭐ **1 – 2 g** | ⭐ **URINARY ALKALINISER (cystitis, dysuria), diuretic, expectorant** |
| ⭐ **Potassium iodide** | Colourless cubic crystals; ⭐ **saline-bitter**; ⭐ **AgNO₃ → yellow ppt; with chlorine water + starch → BLUE**; slightly hygroscopic | ⭐ **250 – 500 mg** (up to 1 g) | ⭐ **EXPECTORANT; dissolves IODINE in water (Lugol's & weak iodine solution); goitre & thyrotoxicosis prophylaxis; antifungal (sporotrichosis)** |
| ⭐ **Potassium bromide** | Colourless crystals; saline-bitter; AgNO₃ → pale yellow ppt | 0.6 – 2 g | **Sedative & anticonvulsant** (obsolete); ⚠️ **bromism on cumulation** |
| ⭐ **Potassium nitrate** (Saltpetre, *Shora*) | Colourless prismatic crystals; **cooling saline** taste; ⭐ **brown-ring test (nitrate)**; deflagrates on charcoal | 1 – 2 g | ⭐ **REFRIGERANT & diuretic**; in **asthma powders/ papers** (burnt & fumes inhaled); toothpaste for sensitivity |
| ⭐ **Potassium tartrate/ bitartrate** (Cream of tartar) | White gritty crystalline powder; **acidic** | 1 – 4 g | Saline purgative, diuretic; in **effervescent & baking powders** |

### 📋 TABLE B — MAGNESIUM, CALCIUM & ALUMINIUM COMPOUNDS ⭐

| Drug (synonym) | ⭐ RECOGNITION | ⭐ DOSE | ⭐ USES |
|---|---|---|---|
| ⭐⭐ **Magnesium sulphate** (**EPSOM SALT**, *Sendha/ Kadwa namak*) | ⭐ **Colourless prismatic EFFLORESCENT crystals**; ⭐ **BITTER-SALINE taste**; ⭐ **BaCl₂ → white ppt (sulphate)**; NaOH → white ppt insoluble in excess (Mg); freely water-soluble | ⭐ **5 – 15 g** in a tumblerful of water, on an empty stomach | ⭐ **SALINE (osmotic) PURGATIVE — the classic; CHOLAGOGUE; injection = ANTICONVULSANT in ECLAMPSIA & for hypomagnesaemia; ⭐ Magnesium Sulphate PASTE (Morison's) for BOILS & carbuncles**; foot soak. ⚠️ ⭐ **Fatally confused with OXALIC ACID** |
| ⭐ **Magnesium carbonate (Light & Heavy)** | White, very **light, bulky** (light) or dense (heavy) odourless powder; **earthy, slightly alkaline** taste; ⭐ **effervesces with acid**; ⭐ **DIFFUSIBLE in water** | ⭐ **0.3 – 2 g** (up to 5 g) | ⭐ **ANTACID & mild laxative; ⭐ ABSORBENT/ DISPERSING AGENT for eutectics & volatile oils (inhalations)**; dusting powder |
| ⭐ **Magnesium oxide (Light & Heavy)** (**Calcined magnesia**) | White very light amorphous powder; **alkaline earthy taste**; ⭐ **does NOT effervesce with acid** (distinguishes it from the carbonate); slowly absorbs CO₂ & water | ⭐ **0.25 – 1 g** (up to 4 g) | ⭐ **ANTACID (non-effervescent), laxative; absorbent; ⭐ ingredient of the UNIVERSAL ANTIDOTE** |
| ⭐ **Magnesium hydroxide** (Milk of magnesia = the mixture) | White bulky powder / white **magma (suspension)** | Powder 0.3–1 g; ⭐ **Mixture 5 – 15 mL** | ⭐ **ANTACID + LAXATIVE** |
| ⭐ **Magnesium trisilicate** | White, **light, bulky, tasteless, odourless** powder; ⭐ **DIFFUSIBLE**; does not effervesce; reacts slowly with acid forming silicic acid gel | ⭐ **0.5 – 2 g** | ⭐ **ANTACID with a slow, prolonged action + ADSORBENT/ protective gel** (silicic acid coats the ulcer) |
| ⭐ **Calcium carbonate** (Precipitated chalk, *Chuna/ Khariya*, Prepared chalk) | White, odourless, tasteless fine powder; ⭐ **EFFERVESCES with acid**; ⭐ **brick-red flame (Ca)**; insoluble in water | ⭐ **1 – 5 g** | ⭐ **ANTACID, calcium supplement, mild astringent/ antidiarrhoeal (Chalk Mixture), DENTIFRICE, dusting powder, tablet diluent** |
| ⭐ **Calcium hydroxide / Lime water** (Slaked lime, *Chuna*; **Lime water = Liquor Calcis**) | White powder / ⭐ **clear alkaline "lime water" (a saturated solution, ~0.15 % w/v)**; ⭐ turns **milky with CO₂** | ⚠️ Lime water is used chiefly **externally/ as a vehicle** | ⭐ **Antacid; ⭐ LIME LINIMENT (CARRON OIL) = LIME WATER + OLIVE OIL in EQUAL PARTS — a classic BURN dressing (the emulgent is calcium oleate, formed *in situ*)**; astringent lotion |
| ⭐ **Calcium sulphate** (**PLASTER OF PARIS**, *Gypsum*) | White powder; ⭐ **sets to a hard mass when mixed with water** | External | ⭐ **PLASTER CASTS/ immobilising bandages, dental impressions, tablet diluent** |
| ⭐ **Calcium chloride** | White ⭐ **DELIQUESCENT** granules/ fused masses; bitter-saline; very soluble, gives out heat on dissolving | 0.3 – 2 g; **10 % injection IV** | Calcium replacement (tetany, hypocalcaemia, antidote to Mg toxicity); ⭐ **DESICCANT/ drying agent** |
| ⭐ **Calamine** | ⭐ **PINK, very fine, tasteless amorphous powder** (= zinc oxide with a little **ferric oxide** for colour); insoluble in water, soluble in dilute acid | ⭐ **EXTERNAL** | ⭐ **SOOTHING, protective, mildly astringent & ANTIPRURITIC — the classic CALAMINE LOTION (calamine + zinc oxide + bentonite + glycerin + rose water) for prickly heat, sunburn, chickenpox, urticaria & eczema**; ⭐ **INDIFFUSIBLE — needs a suspending agent** |
| ⭐ **Zinc oxide** (*Safeda/ Jasta phool*) | ⭐ **WHITE (or faintly yellowish) very fine amorphous powder**; tasteless & odourless; ⭐ **turns YELLOW on heating and white again on cooling**; insoluble in water, soluble in acids & alkalis (amphoteric) | ⭐ **EXTERNAL** | ⭐ **PROTECTIVE, ASTRINGENT, mild ANTISEPTIC & SOOTHING — Zinc Ointment (15 %), Zinc Paste, Zinc & Castor Oil Ointment (nappy rash), calamine lotion, dusting powders, zinc oxide adhesive PLASTER, sunscreen**; ⭐ **DV = 4.7**; ⭐ **INDIFFUSIBLE** |
| ⭐ **Zinc sulphate** (White vitriol) | Colourless efflorescent crystals; ⭐ **astringent, metallic taste**; BaCl₂ → white ppt; NaOH → white ppt **soluble in excess** (Zn) | ⭐ **Emetic 0.6 – 2 g**; **0.25–1 % eye/ ear drops** | ⭐ **ASTRINGENT (eye lotion, ear drops), EMETIC in poisoning, zinc supplement (diarrhoea in children)** |
| ⭐ **Aluminium hydroxide gel** | White viscous **gel/ suspension**; tasteless | ⭐ **5 – 10 mL** (gel) / 0.5–1 g dried | ⭐ **ANTACID (non-systemic), phosphate binder**; ⚠️ **constipating** (hence combined with magnesium salts) |
| ⭐⭐ **Alum** (Potash alum, *Phitkari*; Ammonium & potassium aluminium sulphate) | ⭐ **Large colourless transparent OCTAHEDRAL crystals or white powder**; ⭐ **sweetish-ASTRINGENT (puckering) taste**; ⭐ **EFFLORESCENT**; ⭐ **on heating it SWELLS & PUFFS UP into a light porous mass ("BURNT/ DRIED ALUM")**; gives **Al³⁺ & sulphate** tests | ⭐ **Externally: 0.5 – 2 % solution** as a lotion/ gargle; internal (emetic/ astringent) 0.3–1 g | ⭐ **ASTRINGENT & STYPTIC/ HAEMOSTATIC — the classic "shaving styptic" for cuts; gargle & mouthwash for spongy gums & sore throat; douche; antiperspirant; ⭐ WATER PURIFICATION (coagulates suspended matter); hardening the skin; ⭐ "burnt alum" as a mild caustic/ escharotic** |
| ⭐ **Kaolin (Light & Heavy)** (China clay, *Multani mitti* type, Bolus alba) | ⭐ **Dull white/ yellowish-white soft, unctuous EARTHY powder**; ⭐ **EARTHY odour when damp**; ⭐ **ADHERES TO THE TONGUE**; insoluble in water & acids | ⭐ **2 – 6 g** (light kaolin) | ⭐ **ADSORBENT & ANTIDIARRHOEAL (Kaolin Mixture, kaolin + pectin); ⭐ KAOLIN POULTICE (applied HOT) for boils & inflammation; dusting powder; ⭐ FILTER AID** ⚠️ ⭐ **LIGHT kaolin is DIFFUSIBLE, HEAVY kaolin is INDIFFUSIBLE**; ⚠️ **adsorbs drugs → reduced absorption** |
| ⭐ **Talc** (French chalk, Purified talc, *Talcum*) | ⭐ **White or greyish-white, very fine, SMOOTH & SOAPY (unctuous) powder**; ⭐ **does NOT adhere to the tongue** (unlike kaolin); insoluble; tasteless | ⭐ **EXTERNAL** | ⭐ **DUSTING POWDER (protective, lubricant, anti-friction), tablet glidant/ lubricant, ⭐ FILTER AID & CLARIFYING/ DISTRIBUTING AGENT (for volatile oils in aromatic waters)**; ⚠️ ⭐ **must be STERILE for wounds (risk of granuloma & tetanus spores)** |
| ⭐ **Bentonite** | Pale buff, very fine powder; ⭐ **SWELLS to about 12 times its volume in water forming a gel**; earthy | External/ excipient | ⭐ **SUSPENDING & THICKENING agent, filter aid, base of lotions & pastes** |
| ⭐ **Barium sulphate** | Heavy, white, fine, tasteless powder; ⭐ **INSOLUBLE in water & acids (hence non-toxic)** | 300 g in suspension | ⭐ **RADIO-OPAQUE (X-ray) CONTRAST MEDIUM — barium meal/ enema**; ⚠️ ⭐ **soluble barium salts are POISONOUS** |

---


### 📋 TABLE C — BORON, IODINE, SULPHUR & OTHER ANTISEPTIC CHEMICALS ⭐

| Drug (synonym) | ⭐ RECOGNITION | ⭐ DOSE | ⭐ USES |
|---|---|---|---|
| ⭐⭐ **Boric acid** (Boracic acid, *Boric powder*) | ⭐ **White, odourless, glistening SCALES/ crystals or white powder**; ⭐ feels **SOAPY/ unctuous** to the touch; faintly bitter; ⭐ **FAINTLY ACID to litmus**; ⭐ **turmeric paper → REDDISH-BROWN (→ green-black with alkali)**; ⭐ **burns with a GREEN-EDGED flame with alcohol + H₂SO₄**; soluble 1 in 20 water, freely in glycerin | ⚠️ ⭐ **EXTERNAL ONLY** — **2 – 4 % solution**; ⭐ **isotonic at 1.9 %** | ⭐ **MILD ANTISEPTIC, ASTRINGENT & bacteriostatic — eye lotions & eye drops (⭐ 1.9 % is ISOTONIC and is the vehicle of choice for eye drops of ALKALOIDAL, SILVER & ZINC salts, which sodium chloride would precipitate); dusting powders; ear drops (in alcohol); Boric Acid Ointment; mouthwash; preservative** ⚠️ ⭐ **TOXIC IF ABSORBED — NEVER use on large raw areas, on nappy rash or in INFANTS (fatal poisoning has occurred); not for internal use** |
| ⭐⭐ **Borax** (Sodium borate/ tetraborate, *Suhaga*, Sodium biborate) | ⭐ **Colourless transparent crystals or white powder**; ⭐ **EFFLORESCENT**; ⭐ **sweetish-ALKALINE, soapy taste**; ⭐ **ALKALINE to litmus** (⭐ *this distinguishes it from boric acid*); ⭐ **on heating it SWELLS, froths and fuses to a clear glassy "BORAX BEAD"**; turmeric & green-flame tests positive; soluble 1 in 16 water, freely in glycerin | ⚠️ ⭐ **EXTERNAL ONLY** (internal obsolete 0.3–1 g) | ⭐ **ANTISEPTIC, astringent, detergent & deodorant — mouthwash & gargle; ⭐ HONEY/ GLYCERIN OF BORAX for ORAL THRUSH (candidiasis) in infants & sore mouth; eye lotion; ⭐ water softener; buffer (borate buffer for eye drops); preservative; cleansing agent** ⚠️ Toxic internally |
| ⭐⭐ **Iodine** (*Aiodin*) | ⭐ **Heavy, greyish-black/ bluish-black LUSTROUS (metallic-shining) plates, scales or crystals**; ⭐ **characteristic PUNGENT, irritating odour**; ⭐ **SUBLIMES readily giving a VIOLET vapour**; ⭐ **BLUE-BLACK colour with STARCH**; ⚠️ **only slightly soluble in water (1 in 2500) but FREELY SOLUBLE in a solution of POTASSIUM IODIDE, in alcohol, chloroform & ether**; stains the skin brown | ⭐ **Weak Iodine Solution (Tincture of Iodine) = Iodine 2.5 % + KI 2.5 % in 90 % alcohol → EXTERNAL antiseptic.** ⭐ **Strong Iodine Solution (LUGOL'S) = Iodine 5 % + KI 10 % in water → 0.1 – 0.3 mL well diluted, ORAL** | ⭐ **POWERFUL ANTISEPTIC & germicide (skin preparation before surgery, cuts & wounds), counter-irritant (iodine paint/ ointment), ⭐ Lugol's solution in THYROTOXICOSIS (pre-operative) & goitre, expectorant, test reagent for starch** ⚠️ ⭐ **ANTIDOTE for iodine poisoning = STARCH/ flour suspension**; ⚠️ stains; ⚠️ ⭐ **reacts with metal — use a HORN/ PLASTIC spatula & a GLASS mortar**; ⚠️ **explosive with ammonia** |
| ⭐ **Potassium iodide** | *(See Table A)* | 250–500 mg | ⭐ **Solubiliser for iodine**, expectorant |
| ⭐⭐ **Sulphur — Sublimed ("Flowers of sulphur", *Gandhak*) & Precipitated ("Milk of sulphur", *Lac sulphuris*)** | ⭐ **Bright/ pale YELLOW powder**; ⭐ **Sublimed = coarse, gritty, crystalline, faint characteristic odour; Precipitated = very fine, pale, almost odourless & tasteless**; ⭐ **INSOLUBLE in water (and hydrophobic — hence INDIFFUSIBLE)**, soluble in carbon disulphide; ⭐ **burns with a BLUE flame giving pungent, suffocating SO₂** | ⭐ **1 – 4 g** orally; ⭐ **Sulphur Ointment 10 %** externally | ⭐ **INTERNAL: mild LAXATIVE (with senna & liquorice — compound liquorice powder).** ⭐ **EXTERNAL: SCABICIDE (sulphur ointment for SCABIES — the classic, safe in infants & pregnancy), keratolytic & antiseborrhoeic (ACNE, dandruff, ringworm, seborrhoea)**; ⚠️ ⭐ **INDIFFUSIBLE — needs a suspending agent in a mixture; and a wetting agent for lotions** |
| ⭐ **Hydrogen peroxide solution** (*Peroxide*) | ⭐ **Colourless, odourless (or faintly ozone-like) LIQUID**; slightly acid; ⭐ **EFFERVESCES/ liberates OXYGEN vigorously on contact with tissue, blood, alkali or an oxidisable substance**; bleaches; ⚠️ **decomposes on standing, in light & heat** | ⭐ **EXTERNAL — 3 % (10 volume) or 6 % (20 volume)**; ⭐ mouthwash **diluted (1.5–3 %)** | ⭐ **ANTISEPTIC, DEODORANT & CLEANSING (the effervescence mechanically loosens pus, debris & slough) — foul wounds & ulcers, ⭐ especially ANAEROBIC infection; mouthwash/ gargle for gingivitis & sore mouth; ⭐ SOFTENING & REMOVING EAR WAX; bleaching hair** ⚠️ ⭐ **Store in an AMBER bottle, in a COOL place, with a VENTED closure (it decomposes releasing O₂ and may burst a tight bottle)**; ⚠️ strong solutions are caustic |
| ⭐ **Phenol** (Carbolic acid, *Phenyl*) | ⭐ **Colourless/ pink needle-shaped crystals or a colourless oily liquid ("liquefied phenol", ~80 %)**; ⭐ **characteristic strong PHENOLIC/ "carbolic" odour**; ⭐ **turns PINK on exposure to light & air**; ⭐ **FeCl₃ → VIOLET**; caustic to the skin; soluble 1 in 15 water, freely in alcohol & glycerin | ⚠️ ⭐ **EXTERNAL, 0.5 – 2 %** (⚠️ **1 : 20 = 5 % is a disinfectant strength**) | ⭐ **ANTISEPTIC & DISINFECTANT (the original "carbolic" — Lister); local ANAESTHETIC/ ANTIPRURITIC in low concentration (calamine & phenol lotion); ⭐ CAUSTIC/ ESCHAROTIC & nerve-destroying in strong solution (injection for haemorrhoids); preservative; ⭐ the STANDARD for the "PHENOL COEFFICIENT" of disinfectants** ⚠️ ⭐ **CAUSTIC & TOXIC — absorbed through the skin (kidney damage); ANTIDOTE = wash with alcohol, glycerin, or vegetable oil (NOT water alone); ⚠️ EUTECTIC with camphor, menthol & thymol; ⚠️ softens ointment bases; ⚠️ not on nappy areas or in infants** |
| ⭐ **Cresol / Lysol** | Colourless to brownish-yellow **oily liquid**; ⭐ **phenolic, tarry odour**; ⭐ **Lysol = cresol + soap solution (50 %)** | ⚠️ **EXTERNAL/ disinfectant, 1 – 2 %** | ⭐ **DISINFECTANT for floors, drains, excreta, utensils — 3–5 times more powerful than phenol**; ⚠️ **caustic, never on skin undiluted** |
| ⭐ **Camphor** (*Kapoor*) | ⭐ **Colourless/ white translucent CRYSTALLINE masses or tough, TRANSLUCENT lumps that are difficult to powder**; ⭐ **strong, penetrating, characteristic aromatic ODOUR**; ⭐ **pungent then COOLING taste**; ⭐ **SUBLIMES readily & is VOLATILE at room temperature**; ⭐ **floats on water & gyrates ("dances") on the surface**; almost insoluble in water, freely soluble in alcohol, ether, chloroform & fixed oils | **Internal (obsolete) 120–300 mg**; ⭐ mostly **EXTERNAL** | ⭐ **COUNTER-IRRITANT, RUBEFACIENT & mild local ANAESTHETIC/ ANTIPRURITIC — camphor liniment, camphorated oil, balms & chest rubs; carminative & mild expectorant; ⭐ INHALATION for nasal congestion; cardiac & respiratory stimulant (classical injection); preservative & insect repellent; moth balls** ⚠️ ⭐ **Powdered only by "PULVERISATION BY INTERVENTION" — with a few drops of ALCOHOL/ ether**; ⚠️ ⭐ **forms a EUTECTIC (liquefies) with MENTHOL, THYMOL, PHENOL & chloral hydrate**; ⚠️ **DV = 0.7**; ⚠️ toxic in overdose (convulsions) |
| ⭐ **Menthol** (*Pudina ka phool*, Peppermint camphor) | ⭐ **Colourless, acicular (needle-shaped) or prismatic crystals**; ⭐ **strong PEPPERMINT odour**; ⭐ **warm, then intensely COOLING taste**; ⭐ **sublimes/ volatile**; ⭐ **LAEVO-rotatory**; almost insoluble in water, freely soluble in alcohol & oils | Internal 15–60 mg; mostly **external** | ⭐ **CARMINATIVE, mild local ANAESTHETIC & ANTIPRURITIC (the COOLING sensation is due to stimulation of cold receptors), counter-irritant, ⭐ NASAL DECONGESTANT INHALATION (Menthol & Eucalyptus Inhalation), throat lozenges, balms, toothpaste & mouthwash flavour** ⚠️ ⭐ **EUTECTIC with camphor, thymol & phenol** |
| ⭐ **Thymol** | ⭐ **Colourless large crystals or white crystalline powder**; ⭐ **characteristic aromatic THYME-like odour**, pungent taste; volatile; almost insoluble in water, freely soluble in alcohol & oils | External/ gargle | ⭐ **ANTISEPTIC & antifungal (about 25× more powerful than phenol but far less irritant), DEODORANT — ⭐ compound GLYCERIN OF THYMOL mouthwash/ gargle, dusting powders, hookworm (classical), preservative** ⚠️ **Eutectic with camphor & menthol** |
| ⭐ **Formaldehyde solution (Formalin)** | ⭐ **Clear colourless liquid (~ 37 % HCHO)**; ⭐ **extremely PUNGENT, suffocating, irritating odour (lacrimatory)**; may deposit a white **paraformaldehyde** on standing in the cold | ⚠️ ⭐ **EXTERNAL/ disinfectant — 1 – 10 %** | ⭐ **DISINFECTANT & FUMIGANT (rooms, instruments), ⭐ PRESERVATIVE FOR ANATOMICAL SPECIMENS (10 %), hardening the skin (5–10 % for hyperhidrosis of the feet & to prevent bed-sores), antiseptic (plantar warts)** ⚠️ ⭐ **irritant, carcinogenic; neutralise fumes with AMMONIA** |
| ⭐ **Boro-glycerin / Glycerin of boric acid** | Clear viscous syrupy liquid | External | Mild antiseptic & soothing application, ear drops |
| ⭐ **Gentian violet (Crystal violet)** | ⭐ **Dark green/ purple metallic-lustrous powder or crystals**; gives an intense **violet** solution | ⭐ **EXTERNAL 0.5 – 1 % paint** | ⭐ **ANTISEPTIC & ANTIFUNGAL — oral thrush, impetigo, ringworm, burns**; bacteriological stain ⚠️ stains |
| ⭐ **Mercurochrome / Merbromin, Acriflavine, Proflavine** | Red/ orange-red or yellow powders giving coloured solutions | External 1–2 % | Antiseptic dyes for wounds |
| ⭐ **Bleaching powder** (Chlorinated lime, *Chuna-chlorine*) | ⭐ **Dull white powder with a strong CHLORINE odour**; ⭐ **liberates chlorine with acid**; deteriorates on storage | ⚠️ **External/ disinfectant** | ⭐ **DISINFECTANT — water purification, drains, excreta, ⭐ EUSOL (Edinburgh University Solution of Lime — chlorinated lime + boric acid) for wound irrigation**; bleaching |
| ⭐ **Sodium hypochlorite solution** (Dakin's/ household bleach) | Pale yellow liquid, chlorine odour | External | ⭐ **Wound cleansing (Dakin's solution), surface & water disinfection** |
| ⭐ **Copper sulphate** (Blue vitriol, *Neela thotha*) ⚠️ | ⭐ **BLUE triclinic crystals or blue crystalline powder**; ⭐ **metallic astringent taste**; ⭐ **turns WHITE on heating (loses water of crystallisation) and blue again on adding water**; ⭐ **ammonia → DEEP BLUE solution**; ⭐ **iron nail placed in the solution becomes copper-coated** | ⭐ **EMETIC 250 mg** (⚠️ obsolete & dangerous); external 0.1–1 % | ⭐ **ASTRINGENT, EMETIC (in phosphorus poisoning — classical), caustic/ escharotic for granulations & warts, antifungal (Bordeaux mixture), algicide, ⭐ reagent in Fehling's & Benedict's solutions** ⚠️ **POISONOUS internally** |
| ⭐ **Ferrous sulphate** (Green vitriol, *Hara kasees*) | ⭐ **Pale bluish-GREEN crystals or powder**; ⭐ **EFFLORESCENT and slowly OXIDISES/ turns BROWN-YELLOW on exposure to air**; ⭐ **saline, metallic, astringent (inky) taste**; ⭐ **potassium ferricyanide → deep BLUE ppt (Fe²⁺)** | ⭐ **200 mg t.d.s.** (≈ 60 mg elemental iron) | ⭐ **HAEMATINIC — the classic treatment of IRON-DEFICIENCY ANAEMIA** (⭐ take **after food** to reduce gastric irritation; ⚠️ blackens the stools & may stain the teeth — give a liquid preparation with a straw) ⚠️ ⭐ **use the EXSICCATED form in powders (it is efflorescent); brown-ring test for nitrate uses it; ⚠️ FATAL in children on overdose** |
| ⭐ **Silver nitrate** (Lunar caustic) ⚠️ | ⭐ **Colourless transparent crystals or white crystalline powder**; ⭐ **turns GREY/ BLACK on exposure to LIGHT & organic matter**; ⭐ **with chloride → white curdy AgCl**; caustic | ⭐ **EXTERNAL 0.5 – 2 %; the solid stick as a caustic** | ⭐ **ANTISEPTIC, ASTRINGENT & CAUSTIC/ ESCHAROTIC — warts, granulation tissue, aphthous ulcers, umbilical granuloma; 1 % eye drops for OPHTHALMIA NEONATORUM (Credé's method, classical); silver nitrate stick** ⚠️ ⭐ **stains the skin & tissues BLACK; store in an AMBER bottle; use a HORN/ PLASTIC spatula & glass apparatus; incompatible with chlorides, alkaloids & organic matter** |

### 📋 TABLE D — OILS, FATS, WAXES & VEHICLES ⭐

| Drug (synonym) | ⭐ RECOGNITION | ⭐ DOSE | ⭐ USES |
|---|---|---|---|
| ⭐⭐ **Castor oil** (*Arandi ka tel*, Oleum ricini) | ⭐ **Pale straw-coloured/ almost colourless, thick VISCOUS oil**; ⭐ **faint characteristic odour and a NAUSEATING, acrid taste**; ⭐ ⚠️ **THE ONLY FIXED OIL FREELY SOLUBLE IN ALCOHOL (90 %)** — the classic identification point; sp. gr. 0.945–0.965; ⭐ **high ACETYL value** (ricinoleic acid –OH) | ⭐ **5 – 20 mL** (up to 30 mL), on an empty stomach | ⭐ **PURGATIVE (irritant/ stimulant — ricinoleic acid liberated by lipase acts on the small intestine; acts in 2–6 h), used before radiography & surgery; EXTERNAL: emollient, ⭐ Zinc & Castor Oil Ointment for NAPPY RASH, in eye drops & castor-oil "eye bath" to remove foreign bodies, hair oil, plasticiser in collodions & plasters** ⚠️ ⭐ **NEVER with MALE FERN (increases filicin absorption → toxicity)**; ⚠️ not in pregnancy or intestinal obstruction |
| ⭐⭐ **Liquid paraffin** (Liquid petrolatum, Mineral oil, *Paraffin ka tel*) | ⭐ **Colourless, transparent, TASTELESS & almost ODOURLESS oily liquid**; ⭐ **does NOT become rancid** (a saturated hydrocarbon); ⭐ **INSOLUBLE in water & alcohol, soluble in ether & chloroform**; ⭐ **MINERAL (petroleum) origin — NOT a vegetable or animal oil**; ⭐ **unsaponifiable** | ⭐ **10 – 30 mL** at bedtime | ⭐ **LUBRICANT LAXATIVE (it softens the faeces & lubricates the bowel — it is NOT absorbed and NOT digested); EXTERNAL: emollient, levigating agent, ointment & cream ingredient, mould lubricant for water-soluble suppository bases, nasal & aural use (⚠️ but see below)** ⚠️ ⭐ **On prolonged use it interferes with the absorption of the FAT-SOLUBLE VITAMINS A, D, E & K**; ⚠️ **anal leakage & pruritus ani**; ⚠️ ⭐ **RISK OF LIPOID PNEUMONIA if aspirated → NEVER give to infants, the very old, the bedridden or the unconscious, and avoid oily nasal drops** |
| ⭐ **Soft paraffin — WHITE & YELLOW** (Petrolatum, **Vaseline**, Petroleum jelly) | ⭐ **Translucent, unctuous, greasy SEMISOLID mass; white or yellow; tasteless & odourless; shows a slight FLUORESCENCE in daylight**; insoluble in water | ⭐ **EXTERNAL** | ⭐ **THE STANDARD OINTMENT BASE — emollient, occlusive & protective; lubricant; vehicle for topical drugs; barrier for skin protection; ⭐ 85 % of Simple Ointment, 50 % of Emulsifying Ointment** |
| ⭐ **Hard paraffin** (Paraffin wax) | ⭐ **Colourless/ white, translucent, ODOURLESS, TASTELESS waxy SOLID mass; slightly greasy to the touch**; m.p. **50–57 °C** | External | ⭐ **STIFFENING/ HARDENING AGENT in ointments & suppositories; wax baths for arthritis; sealing** |
| ⭐ **Wool fat / Lanolin** (Anhydrous lanolin; **Hydrous wool fat = Lanolin**) | ⭐ **Pale yellow, TENACIOUS, UNCTUOUS, greasy semisolid** with a **faint characteristic (sheepy) odour**; ⭐ **can absorb about 2 times (or more) its own weight of WATER without losing its ointment-like consistency**; from the **wool of sheep (*Ovis aries*)** | External | ⭐ **EMOLLIENT & ABSORPTION-BASE component — ⭐ used to enable a GREASY base to take up AQUEOUS solutions; emollient for dry & cracked skin, nipples; ingredient of Simple Ointment (5 %) & Simple Eye Ointment (10 %)** ⚠️ ⭐ **may cause CONTACT SENSITISATION (lanolin allergy) and can become rancid** |
| ⭐ **Beeswax — Yellow & White** (Cera flava/ alba, *Mom*) | ⭐ **Yellow (or bleached white) SOLID with a faint honey-like odour**; granular fracture; m.p. **62–65 °C**; from the honeycomb of ***Apis mellifera*** | External | ⭐ **STIFFENING/ HARDENING AGENT (⭐ used to RAISE the melting point of a suppository or ointment base); w/o emulsifier with borax (cold cream); polishes & candles** |
| ⭐ **Olive oil** (*Zaitoon ka tel*) | ⭐ **Pale yellow or greenish-yellow oil**; faint characteristic odour; bland, nutty taste; sp. gr. 0.91–0.92; congeals at about 0–5 °C | 15 – 30 mL | ⭐ **EMOLLIENT, nutrient, mild laxative & cholagogue, ⭐ ear drops to soften WAX, enema, base for liniments, ⭐ LIME LINIMENT (CARRON OIL = olive oil + lime water, equal parts) for BURNS** |
| ⭐ **Arachis (groundnut/ peanut) oil** | Pale yellow, bland oil, faint nutty odour | 15 – 30 mL | ⭐ **Solvent for oily INJECTIONS, emollient, enema, vehicle, substitute for olive oil** ⚠️ **peanut allergy** |
| ⭐ **Sesame (til/ gingelly) oil** | Pale yellow, bland | 15–30 mL | Vehicle for injections, emollient; ⭐ **Baudouin test identifies it** |
| ⭐ **Coconut oil** (*Nariyal ka tel*) | ⭐ **White semisolid below ~ 24 °C, colourless liquid above**; characteristic odour | — | Emollient, hair oil, soap & suppository base, ⭐ **source of medium-chain triglycerides & of hydrogenated suppository bases** |
| ⭐ **Cod liver oil** | ⭐ **Pale yellow oil with a characteristic FISHY odour & taste**; from the **fresh liver of *Gadus* spp. (cod)** | ⭐ **5 – 10 mL** | ⭐ **RICH SOURCE OF VITAMINS A & D — rickets, night blindness, general nutrition; also applied to ulcers & burns** |
| ⭐ **Shark liver oil** | Yellow oil, fishy | — | Vitamin A & D source; haemorrhoid preparations |
| ⭐ **Glycerin** (Glycerol, *Glycerine*) | ⭐ **Clear, colourless, ODOURLESS, syrupy VISCOUS liquid**; ⭐ **distinctly SWEET taste**; ⭐ **HYGROSCOPIC**; ⭐ **sp. gr. 1.26**; ⭐ **MISCIBLE with water & alcohol but INSOLUBLE in ether & fixed oils**; when heated with KHSO₄ → acrolein (pungent) | ⭐ **2 – 10 mL**; **glycerin suppository** | ⭐ **HUMECTANT, SOLVENT & sweet VEHICLE; EMOLLIENT & soothing (glycerin of borax, of thymol, of starch, of boric acid); ⭐ OSMOTIC LAXATIVE (glycerin suppository/ enema); throat paints (it CLINGS to mucosa); PRESERVATIVE in high concentration; levigating agent for water-miscible bases; base of glycero-gelatin; in ear drops (hygroscopic, dehydrating)** ⚠️ ⭐ **EXPLOSIVE with potassium permanganate & other strong oxidisers**; ⚠️ irritant if applied undiluted (dilute with water) |
| ⭐ **Propylene glycol** | Clear, colourless, viscous, almost odourless, slightly sweet liquid; miscible with water & alcohol | — | ⭐ **Solvent, cosolvent, humectant, preservative, levigating agent, vehicle for ear drops** |
| ⭐ **Alcohol (Ethanol) — Rectified spirit 90 %, Absolute 99.5 %** | ⭐ **Clear, colourless, mobile, volatile, INFLAMMABLE liquid**; characteristic spirituous odour; burning taste; ⭐ **burns with a pale BLUE, smokeless flame**; miscible with water | External/ vehicle | ⭐ **SOLVENT & preservative (self-preserving above ~ 20 %), antiseptic (⭐ most effective at 70 %), skin disinfectant & astringent (prevents bed sores), extraction menstruum, cooling sponge** ⚠️ **inflammable; denatured/ methylated spirit is POISONOUS (methanol → blindness)** |
| ⭐ **Surgical spirit** | Methylated spirit + castor oil + methyl salicylate + diethyl phthalate | External | ⭐ **Hardening the skin & preventing BED SORES; antiseptic rub** |
| ⭐ **Ether (Solvent/ Anaesthetic ether)** | ⭐ **Colourless, very volatile, extremely INFLAMMABLE mobile liquid**; ⭐ **characteristic sweet, penetrating odour**; **b.p. 34.6 °C**; sp. gr. 0.72 | Inhalation (anaesthetic) | ⭐ **General ANAESTHETIC (classical); solvent for fats, resins & collodion; cleansing the skin** ⚠️ ⭐ **HIGHLY INFLAMMABLE & EXPLOSIVE — store in a cool, dark place away from any flame; forms explosive peroxides on storage** |
| ⭐ **Chloroform** | ⭐ **Clear, colourless, heavy, volatile, mobile liquid**; ⭐ **characteristic sweet, ethereal odour & burning sweet taste**; ⭐ **sp. gr. 1.47–1.48 (HEAVIER than water)**; ⭐ **NON-inflammable**; b.p. 61 °C | ⭐ **Chloroform water 0.25 % as a vehicle**; internal 0.05–0.3 mL | ⭐ **PRESERVATIVE, flavour & carminative in CHLOROFORM WATER (the standard mixture vehicle); solvent; counter-irritant liniment; general anaesthetic (obsolete — hepatotoxic & cardiotoxic)** ⚠️ **store in an amber bottle away from light (forms phosgene)** |
| ⭐ **Turpentine oil** (Oil of turpentine, *Tarpeen ka tel*) | ⭐ **Colourless mobile liquid with a characteristic PENETRATING TEREBINTHINATE odour**; pungent bitter taste; ⭐ **from *Pinus* spp.; DEXTRO-rotatory; sp. gr. ~0.86**; insoluble in water | Internal (obsolete) 0.3–1 mL; ⭐ **mostly EXTERNAL** | ⭐ **COUNTER-IRRITANT & RUBEFACIENT (turpentine liniment for rheumatic & muscular pain), antiseptic, carminative, ⭐ turpentine ENEMA for abdominal distension, expectorant, solvent for waxes, resins & paints** ⚠️ irritant, nephrotoxic if absorbed |
| ⭐ **Eucalyptus oil** | ⭐ **Colourless/ pale yellow liquid with a characteristic CAMPHORACEOUS, cineole odour** and a pungent, cooling taste | 0.05–0.2 mL | ⭐ **INHALATION for nasal & bronchial congestion (⭐ Menthol & Eucalyptus Inhalation), antiseptic, counter-irritant, insect repellent, flavour** |
| ⭐ **Peppermint oil** | ⭐ **Colourless/ pale yellow liquid, strong PEPPERMINT odour, pungent then COOLING taste; LAEVO-rotatory** | 0.05–0.2 mL | ⭐ **CARMINATIVE, antispasmodic, flavour; peppermint water as a carminative vehicle** |
| ⭐ **Clove oil** | ⭐ **Colourless/ pale yellow liquid with the strong odour of cloves; ⭐ HEAVIER than water (sp. gr. 1.04–1.06); contains ~ 85 % EUGENOL; FeCl₃ → blue** | 0.05–0.2 mL | ⭐ **Local ANALGESIC/ ANAESTHETIC for TOOTHACHE (dental use), antiseptic, carminative, flavour, clearing agent in microscopy** |
| ⭐ **Methyl salicylate** (Oil of wintergreen) | ⭐ **Colourless oily liquid with the characteristic odour of wintergreen**; ⭐ **FeCl₃ → VIOLET** | ⭐ **EXTERNAL** | ⭐ **COUNTER-IRRITANT & analgesic in balms & liniments for rheumatic/ muscular pain** ⚠️ **toxic if swallowed (a teaspoonful can kill a child)** |

### 📋 TABLE E — COMMON CRUDE DRUGS & MISCELLANEOUS BAZAAR ITEMS ⭐

| Drug (synonym) | ⭐ RECOGNITION | ⭐ DOSE | ⭐ USES |
|---|---|---|---|
| ⭐ **Isapghula** (*Isabgol*, Psyllium/ Spogel seeds, husk) | ⭐ **Pinkish-grey/ pinkish-white boat-shaped seeds, or white translucent flaky HUSK**; ⭐ **odourless & tasteless**; ⭐ **SWELLS enormously in water forming a colourless mucilage — SWELLING INDEX NOT LESS THAN 40** | ⭐ **Seed 4 – 16 g** (usually 5–10 g) / **husk 3 – 6 g** with plenty of water | ⭐ **BULK LAXATIVE (constipation), demulcent; chronic DYSENTERY & diarrhoea (absorbs water & toxins); hypocholesterolaemic** ⚠️ **must be taken with adequate water (risk of obstruction)** |
| ⭐ **Senna** (*Sanay ki patti*, Alexandrian/ Tinnevelly) | ⭐ **Pale green/ yellowish-green thin LANCEOLATE leaflets with an ASYMMETRIC (unequal) BASE**; faint characteristic odour, mucilaginous then bitter taste; ⭐ **BORNTRAGER'S TEST positive (pink/red in ammonia)** | ⭐ **Leaf 0.5 – 2 g**; infusion 30–60 mL; tincture 2–4 mL | ⭐ **STIMULANT PURGATIVE (anthraquinone/ sennoside — acts on the colon in 8–12 h)**; ⚠️ **griping (give with a carminative); colours the urine reddish; not in pregnancy/ intestinal obstruction** |
| ⭐ **Nux vomica** (*Kuchla*) ⚠️ | ⭐ **Flat, disc-shaped, ash-grey seeds densely covered with SILKY APPRESSED HAIRS ("button-like"); extremely BITTER; VERY HARD & horny** | ⚠️ **Powder 60–120 mg; tincture 0.5–2 mL (⭐ max 2 mL)** | ⭐ **BITTER TONIC & appetite stimulant, CNS stimulant (strychnine)** ⚠️ ⭐ **POISONOUS — strychnine causes convulsions** |
| ⭐ **Gentian, Quassia, Chirata** | Bitter roots/ wood/ herb; ⭐ **intensely BITTER**; gentian = brownish root with a bitter, sweetish taste | Gentian 0.6–2 g; infusion 15–30 mL | ⭐ **BITTER TONICS & appetite stimulants (reflex increase of gastric secretion); Quassia infusion as an ENEMA for THREADWORMS; quassia cups** |
| ⭐ **Ginger** (*Adrak/ Sonth*) | ⭐ **Buff/ pale brown, laterally flattened, branched rhizome ("hands"/ "races"); characteristic AROMATIC odour; PUNGENT taste (gingerol)**; starch abundant | ⭐ **0.5 – 2 g**; tincture 0.5–2 mL | ⭐ **CARMINATIVE, aromatic STIMULANT, ANTIEMETIC (motion sickness & morning sickness), flavour, sialagogue, counter-irritant** |
| ⭐ **Cardamom, Fennel, Coriander, Dill, Ajowan, Cinnamon, Clove, Caraway, Anise, Peppermint** | Aromatic fruits/ seeds/ barks/ flower buds with characteristic **aromatic odours & pungent-sweet tastes**; all contain **volatile oil** | 0.3–2 g each | ⭐ **CARMINATIVES, flavouring & aromatic stimulants; the basis of "carminative mixtures", aromatic waters & spirits; ajowan (thymol) & clove (eugenol) also antiseptic** |
| ⭐ **Liquorice** (*Mulethi*, Glycyrrhiza) | ⭐ **Yellowish, coarsely FIBROUS root/ stolon; characteristically SWEET taste (glycyrrhizin, ~50× sweeter than sugar)** | 1 – 4 g; liquid extract 2–4 mL | ⭐ **DEMULCENT & EXPECTORANT, sweetening & flavouring agent (⭐ masks bitter/ salty tastes — the classic CORRECTIVE), mild laxative, ANTI-ULCER (carbenoxolone)** ⚠️ ⭐ **excess → sodium & water retention, hypokalaemia & hypertension** |
| ⭐ **Aloe** (*Musabbar*, Curaçao/ Cape aloes) | ⭐ **Dark brown to blackish-brown OPAQUE masses with a characteristic disagreeable odour and an intensely BITTER, nauseous taste**; ⭐ **Borntrager's test positive**; fluoresces | ⭐ **100 – 300 mg** | ⭐ **STIMULANT PURGATIVE & emmenagogue; the fresh LEAF GEL as an emollient & wound-healing application (burns, sunburn)** ⚠️ **griping; not in pregnancy** |
| ⭐ **Catechu (Kattha) & Kino** | ⭐ **Dark brown/ blackish brittle masses; strongly ASTRINGENT taste; FeCl₃ → green-black (catechol tannin); gelatin → white ppt** | 0.3 – 2 g | ⭐ **ASTRINGENT — diarrhoea, gargle, mouthwash for spongy gums, dentifrice; in "paan"** |
| ⭐ **Opium & its preparations** ⚠️ | Dark brown plastic masses/ powder; ⭐ **characteristic heavy odour, bitter taste; ⭐ FeCl₃ → reddish-purple (MECONIC ACID)** | ⚠️ **Opium 30–120 mg; Tincture 0.3–2 mL; Paregoric 2–8 mL** | ⭐ **Narcotic ANALGESIC, antitussive, ANTIDIARRHOEAL** ⚠️ ⭐ **NARCOTIC — Schedule X/ NDPS Act; addiction; respiratory depression; antidote NALOXONE** |
| ⭐ **Acacia & Tragacanth** | ⭐ **Acacia: white/ pale yellow, brittle, glassy TEARS or spray-dried powder; almost odourless; MUCILAGINOUS taste; FREELY & COMPLETELY soluble in water giving a slightly acid, non-viscous mucilage; positive OXIDASE/ benzidine test.** ⭐ **Tragacanth: flattened, curved, horny, translucent RIBBONS/ flakes with concentric ridges; odourless, insipid; SWELLS but does NOT dissolve; ruthenium red → pink; VERY viscous** | Demulcent 0.5–2 g | ⭐ **Acacia = EMULSIFYING agent, binder, demulcent (mucilage 35 %); Tragacanth = the best SUSPENDING agent, thickener (mucilage 1.25 %)** *(full detail in Ch. 2)* |
| ⭐ **Starch** (*Arrowroot/ maize/ rice starch*) | ⭐ **White, odourless, tasteless fine powder that CREAKS when pressed between the fingers**; ⭐ **IODINE → BLUE-BLACK**; insoluble in cold water, forms a paste on boiling | — | ⭐ **DUSTING POWDER (with talc & zinc oxide), tablet diluent, binder & DISINTEGRANT, glycerin of starch, ⭐ ANTIDOTE for IODINE poisoning, poultice, absorbent for eutectics** |
| ⭐ **Honey** (*Shahad*, Purified honey) | ⭐ **Pale yellow to reddish-brown, thick, syrupy, translucent liquid; characteristic odour & very SWEET taste**; may crystallise; ⭐ **Fiehe's test detects added invert sugar** | — | ⭐ **SWEETENING & demulcent VEHICLE (linctuses), mild laxative & nutrient, WOUND & BURN dressing (hygroscopic & antibacterial), ⭐ base of HONEY OF BORAX for oral thrush** ⚠️ **not for infants under 1 year (botulism risk)** |
| ⭐ **Activated charcoal** (Adsorbent charcoal) | ⭐ **Fine, light, black, ODOURLESS & TASTELESS powder**; insoluble; ⭐ **adsorbs dyes/ gases (decolourises a methylene-blue solution)** | ⭐ **50 – 100 g in water** (poisoning); 0.5–4 g flatulence | ⭐ **UNIVERSAL ADSORBENT ANTIDOTE in POISONING (the single most useful first-aid antidote), flatulence & diarrhoea, deodorant dressing, decolourising & filter aid** ⚠️ ⭐ **adsorbs drugs → do not give with oral medicines** |
| ⭐ **Sugar (Sucrose)** & ⭐ **Glucose (Dextrose)** | ⭐ **Sucrose: colourless crystals/ white powder, sweet, ⭐ CHARS with a CARAMEL smell, non-reducing (Fehling's NEGATIVE).** ⭐ **Glucose: white crystalline powder, less sweet, ⭐ Fehling's/ Benedict's POSITIVE (brick-red)** | Nutrient; ⭐ **glucose 5 % = isotonic** | ⭐ **Sweetening agent, syrup (66.7 % w/w), tablet diluent & coating, preservative in high concentration; Glucose = nutrient, energy source, IV infusion (5 %, 25 %, 50 %), ORS component** |
| ⭐ **Oral Rehydration Salts (ORS)** | White crystalline powder in a sachet | ⭐ **NaCl 2.6 g + trisodium citrate 2.9 g + KCl 1.5 g + anhydrous glucose 13.5 g in 1 LITRE of water** | ⭐ **PREVENTION & TREATMENT OF DEHYDRATION in diarrhoea — the most important public-health medicine** |
| ⭐ **Aspirin** | ⭐ **White crystalline powder/ needles; odourless (⚠️ or with a faint ACETIC/ VINEGAR smell IF HYDROLYSED/ old); slightly bitter; sparingly soluble in water, soluble in alcohol; ⭐ FeCl₃ after boiling with alkali → VIOLET (salicylate)** | ⭐ **300 – 900 mg** 4–6 hourly (max 4 g/day); **antiplatelet 75–150 mg** | ⭐ **ANALGESIC, ANTIPYRETIC, ANTI-INFLAMMATORY & ANTIPLATELET** ⚠️ **gastric irritation & bleeding — take AFTER FOOD; ⚠️ REYE'S SYNDROME — avoid in children with viral fever; ⚠️ INDIFFUSIBLE in mixtures; DV = 1.1** |
| ⭐ **Paracetamol** | White, odourless, crystalline powder; slightly bitter | ⭐ **500 mg – 1 g** (max 4 g/day) | ⭐ **ANALGESIC & ANTIPYRETIC (no anti-inflammatory action, no gastric irritation)** ⚠️ ⭐ **HEPATOTOXIC in overdose — antidote N-ACETYLCYSTEINE** |
| ⭐ **Quinine (bisulphate/ sulphate)** | ⭐ **White fine needle-like crystals or powder; INTENSELY BITTER; ⭐ shows a BLUE FLUORESCENCE in dilute acid (sulphuric acid) solution** | 300 – 600 mg t.d.s. | ⭐ **ANTIMALARIAL, bitter tonic, night cramps** ⚠️ **cinchonism (tinnitus, deafness, headache); efflorescent** |
| ⭐ **Sodium/ Potassium bitartrate, Citric & Tartaric acid** | ⭐ **Colourless crystals with a strongly SOUR/ ACID taste; ⭐ citric acid is EFFLORESCENT; both effervesce with bicarbonates** | 0.3–2 g | ⭐ **Acidulant & flavour, ⭐ the ACID component of EFFERVESCENT powders & granules, refrigerant drink, anticoagulant (citrate), chelating agent** |
| ⭐ **Naphthalene / Paradichlorobenzene** | White crystalline balls; ⭐ **strong characteristic odour; SUBLIME readily** | External | ⭐ **Moth repellent, insecticide, deodorant** ⚠️ toxic; haemolysis in G6PD deficiency |
| ⭐ **Petroleum jelly, gauze, cotton wool, bandages, adhesive plaster, thermometer** | Surgical dressings & appliances | — | ⭐ **First-aid & dressing items every bazaar dispensary stocks; ⭐ absorbent cotton wool must be free of fat & absorb water readily (a "sinking test")** |

## 13.3 ⭐ THE "TOP 25" BAZAAR MEDICINES — ONE-LINE DOSE & USE FLASH TABLE

| Drug | ⭐ Dose | ⭐ Use in one word/ phrase |
|---|---|---|
| **Sodium bicarbonate** | 1–5 g | Antacid |
| **Magnesium sulphate (Epsom salt)** | **5–15 g** | Saline purgative |
| **Sodium sulphate (Glauber's salt)** | 8–16 g | Saline purgative |
| **Magnesium carbonate / oxide** | 0.3–2 g / 0.25–1 g | Antacid & laxative |
| **Magnesium trisilicate** | 0.5–2 g | Antacid (prolonged) |
| **Calcium carbonate (chalk)** | 1–5 g | Antacid & antidiarrhoeal |
| **Aluminium hydroxide gel** | 5–10 mL | Antacid |
| **Castor oil** | **5–20 mL** | Purgative |
| **Liquid paraffin** | **10–30 mL** | Lubricant laxative |
| **Isapghula** | **4–16 g** | Bulk laxative |
| **Senna** | 0.5–2 g | Stimulant purgative |
| **Sulphur** | 1–4 g / 10 % ointment | Laxative / scabicide |
| **Ferrous sulphate** | **200 mg t.d.s.** | Haematinic |
| **Potassium citrate** | 1–2 g | Urinary alkaliniser |
| **Potassium iodide** | 250–500 mg | Expectorant |
| **Ammonium chloride** | 0.3–2 g | Expectorant |
| **Sodium salicylate** | 0.6–2 g | Antirheumatic |
| **Aspirin** | 300–900 mg | Analgesic/ antipyretic |
| **Paracetamol** | 500 mg–1 g | Analgesic/ antipyretic |
| **Quinine** | 300–600 mg | Antimalarial |
| **Activated charcoal** | **50–100 g** | Antidote (adsorbent) |
| **Zinc sulphate** | 0.6–2 g (emetic) | Emetic/ astringent |
| **Cod liver oil** | 5–10 mL | Vitamins A & D |
| **Glycerin** | 2–10 mL | Laxative/ humectant |
| **Honey / Sugar / Glucose** | As required | Sweet vehicle/ nutrient |
| ⭐ **EXTERNAL ONLY** | — | **Potassium permanganate 1:5000–1:10 000 · Boric acid 2–4 % (isotonic 1.9 %) · Borax · Iodine (tincture 2.5 % + KI 2.5 %) · Hydrogen peroxide 3–6 % · Phenol 0.5–2 % · Alum 0.5–2 % · Zinc oxide · Calamine · Talc · Kaolin poultice · Silver nitrate 0.5–2 % · Formaldehyde 1–10 % · Camphor · Menthol · Thymol · Turpentine oil · Methyl salicylate** |
